measurable. The run is four interleaved pairs in one invocation on one binary, with
`ROCKET_RK3576_BO_POOL` the only treatment. The per-pair gains are +2.115, +2.112, +1.385
and +1.820, mean +1.8579%, with the pool at 13.9800 against 13.7250 t/s.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 8 cells, 2026-08-20]

The mechanism shows on the counter as DRAM traffic, not host CPU. The pooled arm's
`l2d_cache_refill` level is -1.50% below the unpooled arm's (16166.3M vs 16412.6M). That is
the same order as the wall gain, and in the same direction as the L2-refill term that
carries this part's per-process wall constant.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 8 cells, 2026-08-20]

The scope is the whole caveat: `setarch -R` pins one virtual layout, so +1.86% is the
effect at the modal layout. It is not the ASLR-on expected value a shipped default would
see, and the pool stays default off. One of the four pooled cells ran 0.501% slow with its
refill count at its arm's mean. That is not the page-color draw, and it is so far
unexplained. The cell biases the figure downward, since dropping it widens the gap to
+2.028%.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 8 cells, 2026-08-20]

#### The per-process wall term

**The pass's wall value is not measurable on this instrument.** The first campaign read the
knob as +6.70% of `pp2048` and the rebuild as -6.96%. Re-run with the order reversed (arm,
iso, control, one process each), the knob flip read +1.7% and the binary flip +2.0% the
other way, while every bucket reproduced its attribution. The wall moves by up to ~5%
between processes with every bucket flat and no reproducible structure. Fourteen processes
over three campaigns show it:

- The first campaign's five same-cell sequences were flat inside their blocks (spread
  <= 0.4%, ctl 12.33/12.37/12.35, arm 12.28/12.23/12.27, iso 11.48/11.50), with monotone
  steps between blocks.
- The reversed campaign declined monotonically by position (12.24 -> 12.03 -> 11.79).
- A same-day A/A, the arm cell three times inside one invocation, read 11.77, 12.26 and
  11.69. It broke both patterns while its buckets held (`quant+rot` within 3.4%, `packB`
  1.1%, `wait` 0.2%, `sumabs` 0).

The same cell spans 11.69-12.28 within one day and +-4.5% across days. Reps inside a
process agree to +-0.25%, so the term is a per-process constant, fixed at process birth. It
lives in the unbucketed ~78% of prefill (attention, norms, rope, the CPU-placed matmuls).
Its mechanism is [unknown], and it defeats every ordering story tried on it (cell identity,
invocation position, thermal soak, idle reset).

**A `pp2048` difference under ~5% between two processes means nothing here, regardless of
order.** A wall A/B needs interleaved arms and enough processes to beat a ~2% per-process sd
read from its own controls. A lever's durable price is its per-call bucket. The
buckets are stable to 2-3.5% across sessions, where the effects of interest are 10-43%.

The per-process term is an L2-refill (DRAM) term at the counter level, and the gap
instrument watches it move. An A/A triple ran under `perf stat` on 2026-08-19 in one
invocation, with the governor pinned and an equality-gated epoch:

| process | wall, t/s | inter-call `gap`, s | buckets, s |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 12.57 | 545.5 | 136.6 |
| 2 | 12.56 | 545.2 | 137.1 |
| 3 | 12.11 | 569.2 | 135.0 |

The slow process carries its whole excess in `gap`, with the smallest bucket sum of the
three. The term therefore lives between calls inside the span, not in the bench scaffolding
outside it. Its counters read instructions +0.05%, `l1d_cache_refill` +0.0% and
`l2d_cache_refill` +23.7% (+4.0e9), with cycles +4.4% tracking the wall. Frequency is
therefore excluded, and the constant is DRAM traffic from L2 misses.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `aap_p{1,2,3}`]

The physical-page mechanism one level down is refuted for the per-process component. That
mechanism says the L2 is physically indexed, so the physical pages a process is handed at
birth set its conflict rate for life. Pinning the virtual layout with `setarch -R` collapses the
`l2d_cache_refill` CV from 9.699% to 0.099% (~98x) over 16 interleaved cells. The same pin
collapses the `pp2048` wall spread from 4.966% to 0.292% (17x). The draw is virtual, not
page luck.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, refuted by arm (i), cells `walr_{on,off}_p{1..8}`]

The 512 KiB, 512-set L2 that the physical story was argued from is the A53 cluster's. Every
cell here runs `taskset 4-7` on the A72, whose L2 is 1024 KiB, 16-way and 1024 sets: 16 page
colors, not 8. The same triple shows the q8/L1d dial moving the other way in the slow
process. The two locality terms are independent draws, and both are virtual.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `aap_p{1,2,3}`, refuted by arm (i), cells
`walr_{on,off}_p{1..8}`]

The term is not an unconditional per-process constant: ten consecutive fresh processes can
come back flat on every counter. A ten-cell campaign ran on 2026-08-19 with one binary,
interleaved 5+5, the governor pinned and 4 threads on the A72 cluster. Across its five plain
processes, `l2d_cache_refill` max/min reads 1.0061, against the +23.7% the A/A triple showed
between two of three. Cycles read 1.0013, instructions 1.0002 and `l1d_cache_refill`
1.0014, and the wall spans 0.15% (13.68-13.70 t/s).
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `htlb_{n,a}_p{1..5}`]

Whatever draws the term did not draw on this campaign. A wall A/B run under these
conditions therefore resolves far better than the ~2% per-process sd implies. The sd is a
property of the campaign, not a constant of the board. It must be read from each campaign's
own controls rather than inherited.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `htlb_{n,a}_p{1..5}`]

The campaign held a 1024-page hugetlb pool and ran `compact_memory` before it. That
defragments and shrinks the physical pool every process then draws from, and it is the
leading suspect for the flatness [hypothesis]. The protocol and epoch rivals to it are
eliminated below.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `htlb_{n,a}_p{1..5}`]

The term is a lottery, not a spread, and it is alive on every epoch measured. Across 30
archived processes from seven campaigns, 26 sit within 0.30% of their group median and 4
sit 2.8-3.6% out. An empty gap separates the two populations, and one of the four is fast,
so the modal cluster is not the best case.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `nopool_p{1..5}`]

A five-cell no-pool control on the current binary (2026-08-19, governor pinned,
`taskset -c 4-7`) read walls 13.73, 13.72, 13.71, 13.71 and 13.26. That fifth process
reproduces the A/A triple's signature exactly:
`l2d_cache_refill` +15.32% against the mean of the other four, on instructions +0.05% and
`l1d_cache_refill` +0.01%. Its elapsed time is +3.11%, with every timed bucket flat to
+-0.2%, so the term survives the binary epoch.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `nopool_p{1..5}`]

The consequence for measurement is sharper than a spread would be. **A CV or a span at
n=3-5 measures whether a draw landed in the sample, not the term's size.** That is why the
CVs of these campaigns range 0.05% to 2.15% with no dial explaining them. Price a wall A/B
at the bucket, or interleave and count draws. Do not quote a CV.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `nopool_p{1..5}`]

A second per-process lottery runs in the weight-convert loops, and it is a draw in a
different address space from the wall's. The `wt_rotate` loop is sharply two-state across
processes: 10753 ms or 11927 ms, a 1.109x separation. The empty band between the modes is
7.5x the next-widest gap in the sample. The work is byte-identical every run (166 convert
calls, 2988 library calls, 6876 tiles, 27504 submits), so this is a timing draw and not a
workload difference.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, re-read of 30 processes across seven campaigns]

On the epochs where `wt_quant` is still a scalar loop, it is two-state with `wt_rotate`
(5800 or 6558 ms, its own gap 739 ms against a next-widest of 8 ms). The two loops agree on
the mode 12 of 12. One state the process is handed spans two independent loops, which places
it in the allocation-layout family rather than in code alignment. The draw does not move the
`pp2048` wall (-0.02% and -0.09% within the two flat campaigns), because it runs in weight
conversion, outside the timed reps.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, re-read of 30 processes across seven campaigns]

Its value is as a probe: it is binary per process at a ~50% base rate, with cleanly
separated modes. Every profiler log written here prints it, so it is readable retroactively
at no cost. The wall's own draw, by contrast, is rare enough (4 of 30) that n=5 sees nothing
24-53% of the time. **Do not read a cross-epoch `wt_rotate` or `wt_quant` difference without
matching the modes.** A 7.3% difference between two n=3 samples was the mode mix, not the
epoch.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, re-read of 30 processes across seven campaigns]

The convert-loop mode is a virtual layout draw, and `setarch -R` pins it. Running the same
binary with ASLR disabled collapses the draw to 0 low of 16 at a full spread of 0.134%. An
interleaved ASLR-on control reads 4 low of 16 at 11.756%. That is an 88x variance collapse,
at one-sided Fisher p=0.0506 on the mode mix alone. The state a process is handed is
therefore set by the layout ASLR randomizes, not by which physical frames it draws.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 32 interleaved cells on one binary, governor pinned, dial armed by
address readback]

**The convert-loop mode cannot be used to screen for a wall roll.** Within the same ASLR-on
control, mode-low cells are bench-wall outliers 2 of 4, against 4 of 12 for mode-high. At
one-sided Fisher 0.489, the two draws are independent [measured]. On any A/B that touches
the convert loops, `setarch -R` removes an 11.8% per-process nuisance draw, but it does not
buy the fast state. The fixed level (11907-11923 ms) does not overlap the ASLR-on modal cluster
(11874-11895), and it is 0.15% slower than that cluster and 10.8% slower than low.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 32 interleaved cells on one binary, governor pinned, dial armed by
address readback]

Treat high as a band, not a state. The ASLR-on high draws separate into that modal cluster
(9 of 12), an 11930 and a pair near 12015. Low is a discrete state: 10752, 10753, 10753 and
10754 over four independent processes, a spread of 0.019%. A mode reading costs one
`pp512 -r 1` process, 99-103 s, against 630 s for a wall cell.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 32 interleaved cells on one binary, governor pinned, dial armed by
address readback]

The bench wall's per-process draw is virtual too. The term decomposes into a draw the layout
fixes plus a slower one that survives the pin. The same 32 cells each ran a `pp512` bench.
The campaign was two runs of the harness 47 s apart, so it is scored within an invocation.
Pooling across that boundary turns the level step below into apparent per-process scatter,
and inverts the answer.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, same 32 cells, no additional board time]

Within an invocation, the ASLR-on control shows 6 of 16 wall outliers (>= 1.0% off its own
median), against 0 of 16 with ASLR off, at one-sided Fisher p=0.0088. The ASLR-on outliers form a discrete band at -2.4% to -4.1%, 3 of 8 in both blocks, with full
spreads of 4.366% and 4.188%. The ASLR-off full spreads are 0.592% and 0.329%. **The layout a
process is handed therefore sets its bench wall as well as its convert-loop mode.** The two
are independent draws in that one address space.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, same 32 cells, no additional board time]

The magnitude and the empty-gap signature match the `pp2048` wall lottery's (2.8-3.6% off
median, 4 of 30) closely enough to suspect one phenomenon. This run is `pp512 -r 1`, though,
so that identification is [hypothesis]. One component does not collapse: under ASLR off the
bench wall is flat inside an invocation, and its level moved +2.508% between the two
invocations. The interleaved ASLR-on arm did not move (+0.031% overall, high band -0.230%,
low band +0.484%), so a system-wide drift is excluded. The convert loop's ASLR-off level did
not move either (-0.030%, 0.134% across all 16).
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, same 32 cells, no additional board time]

**`setarch -R` bounds the per-process draw and cannot bound the between-invocation
component.** The candidate for that component is the physical/system state the allocator
hands out [hypothesis].
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, same 32 cells, no additional board time]

The per-process wall draw is virtual at `pp2048` as well, the depth the wall lottery was
fitted on, so it is not page luck. The run is 16 interleaved `pp2048 -r 3` cells in one
invocation, with both arms otherwise identical. The ASLR-on control reproduced the lottery:
3 of 8 outliers at a 4.966% full spread. Two of them sit inside the 2.8-3.6% band the corpus
recorded, and the third is at -4.487%. The ASLR-off arm is 0 of 8 at a 0.292% spread
(13.69-13.73 t/s), a spread ratio of 16.99x against ~10x at `pp512`.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `walr_{on,off}_p{1..8}`]

The mechanism reads on the counter: `l2d_cache_refill` CV 9.699% on -> 0.099% off. The mode
mix alone is underpowered at this n by construction (Fisher 3/8 vs 0/8 = 0.1000), so the
magnitude is what carries the result. "Full spread" is range/min throughout, so the 16.99x
is like-for-like against the `pp512` figures, and by the median the same cells give 16.25x.
The pin selects the modal layout rather than creating a new operating point. The two arms'
medians are 13.705 and 13.720, 0.109% apart, and the ASLR-on non-outlier band's median is
13.72 exactly.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `walr_{on,off}_p{1..8}`]

The arms' means differ by 1.33% only because three slow draws drag the control's mean. So
`setarch -R` removes the slow tail and does not move the level. Its scope is the
per-process draw only, because `setarch -R` pins the virtual layout, not physical frames. It
cannot separate "the draw is virtual" from "frame assignment goes near-deterministic once
the layout is fixed". The +2.508% between-invocation step above is untouched by it.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `walr_{on,off}_p{1..8}`]

**For anyone benchmarking on this part, `setarch -R` plus one invocation takes the
process-to-process wall sd to 0.087%.** That is what makes sub-1% host levers measurable
here at all.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `walr_{on,off}_p{1..8}`]

The physical frames a bench is handed are a fresh set every process, so frame identity is
not what carries the wall's level. Reading `/proc/<pid>/pagemap` concurrently with a live
`pp512` bench, at fixed virtual addresses under `setarch -R`, gives the (virtual address ->
physical frame) map directly. Two cells compare by positional agreement, the fraction of
commonly sampled addresses whose PFN is identical. That measure is a magnitude, and it is
indifferent to physical contiguity. Over 8 cells in 4 invocations the comparison is total
(one VMA layout, `common = union = 12655` on every anon pair).
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `armj2_i{1..4}_c{1,2}`]

Anon agreement is 0.00000 on 26 of 28 pairs and <= 0.00063 on the other two, against a
run-count standard error of 0.0094. It is 0.00000 within an invocation, where the wall is
flat, as well as between invocations. No timescale therefore exists on which the anon frames
repeat. The GGUF's page cache is the opposite: file agreement is 1.00000 on all 28 pairs,
byte-identical frames across every invocation. This answers frame identity, not whether
physical memory matters: a statistical property of the frame set can still track the wall
while every identity differs.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `armj2_i{1..4}_c{1,2}`]

Anon allocation is contiguous in buddy blocks, on a kernel with no THP. The mean run is 69.6
pages (278 KiB), the median 32 pages, and 96.8% of run lengths are powers of two over orders
4-9. Read the granularity with it: the sample is every 16th page, so 39.7% of runs sit at
that floor and shorter ones are unresolved. Contiguity is why a 16-bin L2 page-color
histogram carries no information on this workload. Inside a run the color walk is
deterministic, and long runs cover the 16 colors near-uniformly, so the histogram is flat by
construction.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `armj2_i{1..4}_c{1,2}` and `armjprice`]

A stride-1 read measured chi2 0.12 on 15 df at n=202,459, sub-multinomial by 10.9x. **A color
histogram needs fragmented allocation to be informative, and allocation here is not
fragmented.** A sampling stride that is a multiple of 16 makes it worse. That stride advances
the color by zero inside a run and produces a large chi2 that is pure aliasing.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `armj2_i{1..4}_c{1,2}` and `armjprice`]

The between-invocation step is not a property of starting a new invocation. Across four
invocations of two ASLR-off `pp512` cells (60 s apart on an otherwise idle board, one binary,
`performance`), the boundaries move -0.082, +0.123 and +0.082%. Decomposed, the
within-invocation sd is 0.1085% (4 df), and the sd of the four invocation means is 0.0854%
against 0.0767% expected from within-noise alone. That gives a between component of
0.0374%, F = 1.238 on 3,4 df, with a 95% upper bound of 0.237%. The bound is an order of
magnitude under the +2.508% step recorded above, whose two invocations were separated by a
session's worth of other work.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `armj2_i{1..4}_c{1,2}`]

**Read that bound only under a continuous-nuisance model.** The wall term on this part
behaves as a discrete draw, with 37.5% outlier rates at the process level. Against a draw, 0
fires in 3 boundaries bounds the per-boundary rate only at 0.63, which excludes nothing. At a
rate of 0.375 it takes seven boundaries to say anything. An sd decomposition is not a bound
on a draw, and the flatness of these four invocations describes them without bounding the
step.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `armj2_i{1..4}_c{1,2}`]

Backing the multi-MB anon buffers with 2 MB pages costs 0.64% of the prefill wall and does
not move the L2 refill count. The kernel carries no THP (the sysfs node is absent), so the
route is hugetlbfs through glibc 2.42's `glibc.malloc.hugetlb=2`. It reaches 601-606 2 MB
pages (~1.2 GB of anon) at the bench's peak, against 0 without it. Over five interleaved
pairs the hugetlb arm reads 13.600 t/s against 13.688, 0.64% slower. The sign is consistent
in 5 of 5 pairs at ~4x the 0.15% within-arm spread.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `htlb_{n,a}_p{1..5}`]

Elapsed time is +0.53%, cycles +0.20%, and `l2d_cache_refill` is unchanged at -0.2%. The
host buckets move with the wall (mm_wall +1.8%, entry +1.5%, alloc +1.4%, packB +1.3%).
The `read` bucket at -9.4% and `wt_rotate` at -4.1% sit inside their own 7.3% and 12%
process spreads, and carry nothing. The campaign that measured this had no process roll
unlucky, so it prices the tunable at the flat operating point. It does not test whether
2 MB pages would blunt the term when the term fires.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, cells `htlb_{n,a}_p{1..5}`]

#### L1d set congruence across rebuilds

**A rebuild can move an untouched hot loop 2.2x through L1d set congruence.** The counters
plus the disassembly separate that from codegen in one pair. The frontend commit that handed
the entry its weight sums moved `quant+rot` 32.6-32.8 -> 45.6-47.2 s a process, cell-locked
across days. That diff's only code is in the weight-convert path.
[HW sweep, objdump, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-19, chains `armAB2` and `armC`]

Phase stamps applied to both frontends put the whole move in `q8`, the quantize loop. The
loop went 9942 -> 21870 ms over the same 2656 calls, 96% of the delta, while copy, rot and
max held within 3.1%. The stamps are equality-gated: the old binary with and without stamps
prints PPL 8.7433 to every digit.
[HW sweep, objdump, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-19, chains `armAB2` and `armC`]

The two binaries' q8 loops are instruction-identical (same 11 instructions, same registers,
one `lrintf@plt` call per element). The whole-process a72 counter pair reads instructions
-0.18%, `l1d_cache_refill` +8.42% and `l2d_cache_refill` +0.11%. That is zero added
instructions and extra L1d misses that hit in L2: an L1d conflict cost, not a footprint one.
[HW sweep, objdump, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-19, chains `armAB2` and `armC`]

The set-level story is a hypothesis, but it fits every observation. This core's L1d is
32 KiB 2-way. The loop interleaves a float read stream and a byte write stream with a
per-element libm stack frame. In that story, the binary's frame sizes and startup allocation
sequence fix the three-way congruence. The story explains why page-padding one allocation
moved the level partially (42.4 s) and could not restore it.
[HW sweep, objdump, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-19, chains `armAB2` and `armC`]

A third roll clears the commit itself. The same diff rebuilt against the newer library (a
different link state) reads `q8` at 9.9-10.0 s, the good level, in all three of that
epoch's cells. The congruence is therefore an epoch property that any translation-unit or
link change re-rolls, and the diff was one way to roll the dice. Five rolls are on record:

- Old frontend, old library: good.
- New frontend, old library: bad.
- Old frontend, new library: good.
- New frontend, new library: good.
- The shipped resident-cube epoch (`427aee98`): good, with `quant+rot` at 31-33 s across its
  nine cells.

[HW sweep, objdump, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-19, chains `armAB2` and `armC`]

Two consequences stand. A cross-binary bucket A/B on this core must carry a same-binary control. This holds even when the touched code is nowhere near the bucket,
because a rebuild can land a 2.2x swing on an untouched loop. The durable fix for a loop in
this position is structural. A NEON `fcvtns` quantize (bit-exact `lrintf`, ties to even)
removes the per-element call the congruence rides on.
[HW sweep, objdump, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-19, chains `armAB2` and `armC`]

The quant loops are NEON lanes, and the congruence class has nothing left to form on there.
Both branches of `rk76_quant_act` and `rocket_quant_wt_int8_had` quantize by `fmul`, a float
pre-clamp to +/-127, `fcvtns` and saturating narrows, with amax by `vabs`/`vmax`
(`ggml-rocket` 6e212db). The `fcvtns` instruction rounds to nearest ties-to-even exactly as
`lrintf` does under the default rounding mode. The pre-clamp makes the narrows match the
scalar's integer clamp on every value, including the +/-127.5 ties. The lane form is
therefore bit-identical by construction.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-19]

The `--chunks 4` equality cell prints PPL 8.7433 to every digit, per chunk identical to the
prior epoch. In three w1p0-shape cells, `quant+rot` goes from 30.9, 30.8 and 32.5 s a process
to 19.56, 19.73 and 19.63 s. The `wt_quant` bucket goes from 5.80, 5.80 and 6.56 s to 0.976 s
in all three. The other frontend buckets stay within +-1%, and the wall reads +1.9%
(recorded, not a between-process measurement).
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-19]

A loop with no per-element call gives the three-way congruence nothing to form on
[expected over future epochs, design intent, not yet a measurement]. The class persists for
the loops the change did not convert. The `wt_rotate` loop moved +7.3% across this same epoch with
untouched instructions [hypothesis: the same class's small end].
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-19]

The reversed campaign shows the buckets are cell-locked and position-free. With the knob on,
`sumabs` is 10.83-10.85 s a process, and 0 with it off, at every position on both days. The
knob moves `accounted`, `entry` and `calibrate` by its own size and nothing else.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, fourteen profiled processes over three campaigns, 2026-08-13]

The `quant+rot` binary regression is real. The same diff moved that bucket 32.6-32.8 s ->
45.6-47.2 s a process (+43%), seven new-binary readings against three old-binary ones across
two days and three campaigns. It sits on a function the diff does not touch:
`rk76_quant_act` reads the activation and no weight. So the regression belongs to the
binary. The candidates are codegen, or the allocation layout moved by one added per-chunk
vector, on a part whose host loops are L1d-set sensitive.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, fourteen profiled processes over three campaigns, 2026-08-13]

The page-pad arm refutes the moved allocation as the mechanism. Its `padarm.patch` page-pads
the diff's one new `sumabs` vector, the sole change against the shipped source. That arm
reads `quant+rot` at 42.4 s, 29% above the old level. The regression is therefore not that
allocation's alignment. The 7-10% it sits below the new-binary band is 2-3x the bucket
reproduction spread, so a minority layout slice is plausible and unsettled.
[HW sweep, one cell each, 2026-08-13]

The interior is mapped by `quantsplit.patch`, its own binary epoch, equality-gated at
`--chunks 4` PPL 8.7433. Per process at ubatch 512, `q8` (the quantize loop) is 48%, `rot`
(Hadamard) 36%, `max` 12% and `copy` 4%. The four sum to `quant` within 0.14%, which is the
stamps' own overhead, self-reported as the difference. The discriminating tests are the same
stamps in the old binary, differenced per phase, and the `perf stat` cache-counter pair.
[HW sweep, one cell each, 2026-08-13]

The same diff also doubled `wt_quant`, the once-per-weight cache-insert quantization on the
convert line. It went 5.8-6.6 s -> 10.9-12.5 s a process across all fourteen logs. That cost
is outside the timed reps (first-touch), so it costs process startup rather than `pp2048`.
The convert line printed it in every log of the first campaign, and it was read only when
the reversal made it worth reading.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, fourteen profiled processes over three campaigns, 2026-08-13]

#### Share of the prefill wall

The entry's predicted cap transfers, 39% predicted against 31.3% measured, and its denominator does not.
That 39% is 39% of the entry's own wall, measured at `K=N=2048` with no model around it.
Inside a model the entry is a fifth of the prefill wall, so the same term is 4.5% of
prefill, not 39%. Read from the timed pass at ubatch 512:

| term | per converged call, M=512 | per converged call, M=2048 |
|---|---:|---:|
| `valloc` | 0.41 | 7.47 |
| `quant+rot` | 16.99 | 67.07 |
| `entry` | 36.40 | 99.00 |
| `dequant` | 2.01 | 8.01 |
| **offloaded path** | **55.81** | **181.55** |

At ubatch 512 the timed pass runs 664 converged calls, so the offloaded path is 37.1 s of a
169.7 s prefill wall = 21.8%. The other 78% is outside the handler entirely. The per-call
buckets cannot see any of it, so that 78% is a residual and names no term.
[HW sweep, one cell, 2026-08-13]

Each candidate for the 78% is a [hypothesis]:

- CPU attention, with FA declined
- The norms
- Rope
- The scheduler
- Every CPU-placed matmul

The total is not a residual. The inter-call gap accumulator (a diagnostic driver patch, dumped as `gap=` on the frontend profile line) sums the exit->entry gaps between handler
calls. Its first cell read gap 554.2 s beside 149.5 s of buckets over 2656 calls (78.7%),
closing the span. The per-process wall term of the campaigns is therefore directly
observable per process, still without a name. [HW sweep, one cell, 2026-08-13]

The `-ub 2048` lever is worth 4.1% of prefill. Per weight per 2048 tokens, the offloaded
path costs 4 x 55.81 = 223.2 ms at ubatch 512 against 1 x 181.55 at ubatch 2048. That is an
18.7% cut, and over 166 weights it is 37.05 s against 30.14 s on a 169.7 s wall. The entry-side
cut alone is 32.0% (145.6 -> 99.0 ms). **A two-point wall comparison cannot resolve 4%
against this arm's +-15% spread.** That is why the 0.2% reading meant nothing either way.

The `valloc` bucket is not a term. The measurement refutes an expectation that the three
value-initialized scratch vectors are non-trivial: they are 0.59% of the offloaded path at
`M`=512. At `M`=2048 they are 2.7%, where the cost is superlinear in `M`. That is consistent
with fresh pages being faulted rather than with the memset itself.

#### The 78% residual

The 78% residual is CPU matmul, and it is one declined op. The matmul profiler accounts the
offloaded path and nothing else, so the rest of the wall is a residual that names no term.
`perf` attributes all of it. [HW sweep, Qwen2.5-1.5B F16 on llama.cpp b10356,
`-p 2048 -ub 512 -r 3`, `ROCKET_INT8=1`, `ROCKET_FLASH_ATTN=0`, `taskset -c 4-7 -t 4`,
governor pinned, 803.0 s, 509,684 samples (`perf record`, grouped by event group)]

| term | share of the prefill wall |
|---|---:|
| CPU matmul, one tinyBLAS f16 kernel | **62.6%** |
| CPU flash attention, `ggml_compute_forward_flash_attn_ext_tiled` | **19.2%** |
| ggml-rocket host work, `libggml-rocket` user space | 8.0% |
| kernel, the rocket `alloc` / `submit` / `wait` ioctls | 5.3% |
| `lrintf32` and `memcpy`, called by the ggml-rocket quant path | 2.5% |
| ggml's f16 <-> f32 conversion | 1.1% |
| norms + elementwise + copies + every thread barrier | **0.7%** |
| page faults and model load | 0.34% |

**The shares above are wall shares, which is not what `perf` reports by default.** Read as
cycles, the ggml-rocket host work is 3.56% and ggml's GEMM is 91.67%. Weighted by
1/parallelism, they are 7.97% and 82.52%. The 2.2x gap on the ggml-rocket side is exactly the
parallelism gap. The ggml GEMM runs on four threads, while the quant, the packs and the scans
are single-threaded. The wall view requires `perf record --latency` at record time.

The framework is not the residual. Norms, elementwise, copies and every thread barrier
together are 0.7%, and the scheduler and graph allocation do not appear at all. Attention is
not the residual either, at 19.2%. It is a fused CPU kernel, so declining `FLASH_ATTN_EXT`
buys the CPU's tiled implementation rather than a naive path.

The residual is one op the entry declines. The entry refuses `K >= 6176`, and the model's FFN
down-projection has `K`=8960, so all 28 of them run on the CPU. They are 28 of 196 projections
and 29.4% of the model's projection MACs (`down` is 13.76M of 46.8M MAC a layer a token, tied
with `gate` and `up`).

The control says that op is essentially the whole 62.6%. With `ROCKET_INT8=0`, every
projection runs on the CPU, and the same kernel is 91.4% of a 736.9 s wall over 8 forwards.
There it covers 3.399 MAC-units a layer, where the offloaded arm's CPU side covers 1.0. At the
control's own per-unit rate, the 28 declined GEMMs are 24.8 s of the offloaded arm's 50.2 s a
forward. Measured in that arm, they are 31.4 s.

The term is 49-63% of the wall. The 13-point spread is either cache interference from the
interleaved ggml-rocket host work or the two other matmuls the entry does not take. This
instrument cannot separate them, because both are the same symbol.

| per MAC-unit (1 unit = one `gate`/`up`/`down` a layer = 1.973e11 MAC a 512-token forward) | s |
|---|---:|
| four A72 cores, ggml tinyBLAS f16 | **24.8** |
| the NPU offloaded path, converged | **3.86** |
| ratio | **6.4x** |

That 6.4x sits beside the sgemm A/B's 5.4x median. Do not confuse it with the A/B's 2.39x
model-weighted figure. That figure is an end-to-end cap over a whole GEMM mix, not
a per-MAC rate.

The port's converged `pp2048` speedup is 1.96x: 10.91 t/s against the control's 5.57 t/s.
The build, the threads and the governor are the same, and the W8A8 route is the only
difference. This end-to-end number is taken outside the calibration regime. The `pp512`
1.02x / 2.01x pair is a 100% calibration measurement (`pp512 -r 1` reports 332 of 332 calls
as calibration forwards, confirmed directly).

### The W8A8 route against ggml Q8_0 at `pp2048`

Against ggml Q8_0 at `pp2048`, the route is 1.31x. The 1.96x above is against an F16 control,
and the `pp512` 1.02x / 2.01x pair is a 100%-calibration measurement. This section prices the
route at a converged prefill length against the CPU arm it competes with (measured
2026-08-23). The arms run
Qwen2.5-1.5B at `pp2048` and `-ub` 512, on four threads on the A72s, with the governor at
`performance` and one job in flight. Each arm runs three processes, interleaved in one boot:

| arm | GGUF | `pp2048` t/s (three processes) | mean | ratio |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|
| NPU, W8A8 | F16 | 13.37 / 13.34 / 13.38 | 13.36 | n/a |
| CPU only | Q8_0 | 10.31 / 9.95 / 10.27 | 10.18 | **1.31x** |
| CPU only | F16 | 5.64 | 5.64 | 2.37x |

The route is a real but modest win over the strongest CPU arm. It is not the tie the `pp512`
figure implied, and not the 2x the F16 control suggests. The per-pair ratios are
1.30 / 1.34 / 1.30. Quote the F16 ratio and this one together or neither. A drop-in user
reads the F16 ratio, because the port consumes an F16 GGUF. A user who already knows to
quantize gets the Q8_0 ratio.

Run-to-run spread at `pp2048` is small: +-0.02 t/s within a process and 3.5% across the
three Q8_0 processes. **The +-15% spread recorded for this arm elsewhere in this file is a
`pp512` calibration-regime property, not a property of the route.** One process is a
measurement at `pp2048`, and at `pp512` it is not.

The main prefill levers are spent. The host K-split lifts the `K >= 6176` refusal, which costs
62.6% of the wall without it, and resident weights add +10%. A further lever inside the entry
is bounded by a path that already wins 1.31x. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0,
llama.cpp b10558, `ggml-rocket` build of 2026-08-19, measured 2026-08-23]

The largest prefill lever on this part is the `K >= 6176` refusal, not anything inside the
entry. Every lever inside the entry is bounded by the offloaded path's 21.8%. The largest of
them, a BO pool for the per-tile allocations, caps at 4.2%. Routing `ffn_down` onto the NPU
at the offloaded path's own per-unit rate takes the wall from 50.2 s a forward to 22.7 s.
That is 2.21x on prefill, and the port to about 4.3x against pure CPU.

**That figure counts only what the change removes.** With what the change adds enumerated, the
lever is 1.05-1.27x (§"The cost of a host K-split of `ffn_down`" below). The figure prices the
size of the term left on the CPU correctly. It omits the whole cost of putting that term
anywhere else.

The pure planner, `rocket_matmul_plan_int8_rk3576`, costs no device time. Its map over (`M`, `K`, `N`) puts the refusal boundary at
`N`=1536 at exactly `K`=6176, independent of `M` across {58, 128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048}. The
output tile narrows on the way there. The tile width `Nt` is 1536 up to `K`=4096, 768 at
`K`=4480-4608 and 32 at `K`=5120-6144, with `Mt` falling 128 -> 64 -> 32.

**The one device probe of a chunk shape measured the probe, not the entry.** The probe
`rk3576_matmul_probe ncap` at `M`=58 `K`=4480 `N`=1536 refuses with "no row window fits, the
weight slice alone exceeds the CBUF pool at ic=4480 k=1x1". That reads as a refutation of the
chunk route and is not one. Here `mm_int8_ex` builds a `conv_params_t` and drives the raw conv
path with a hand-picked plane, so what refuses is the conv row-window planner. The matmul
entry, which tiles, is a different planner and accepts the same cell. A probe's refusal names
its own clause.

#### The cost of a host K-split of `ffn_down`

The cap above is refuted at its magnitude. The lever is worth 1.05-1.27x on prefill, not
2.21x, and takes the port to 2.06-2.49x, not 4.3x. Three measurements say so, and each also
corrects a piece of the framing the cap was written in.

The preview reconciles with the profiler, and with both its counters. The return value of
`rocket_matmul_plan_int8_rk3576` is `ceil(N/Nt) * ceil(ih/rows)`, N tiles times row tasks. The
profiler in `r76_mm_int8` increments `tiles` once per N tile and `submits` once per row task.
Model-weighted over Qwen2.5-1.5B's six offloaded projections at `M`=512, the preview gives
2.333 tiles and 9.333 submits a call. The measured values are 2.301 and 9.20, the same 0.986
ratio on both, which is what a few zero-tile calls (refusals, folded on every exit) give. So
the preview's `Mt`/`Nt` are the entry's tiling, and a reading against the wrong counter only
appears not to reconcile.

The entry's cost is linear in `K` at fixed (`M`, `N`), with a small per-call intercept. The
table gives warm repetitions of `rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576_perc` at `M`=512 `N`=1536, with the
first rep discarded and the governor pinned to `performance`:

| `K` | 1536 | 1792 | 2048 | 2176 | 2304 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| ms a call | 17.0 | 19.0 | 20.9 | 23.5 | 24.6 |

The fit is 1.94 + 0.00983·`K` ms, with residuals under 0.6 ms over twelve cells on a 64 step.
The `K`-proportional term is split-invariant. These terms are all O(`N*K`) or O(`M*K`) per
call:

- `sumabs`
- The weight cube's `memset`
- `packB`
- `packA`

They sum to the same total however `Ktot` is cut, and the weight BO's `alloc` scales with it
too. A whole `K`=8960 `ffn_down` in `n` chunks therefore costs 1.94`n` + 88.1 ms, and the
chunk count is nearly free. It is 95.9 ms at `n`=4, corroborated at 92 ms of wall and 82 ms
of accounted buckets by a four-chunk run. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 2026-08-11]

Summing independently requantized int8 partials is free. Four chunks (3 x 2304 + 2048) each
carry their own two-pass bootstrap and frozen per-column scale, and each partial is
dequantized by that scale and summed in f32. Against the exact int32 product over the whole
`Ktot`=8960, the composed RMS relative error is 2.2385%. The single-chunk control gives 2.2448%
on chunk 0 alone, a ratio of 0.997, and the shipped route probe gives 2.234% at `K`=1536
`N`=1536. The composition costs nothing measurable. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9,
one seed, 2026-08-11]

The operands are pseudorandom Gaussian, with no rotation. So this says nothing about a real
activation distribution, and nothing about the rotation block size a real split would also
change. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, one seed, 2026-08-11]

The cap, with what the change adds enumerated, is as follows. Per `down` call at `M`=512, the
entry costs 96 ms [measured]. The frontend's rotate-and-quantize of a `K`=8960 activation
costs 96-99 ms ([expected], 21.6 ns an element measured at `K`=1536, scaled by `K` and by the
8/9 butterfly ratio of a 256-block rotation). The dequantize plus f32 sum costs ~3 ms
[expected]. That is 185-200 ms added against the 221-280 ms the CPU GEMM costs. So 112 such
calls a forward save 2.4-10.6 s of 50.2, which is 1.05-1.27x.

The lever is therefore 2-6x the best in-entry lever (1E-4d's 4.2%), not 10-50x it, and the
two are in the same class. The largest single added term is the entry's own O(`N*K`) work,
which a weight cache would remove. The entry has no weight cache, so `sumabs`, the cube
`memset` and `packB` are three passes over `N*K` on every call. Those passes are ~27 ms of the
96 at `Ktot`=8960.

The proposed 2 x 4480 cut is the worst one on the planner's own map. At `M`=512 `N`=1536 the
plan reads:

| `K` | planned submits |
|---|---:|
| 640 | 1 |
| 896-1280 | 2 |
| 1536-2560 | 4 |
| 2880-4096 | 8 |
| **4480-4608** | **32** |
| 5120-6144 | 768 |

At `K`=4480, `Nt` first collapses 1536 -> 768. Two 4480 chunks therefore plan 64 submits,
where four chunks of ~2300 plan 16 at the same four N tiles. Any cut into the 2880-4096 band
costs 8 submits a chunk, and the 1536-2560 band costs 4. [host-computed, pure planner, no device]

#### The host K-split: prefill speed and perplexity

The host K-split runs, and it costs the model 1.60x perplexity. The frontend file
`ggml-rocket.cpp` holds each RK3576 W8A8 weight as a list of K-chunks, one whole W8A8 GEMM per
chunk. Each chunk has its own:

- Rotation
- Per-output-channel weight scale
- Two-pass bootstrap
- Frozen per-column output scale

The frontend sums the dequantized f32 partials. The shipped preference cuts `K`=8960 to
3 x 2304 + 2048. **The split is off by default.** `ROCKET_RK3576_KSPLIT=1` turns it on.

The split is 2.3x on prefill, which is 1.9x what the cap above predicted. The arms run
`pp2048 -b 2048 -ub 2048 -r 3` with the governor pinned to `performance`. The split runs
against the same build's `ROCKET_RK3576_KSPLIT=0`, in two interleaved process pairs:

| pass | nosplit t/s | split t/s | ratio |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 11.71 +- 1.59 | 26.42 +- 8.63 | **2.26** |
| 2 | 11.39 +- 1.51 | 27.01 +- 9.22 | **2.37** |

Those means understate the converged lever, and the standard deviations say why. At
`-ub 2048` a forward is one call per projection, so `NCAL`=2 spends the warmup and the first
timed rep on calibration. The split's calibration is four chunks x two bootstrap passes, where
the control's is one x two. Reading the three reps back out of mean and stddev under the
one-slow-two-fast structure gives a converged 32.3 against 12.3 t/s, or 2.64x. That is an
inference from two summary statistics, not a per-rep reading, and the 2.26-2.37x means are
what is measured.

**Do not multiply either into the port's recorded 1.96x.** That denominator is a pure-CPU F16
arm that was not re-run at these settings, and it costs ~26 minutes a cell.

The per-call terms come from the `ROCKET_MM_PROFILE` buckets, differenced between the two
profile cells over their 27 extra claimed matmuls at `M`=2048. There are 0 redos over 39200
submits:

| term | split - nosplit, per `ffn_down` call at `M`=2048 | quartered to `M`=512 | predicted at `M`=512 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `entry` | 285 ms | 71 | 96 +- 8 |
| `quant+rot` | 289 ms | 72 | 96-99 |
| `calibrate`, per calibration forward | 841 ms | 210 | ~197 |

The quartering assumes both terms are `M`-proportional. The entry's term is only in part
(`packA` is O(`M*K`), `sumabs` and `packB` are not). Read the `M`=2048 column as the
measurement and the rest as arithmetic. Both compute terms land below their bands, in the
direction that makes the lever larger. The cost cap contains nothing about `calibrate`, and it
is the largest of the three terms. It is 122 s even without the split, over two forwards, on a
run whose converged forward is 77 s.

The split is off by default because of what it does to the output. The perplexity arms run
Qwen2.5-1.5B on wikitext-2, 8 windows of 512, in one build and one boot, with `llama-perplexity`:

| arm | what runs on the part | PPL | x CPU | x nosplit |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `cpu` | nothing (F16 CPU) | 9.7872 | 1.000 | |
| `nosplit` | q/k/v/o/gate/up at `K`=1536, `ffn_down` on the CPU | 9.8969 | 1.011 | 1.000 |
| `machinery` | the same set, but `K`=1536 forced to **512+512+512** | 9.8211 | 1.003 | **0.992** |
| `split` | + `ffn_down` as **3 x 2304 + 2048** | 15.8395 | 1.618 | **1.600** |
| `chunk1024` | + `ffn_down` as **8 x 1024 + 512 + 256** | 18.2188 | 1.861 | **1.841** |

`nosplit` reproduces the route's separately recorded 1.011x, which is the harness control.

The split machinery is exact, and that is measured rather than assumed. The `machinery` arm
leaves `ffn_down` on the CPU, so its offloaded set is identical to `nosplit`, and it cuts only
the `K`=1536 projections. At `K`=1536 the block-diagonal fallback is `H_512` over three
aligned blocks, which is bit-identical to what three 512-chunks each take. The rotation is
therefore held fixed. The only things that move are the slicing, the per-chunk activation
scale and the summed partials.

The `machinery` arm comes back at 0.992x, slightly better than unsplit, because each chunk's
per-tensor activation scale covers a narrower slice. The 1.600x is therefore not a defect in
the cut. It is what happens when `ffn_down` reaches the route.

The 1.600x does not come from the frozen output scale, in either direction. The entry's own saturation
report does not fire on either split arm. The rocket channel is confirmed reaching stderr in
the same logs, so the per-column scale is not being outrun. That excludes only the
under-estimation direction. The report's threshold is 0.1% of the surface, not zero, so "does
not fire" means "under a tenth of a percent".

The other direction is measured too. Cutting `CALSAFE` 3.0 -> 1.2, so that a partial spends
106 int8 codes instead of 42, makes the model worse (15.84 -> 17.99). It also starts the
report firing at 0.105-0.225% on four weights. The 3x headroom is not wasted resolution.
Buying resolution back costs more than it returns.

#### Chunk depth and the rotation's block width

The chunking is the accuracy knob, and the rotation's block width is why. The chunk depth is a
bigger lever on this model's output than the whole 1.60x cost of the split. Every cell has the
same `K`=8960, offloaded set, build, boot and eight windows, and only `ROCKET_RK3576_KCHUNK`
moves:

| arm | `ffn_down` cut as | chunks | weighted `H` block width | PPL | x nosplit | windows worse than `split` |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `k2048` | 4x2048 + 512 + 256 | 6 | 1909 | **14.4401** | 1.459 | 3 of 8 |
| `split` (shipped) | 3x2304 + 2048 | 4 | 666 | 15.8395 | 1.600 | n/a |
| `k6144` | 6144 + 2304 + 512 | 3 | 1499 | 17.9495 | 1.814 | 6 of 8 |
| `chunk1024` | 8x1024 + 512 + 256 | 10 | 973 | 18.2188 | 1.841 | n/a |
| `k1792` | 5x1792 | 5 | 256 | 21.8054 | 2.203 | **8 of 8** |
| `k4096` | 4096 + 4096 + 512 + 256 | 4 | 3781 | 22.0116 | 2.224 | **8 of 8** |
| `k1280` | 7x1280 | 7 | 256 | 24.3497 | 2.460 | **8 of 8** |

The rotation is block-diagonal `H_b`, with `b` the largest power of two dividing the chunk's
`K` (`rk_hadamard_rotate_blocks`). A chunk depth therefore chooses how far an outlier is
smeared. A chunk of `K`=2048 gets a full 2048-wide FWHT, and one of `K`=1792 gets `H_256`
seven times over.

The reading "more chunks is worse, monotone and steep" is refuted as stated: it is a confound.
The `k2048` arm cuts `ffn_down` into six chunks and scores better than the shipped four. The
`k1792` arm cuts it into five and scores decisively worse. The `chunk1024` cell varies more
than the count. Cutting a `K` like 8960 into more pieces at non-power-of-two depths also
narrows every piece's rotation, so the two move together. Held at a fixed `b`=256, the count
term is real and much smaller: 5 chunks score 21.81 against 24.35 for 7 chunks.

The rotation width does not order the set either. The `k4096` arm has the widest rotation
reachable, 91% of `K` under a full `H_4096`. Every chunk is a power of two, and the chunk count
matches the shipped arm's. It scores 22.01, 8 of 8 windows worse and level with the narrowest
arm measured. The `k6144` arm has the fewest chunks and twice the shipped arm's weighted
width, and it is 2.11 PPL worse. Any model built on width and count alone fails on both rows.

The chunk depth has an interior optimum. Three of the arms are pure powers of two, so every
chunk takes a full-width FWHT. The rotation construction is therefore constant across them,
and only the depth moves:

| pure-pow2 depth | cut | chunks | PPL |
|---:|---|---:|---:|
| 1024 | 8x1024 + 512 + 256 | 10 | 18.2188 |
| **2048** | 4x2048 + 512 + 256 | 6 | **14.4401** |
| 4096 | 2x4096 + 512 + 256 | 4 | 22.0116 |

Both directions off 2048 are worse. **A one-sided search on this axis finds a monotone story
in whichever direction it starts.** Every cell in this table uses a per-tensor activation
scale, and that scale is the whole of the effect. Re-run with a per-row scale, the same three
chunkings land within 0.04 PPL of each other and of `nosplit` (below). The optimum is
therefore a property of the scale's granularity, not of the depth.

Read the paired per-window evidence, not only the headline PPL. These arms are deterministic.
Both controls reproduce across sessions and boots to four decimals. The reported `+-0.6` to
`+-1.04` is therefore the spread of one arm's own windows, not run-to-run noise. The sample is
the eight windows. A paired-window reader backs the per-window
mean NLL out of the running estimate to compare the arms pairwise:

| arm | paired difference, mean NLL | windows worse |
|---|---|---:|
| `k1792` | +0.3197 +- 0.0813 | 8 of 8 |
| `k1280` | +0.4300 +- 0.1057 | 8 of 8 |
| `k4096` | +0.3291 +- 0.0804 | 8 of 8 |
| `k2048` | -0.0925 +- 0.0566 | 3 of 8 |
| `k6144` | +0.1251 +- 0.0604 | 6 of 8 |
| `CALSAFE`=1.2 | +0.1270 +- 0.0793 | 5 of 8 |

Only the two narrow arms, `k1792` and `k1280`, separate cleanly. The +-1.4 PPL moves
(`k2048`, `k6144` and `CALSAFE`=1.2) are 1.6 to 2.1 standard errors and are not established at
eight windows. **A headline PPL that moves 1.4 on this harness can rest on three windows.** So
`k2048`'s 1.459x is the best cell measured, and not yet a better configuration.

#### Per-row activation scales

The term is the per-tensor input scale. Giving each row its own scale takes `ffn_down` on the
split route to `nosplit` quality. With `ROCKET_RK3576_AROW=1` the route scores 9.9071 against
the same binary's shipped-path 15.9894, 0 of 8 windows worse. The paired difference is
-0.4787 +- 0.1045 of mean NLL. That is 1.0010x of the `nosplit` control's 9.8969, and 1.012x
of the CPU F16 arm. It reproduces to four decimals across builds and boots (9.9348 twice,
9.9071 on the other build).

The arm is not a fallback. It runs at 67.78 s per pass, the split arm's own speed, against
`nosplit`'s 80.29, so `ffn_down` is on the part throughout.

The per-row scale is free at this interface and needs no library change. The entry takes A
already quantized, and its `scale` parameter is the output's. A per-row activation scale
therefore never crosses the ioctl. The frontend normalizes each row to +-127 before the submit
and applies the row factor in the host dequantize.

This result is a rival to the recorded per-axis-input-scales result (better on every per-GEMM
norm, 38x worse end to end on one model of two). That one is measured on `K`=1536 projections
and this one on `ffn_down`, and a single-model reading of that kind is model-specific. **Neither result bounds the other, and neither bounds a second model.**

**An avoidable fp32 re-association in the dequantize moves the shipped arm 15.8395 ->
15.9894.** Folding `a_scale` into the per-column factor once, as the shipped path does, is not
the same float as multiplying it per element. At 0.4 of a paired standard error that is not
an accuracy change. It does cost the shipped path its bit-for-bit reproducibility across
builds, which is the property every arm on this route is read against. The per-tensor case
therefore keeps the original association, and the per-row case is a separate branch.

None of this says the split is the wrong mechanism. The mechanism is fast and exact, and the
op it unlocks needs an activation quantization the shipped route does not give it.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-11]

#### Chunk depth under a per-row scale

Re-running the depth ladder with `ROCKET_RK3576_AROW=1` collapses a 9.9 PPL spread to 0.04.
The three cells hold the chunking fixed against the per-tensor table above, and change only
the activation scale's granularity:

| cut | chunks | per-tensor scale | per-row scale | paired dNLL vs `nosplit` | windows worse |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|
| 3x2304 + 2048 (shipped) | 4 | 15.8395 | **9.9348** | +0.0038 +- 0.0048 | 5 of 8 |
| 4x2048 + 512 + 256 | 6 | 14.4401 | **9.8944** | -0.0003 +- 0.0045 | 6 of 8 |
| 7x1280 | 7 | 24.3497 | **9.9012** | +0.0004 +- 0.0045 | 5 of 8 |

The `nosplit` control is 9.8969. All three per-row cells sit inside one standard error of it,
and none is worse on more than 6 of 8 windows. The same three chunkings with a per-tensor
scale are +0.4703, +0.3778 and +0.9003, at 8 of 8. Pairwise against each other, the three
per-row arms are within 1.1 se (4 of 8 worse each way). They are one cell, not a ladder.

This identifies the term the depth trades. The first candidate is the per-tensor scale's
cross-row reach. A chunk's divisor is a `max` over `M*K` rotated values, so one outlier row
sets the resolution for every other row of that chunk. The second candidate is the number of
independently requantized partials, an output-side count that no input scale touches. The
first accounts for essentially all of it. The second is bounded at <= 0.04 PPL over 4 to 7
chunks, so it is not a term.

The interior optimum at 2048 is a property of the per-tensor arm and does not survive the
per-row scale. On this route, **`ROCKET_RK3576_KCHUNK` is a cost knob, not an accuracy one.**
Choose a chunk size on the entry's cost law and the stall map alone. This does not bound a
chunking far outside 4-7 pieces, and it says nothing about a `K` other than 8960.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-11]

#### A second model: SmolLM2-1.7B

On SmolLM2-1.7B the split costs 6.77x, and the per-row scale still closes it. SmolLM2-1.7B is
the corpus's other ~1.5B member, and it disagrees with Qwen2.5-1.5B on a term's size by 9x and
by 38x. Its `ffn_down` is `K`=8192, which the greedy chunker cuts into 3x2304 + 1024 + 256.
That is five chunks, with a different tail from Qwen's four:

| arm | PPL | x `nosplit` | paired dNLL | windows worse |
|---|---:|---:|---|---:|
| `nosplit` | 10.3436 | n/a | n/a | n/a |
| `split` | **70.0420** | **6.77** | +1.9127 +- 0.6832 | 8 of 8 |
| `split` + `AROW` | **10.3354** | **0.9992** | -0.0008 +- 0.0043 | **3 of 8** |

The per-tensor split hurts this model 4.2x harder than it hurts Qwen: 6.77x against 1.600x.
The saturation report fires at 15.417% of the surface on `blk.7.ffn_down`, where Qwen's worst
arm reaches 0.225%. The per-row scale takes the model back to indistinguishable from
`nosplit`, on the wrong side of zero and worse on only 3 of 8 windows. So the fix is not
Qwen-specific. The per-axis input-scales result (38x worse end to end on one
model of two) does not reach this interface. That result is measured on `K`=1536 projections
under a different quantization of the output, and the two do not bound each other.

Both models therefore say the same thing about the mechanism. What the K-split costs is the
per-tensor activation scale, and nothing else measured here.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-11]

#### A third model: Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct

A third model separates the disagreement from the geometry, and the disagreement is in the
weights. The two models above differ in both their pretraining and their contraction
geometry, so that pair cannot say which one the 4.2x comes from. Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct
carries SmolLM2-1.7B's K set exactly: `n_embd` 2048 and `n_ff` 8192, read off the GGUF
headers, against Qwen2.5-1.5B's 1536/8960. It comes from a third pretraining family, with 16
blocks against 24 and GQA against MHA. Holding the geometry, and therefore the K-split's own
cut, fixed against one recorded model makes it a discriminator rather than a third data
point.

Four cells run at `--chunks 64` on `wiki-test-full.txt` with `-ub 512`, one process each:

| arm | what runs on the part | PPL, 64 windows | x `nosplit` |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| `nosplit` | q/k/v/o/gate/up at `K`=2048, `ffn_down` on the CPU | 15.3461 | 1.000 |
| `ctl` = `split` + `AROW` | + `ffn_down` cut, per-row activation scale | 15.4192 | 1.0048 |
| `ncal1` | `ctl` at `NCAL`=1 | 15.5343 | 1.0123 |
| `split` | + `ffn_down` cut, per-tensor scale | **37.2952** | **2.4303** |

The per-tensor split's damage is a property of the weights, not of the cut. Depth-matched at
the 8 windows the other two ratios are measured at, this model reads 2.0100x. That is 1.26x
from Qwen's 1.600x and 3.37x from SmolLM2's 6.772x, while running SmolLM2's identical
`K`=8192 cut. The whole-offload cost says the same thing from an independent contrast. The
`ctl` arm against `nosplit` is +0.476% (paired +0.0048 +/- 0.0021 of mean NLL), which sits
with Qwen's +0.567% and not with SmolLM2's -0.079%.

The split's damage grows with depth, and the whole-offload cost wanders over the same three
depths:

| windows | `split` x `nosplit` | whole-offload cost, `ctl` against `nosplit` |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 8 | 2.0100x | +0.863% |
| 29 | 2.3434x | +0.123% |
| 64 | 2.4303x | +0.476% |

The two recorded 8-window ratios are therefore lower bounds on their own depth-64 values, and
the disagreement's magnitude at depth is unmeasured. The direction is what the geometry
refutation needs, and it survives depth-matching. The wandering whole-offload cost is one more
reading that the 8-window instrument is inadequate on this route.

The converged-window cost of `NCAL`=1 is small, positive and real. Its indistinguishability on
two models is a power artifact. Over windows 3-64 this model puts `ncal1` at +0.0074 +/- 0.0025
se above `ctl`, 3.0 se from zero. It is worse on 41 of 62 windows, sign test two-sided
p = 0.0151. Qwen separates the same term at 0.56 se and SmolLM2 at 0.55 se, which is what the
reading "does not reach either model" reports. Depth-matched, the three agree: 0.69 se from
Qwen at `d`=8 and 1.45 se from SmolLM2 at `d`=29.

Pooled by inverse variance at each model's deepest cell, the three are homogeneous
(Q = 1.360 on 2 df). The pooled cost is +0.00564 +/- 0.00199 nats = +0.566% of perplexity,
2.83 se from zero.

Scope: one corpus, one `-ub`, a first prompt only, no decode, and the `K` set {2048, 8192}
alone. This model is instruction-tuned where SmolLM2 is a base checkpoint, and these cells
cannot separate that from family.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, `.so` `ea40bc1b`, 2026-08-20]

#### The perplexity instrument

Every cell on this route is deterministic to four decimals. A re-run on a rebuilt `.so`
reproduces its predecessor's cumulative sequence byte-for-byte. A headline's uncertainty is
therefore entirely which windows the text contains, never the measurement's. The
sample is the windows, and the instrument's resolution follows from two measured numbers.

The text, `wiki-test-full.txt`, is `grep -v "^ *$"` over wikitext-2-raw's `wiki.test.raw`. It
is 1287656 bytes and 2891 lines, md5 `3ce70e93`, and holds 298938 tokens under Qwen2.5's
tokenizer -> 583 windows at `n_ctx`=512. The file `wiki-test-small.txt` (md5 `e454e460`) is
exactly its first 60190 bytes, which caps it at 29 windows.

Because the small file is a byte-prefix, the deeper text is a strict extension. Window *i*
covers tokens `[i*512, (i+1)*512)` taken from the start. The seam at byte 60190 falls at token
~15000, against window 29 ending at 14848. The `--chunks` flag is therefore the depth dial,
with every recorded cell preserved as a prefix. This is measured, not assumed. A `--chunks 64`
control cell on the full text reproduces all 29 recorded cumulative values of the 29-window
control byte-for-byte, ending [29]**8.5745**.

The spread is the paired per-window difference on this route, with sd 0.01734 nats measured
over 64 windows. The first 29 give 0.01853, and the 35 beyond them give 0.01655. These are
homogeneous, so no phase correction applies past the `NCAL` calibration windows. The
resolution is `2*sd/sqrt(n)`:

| windows | 2-se floor (nats) | 2-se floor (PPL) | cost, Qwen, split on |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 29 | 0.0064 | 0.65% | 11 min |
| **64** | **0.0043** | **0.43%** | **21 min** |
| 128 | 0.0031 | 0.31% | 42 min |
| 256 | 0.0022 | 0.22% | 84 min |
| 583 (the whole text) | 0.0014 | 0.14% | ~3.2 h |

The corpus therefore has a floor, and two live questions sit under it. Establishing an effect
of size *d* at 2 se needs `(2*sd/d)^2` windows: additivity's 0.0057 needs 38 and the
regime-transfer DiD's 0.0051 needs 47, both reachable. The measured `AROW`-scoping
recovery of 0.00127 needs 746, against the 583 the whole of wikitext-2 test affords. That
effect cannot be established against zero on this corpus at any depth, and deciding its sign
needs a different corpus, not a longer run. Before spending board time on a question, state
which of the two it is.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-13]

#### The `AROW` dial off the split route

`AROW` is a global dial, and off the split route it is a net loss of 0.415%. The frontend
reads `ROCKET_RK3576_AROW` once per context and hands it to `rk76_quant_act` and
`rk76_dequant` on every offloaded GEMM. The K-split does not scope it. The split only decides
which GEMMs are offloaded at all. **A cell that turns the split off still carries the dial,
unless the dial is turned off too.** No arm recorded before this one separates them.

The site map comes from the `[rk3576-cache]` line the driver already prints, with
`ROCKET_DEBUG=1` plus `llama-perplexity -v`. The line goes through `GGML_LOG_DEBUG` and lands
on **stdout**. At `-ub` 512, Qwen2.5-1.5B offloads 197 GEMM sites under `KSPLIT`=1. Of those,
169 are taken whole at K=1536 in one chunk, and 28 are cut at K=8960 into
`2304+2304+2304+2048`, one per layer. Under `KSPLIT`=0 the entry's `K >= 6176` bound refuses
those same 28, and they run on the CPU. The two `nosplit` cells below therefore differ on
exactly the 169.

Three arms run on Qwen2.5-1.5B in one build and one boot, paired window by window, at both
depths. A level is only comparable within a depth, and the paired difference is the quantity:

| arm | `KSPLIT` | `AROW` | PPL @29 | PPL @64 | against the row above, @64 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `nosplit_a0` | 0 | 0 | 8.5236 | 9.3653 | n/a |
| `nosplit` | 0 | 1 | 8.5723 | 9.4042 | **+0.415%**, paired +0.00415 +/- 0.00208 (1.99 se) |
| `ctl` | 1 | 1 | 8.5745 | 9.4184 | **+0.151%**, paired +0.00151 +/- 0.00197 (0.77 se) |

Only the aggregate is established, and the decomposition is not. The whole offload feature,
`ctl` against `nosplit_a0`, both dial and split, costs +0.567%, paired +0.00565 +/- 0.00204
(2.77 se). That figure is stable across depths (+0.597% at 29). Neither of the two terms it
splits into reaches 2 se on its own.

**Do not quote a ratio between them.** At 29 windows they read 0.571% against 0.026%, which
puts the dial at "22x the split". At 64 they read 0.415% against 0.151%, and the same ratio is
2.7x. The denominator is not distinguishable from zero, so the ratio is not a measurement. A ratio whose denominator has not been separated
from zero carries no information about its size.

The dial's regime-dependent sign holds, and it is a large effect, not a statistical one. With
the split on, a per-tensor activation scale is catastrophic (Qwen 15.8395 against 9.9348), so
**`AROW` is mandatory there**. With the split off, it is a loss on every site that never
needed it. That is the same direction the per-axis input scales take end to end, at
a different interface.

The recorded 8-window pair quoted as the K-split feature's accuracy cost is `nosplit` 9.8969
against `arow` 9.9348 (+0.38%). **That pair is the dial plus the split, not the split alone.**
That is why it is 15x the split's own 8-window figure.

The build term on this path is exactly zero, measured three times. The `nosplit_a0` cell
reproduces the recorded 8-window `nosplit` cell byte-for-byte on all eight of its cumulative
values. On the deeper text, both `ctl_L` and `nosplit_a0_L` reproduce their recorded 29-window
predecessors byte-for-byte on all 29. The 0.28% separating the two recorded `arow` levels is
therefore confined to code that only the AROW/split route executes.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-13]

#### The dial scoped to the split's own weights

Scoping the dial to the split's own weights recovers at most 0.43%, not the 0.571% the dial
costs off the route. The setting `ROCKET_RK3576_AROW=2` keeps the per-row activation scale
only where the K-split cuts the weight (`w.ch.size() > 1`). It needs no second activation
quantization. The decision is a property of the weight, and every chunk in one call belongs to
one weight.

The chunk loop already quantizes each chunk's slab separately with its own scale, so the mode
is one boolean at three call sites. The calibration forwards take the same flag, so the frozen
scales match the mode. The chunk map is unchanged (169 x 1 + 28 x 4).

| arm | windows | `KSPLIT` | `AROW` | PPL | against `ctl` |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `ctl2` | 29 | 1 | 1 (all 197) | **8.5745** | n/a (reproduces `ctl` byte-for-byte on all 29) |
| `arow2` | 29 | 1 | 2 (28 only) | **8.5690** | **-0.064%**, paired -0.0006 +/- 0.0034 (0.19 se) |
| `ctl_L` | **64** | 1 | 1 (all 197) | **9.4184** | n/a (reproduces `ctl` byte-for-byte on its first 29) |
| `arow2_L` | **64** | 1 | 2 (28 only) | **9.4064** | **-0.127%**, paired **-0.00127 +/- 0.00217** (0.59 se) |

A 64-window level is not comparable to a 29-window one. Only the paired difference is. The
deeper cells split as windows 1-29 at -0.00064 +/- 0.00344 (reproducing the shallow pair to
the digit) and 30-64 at -0.00180 +/- 0.00280. The effect is therefore homogeneous across the
text, and the deeper windows do not carry it.

At 64 windows additivity is refuted, and the null is not. The 2-se interval on the recovery is
[-0.0056, +0.0031]. It excludes the -0.0057 an additive decomposition predicts (2.04 se, just
past the floor) and comfortably contains zero (0.59 se). The scoping is therefore worth
somewhere between nothing and about a fifth of what the dial costs off the route. The +0.571%
is ruled out. No depth reachable on this corpus can separate 0.127% from 0 (746 windows
needed, 583 available).

The dial's +0.571% does not transfer, and the reason is structural rather than statistical.
Isolating the dial requires turning the split off, which moves the 28 `ffn_down` to the CPU in
f32. The +0.571% is therefore the dial's cost in a regime the scoping does not ship in. The
reachability argument is exact (the same 169 sites, by readout), and the magnitude still moves
8.9x.

**The `(KSPLIT, AROW)` corners do not compose.** The fourth one, `KSPLIT`=1 `AROW`=0, is
catastrophic at 15.84 because it disarms the split sites too. The scoped mode is therefore a
fifth cell and cannot be interpolated. Two dials that each look additive alone need
their fourth corner measured before either is described as composing with the other.

This arm cannot decide whether the scoping is worth anything at all. That effect is 0.59 se
from zero, and out of reach of the corpus. The scope is one model, one `-ub`, a first prompt
only, and nothing about decode. Shipping the mode is a maintainer decision, and the accuracy
case for it is bounded above by 0.43%, not argued from 0.571%.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-13]

#### `AROW` cost in the per-call buckets

`AROW` costs 8.5% of the dequantize and nothing else, which is ~0.5% of a forward. The lever
is priced at the frontend profiler's per-call buckets, not at the wall.

The run is six interleaved processes, `split` / `arow` / `split` / `arow` / `split` / `arow`,
after a discarded warm-up process. The governor is pinned to `performance` under
`taskset -c 4-7`, on Qwen2.5-1.5B `pp2048 -ub 2048`. Each process makes 772 calls, of which
386 are calibration forwards. All 7 of 7 processes return rc=0, with 0 redos, a backstop delta
of 0 and zero `WARNING`s. The table gives per-call ms, the bucket total over `calls`:

| bucket | `split` mean | `arow` mean | delta | 9 pairings | control-to-control spread |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|
| `valloc` | 2.23 | 2.23 | -0.02% | -0.41 .. +0.41 | 0.35% |
| `quant+rot` | 82.62 | 83.45 | +1.00% | -0.70 .. +3.15 | **0.82%** |
| `calibrate` | 220.33 | 224.12 | +1.72% | -0.73 .. +5.11 | 0.63% |
| `entry` | 61.68 | 61.45 | -0.37% | -9.54 .. +11.33 | **9.38%** |
| `dequant` | 9.06 | 9.83 | **+8.50%** | +8.10 .. +8.92 | **0.57%** |
| `quant+rot` + `dequant` | 91.69 | 93.29 | **+1.75%** | +0.18 .. +3.72 | 0.80% |

The whole price is in `dequant`, at +8.50%. Its nine pairings fall between +8.10 and +8.92,
about 15x that bucket's own control spread, and it is the tightest cell on this route. The cost
is the per-row branch's one extra f32 multiply per output element per chunk, which the
per-tensor branch hoists into `f[n]`. The net is +1.60 ms a call at 193 calls a forward. That
is +0.31 s against a converged forward of 62-67 s on the same processes, ~0.5%.

The per-tensor branch's second flat pass over the rotated activation costs nothing
measurable, which refutes the obvious mechanism. The per-tensor branch of `rk76_quant_act`
writes `rot` in the row loop, then makes a second flat pass over `rot[M*K]` to quantize. That
pass re-reads 73.4 MB per tensor per call at `M`=2048 `K`=8960, which is 2.05 GB a forward.
The per-row branch never makes it, because it quantizes each row while its 9.2 KB is still in
a 32 KiB L1d.

Removing the pass moves `quant+rot` by +1.00%, under 0.6 ms of an 82.6 ms/call bucket, with
pairings spanning zero. So that loop is not bandwidth-bound. A byte count is not a cost, and
the 2 GB a forward of streaming re-read is invisible next to whatever `quant+rot` spends its
time on. The candidate is `rk76_rotate`, which is unmeasured [hypothesis].

On the terms this lever touches, the buckets are a 5x tighter instrument than the wall. The
buckets that are not tight show where this route's process-to-process variance lives. Over
the three identical `split` processes, the host arithmetic buckets spread 0.35-0.82%, against
the wall's 4.1% on the same processes. The `entry` bucket spreads 9.38% (12.52% on the `arow`
arm).
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-11]

The matmul profile decomposes that spread over all six processes, at an identical 2940 tiles
and 47040 submits in each:

| Matmul profile term | Range over the six processes |
|---|---|
| `packA` | 13295-24188 ms (+82%) |
| `read` | 19338-24581 ms (+27%) |
| `wait` | 35626-35883 ms (0.7%) |
| `submit`, `packB`, `sumabs`, `coeff`, `gen`, `alloc`, `stamp` | Inside 1.5% |

The variance is the host's activation pack and output readback, not the device. Neither term
is arm-correlated, since both arms span the same `packA` range. The last two of the six
processes carry the two highest values, so the variance drifts within one run. **The
interleave is what protects a measurement here.** A blocked three-then-three design would have
taken up to 50% of `packA`'s range as signal.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-11]

#### Resolution of a `pp2048` wall

A `pp2048` wall cannot resolve a term of this size, and its spread is not a constant. A
`llama-bench` `pp2048 -ub 2048` pair cannot price this lever, because the control arm's own
process-to-process spread is larger than anything the lever can do. The run below is five
processes, with the governor pinned to `performance`, `taskset -c 4-7`, thermals at 47-51 C
and a backstop delta of 0:

| process | arm | converged s/forward | t/s |
|---:|---|---:|---:|
| 1 | `split` (discarded warm-up process) | 61.87 | 33.10 |
| 2 | `split` | 84.32 | 24.29 |
| 3 | `split` + `AROW` | 76.34 | 26.97 |
| 4 | `split` | 74.31 | 27.56 |
| 5 | `split` + `AROW` | 76.64 | 26.72 |

Reps 2-4 within each process agree to under 1%, for example 61.88/61.87/62.53 and
84.56/84.32/84.85. Rep 1 is always ~2x the rest, which is `NCAL`=2 landing on the warm-up plus
the first timed rep, exactly as recorded. Between processes, the identical `split` arm reads
33.10, 24.29 and 27.56 t/s, a 36% spread.

The two adjacent pairs disagree in sign: +11.0% for `AROW` on one, -3.1% on the other. **The
state is latched at process start**, since nothing drifts inside a process. The leading
candidate is a 3.1 GB mmap and a second 3.4 GB model file competing for a 7.9 GB box's page
cache, and it is untested.

The 36% is not a constant of the instrument. Six further processes on the same board, build
and pinning read converged `split` at 32.75/32.80, 32.31/32.54 and 31.51/31.46 t/s, a 4.1%
spread. The `arow` arm reads 30.64/30.60, 31.81/31.86 and 32.47/32.46. Those two arms differ
by -1.8%, the same sign the buckets give and 3.7x the magnitude. The instrument's control
spread is 2.2x that effect, so the wall agrees with the buckets and still cannot resolve it.

What separates the 36% run from the 4.1% one is its timing. It ran within the hour in which a
3.4 GB second model was written to the box's 7.9 GB of RAM. That keeps the page-cache
candidate alive and leaves it a [hypothesis]. One 36% observation is not a rate.

The operational rule survives the smaller spread. **No `pp2048` wall number on this route
means anything from fewer than three processes per arm.** That rule reaches back to the
K-split's own recorded 2.26-2.37x, which was taken over two process pairs. To price a term
inside the W8A8 handler, difference the frontend profiler's `quant+rot` and `dequant` buckets
instead. Those buckets are per call and hold to under 1% where the wall does not.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 7.1.7, 2026-08-11]

#### An int8 matmul job can raise no completion, and the shape decides

Specific `K` make an otherwise ordinary int8-output matmul job not complete. The `rocket`
driver retires every submit at its 125 ms backstop
(`job of 1 task(s) did not report completion in 125000 us; retiring it`). A retired job leaves
a **partial** surface. At `M`=512 `N`=1536, every row is exact up to one column and wrong from
it on:

| `K` | First wrong column | Wrong elements under a write check alone |
|---|---|---|
| 2208 | 384 | 589824 of 786432 |
| 2240 | 320 | 622592 of 786432 |
| 2272 | 288 | 638976 of 786432 |
| 2432 | 160 | 704512 of 786432 |
| 4480 | 160, in each 768-column tile | 622592 of 786432 |

**A guard that asks only whether each task wrote passes that surface.** Under a write check
alone, `rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576()` returned 0 over it at all five `K`, and at 2240 in three
processes of three. With each job's completion scored as well (§"A retired job is redone
whatever it wrote"), the entry **refuses** at all five, after eight retired attempts and about
1.88 s. On the same plan, `K`=2304 stays exact under both guards, at 23.7-26.4 ms.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rocket` 1.6.0, kernel 7.2.3, `tests/rk3576_retire_probe real K`,
scored against the requant model, 2026-09-27]

##### The weight-phase rule

Where a job stops is an arithmetic rule on the weight slice. Output-channel group `g` of a
direct task completes only if

```
(g*S mod 64 KiB) + S <= R_w
S   = 32*ic*kh*kw bytes, one 32-channel group's weight slice (32*K for the matmul)
R_w = 64 KiB * floor((3072 - F) / 1024), the CBUF weight area the feature allowance F leaves
```

The phase counts from the weight base register. The weight area `R_w` is 192 KiB at `F`=0,
128 KiB at `F`=256, 512 and 1024, and 64 KiB at `F`=2048. The first group that breaks the rule
is not written, and neither is any later group. The job raises no completion and `rocket`
retires it at the backstop, with the groups before it exact.

Why the unit is 64 KiB is not known. A likely mechanism, not isolated: the weight side stages
the CBUF in 64 KiB units and holds each 64 KiB window of the slice at once [hypothesis].

`tests/rk3576_stallk_map` runs one job per row task, with no retry. It scores each 32-channel
group against the host int64 reference. In all, 206 jobs over 54 arms stop where the rule puts
them. Every job it calls clean is exact.

| Allowance, plane × rows | `K`: first unwritten channel | Clean `K` |
|---|---|---|
| `F` 1024, 64×2, `M` 512, `N` 1536 | 2144: 672, 2208: 384, 2240: 320, 2272: 288, 2336: 224, 2368: 192, 2400: 160, 2432: 160, 2464, 2496, 2528: 128 | 2080, 2112, 2176, 2304, 2560 |
| `F` 0, 32×1, `M` 512, `N` 768 | 4192: 672, 4384: 224, 4416: 192, 4448, 4480: 160, 4512, 4544, 4576: 128 | 4128, 4160, 4224, 4352, 4608 |

Each clean `K` sits exactly on the edge the rule draws. A `K` of 2048 + d or 4096 + d stays
clean at any tile width exactly when d is a power of two.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rocket` 1.6.0, kernel 7.2.3, BO pool off, 2026-09-27]

The allowance moves the stall, and the rows, `M` and the address do not. At `K`=2240:

- `Mt` 32, 64 and 96 at `F`=0 are clean on four planes, `M` 192 included.
- `Mt` 32 and 64 with `F` forced to 1024 stall at channel 320.
- `Mt` 128 as 32×4, 64×2 and 128×1 stalls at 320, and so does every row task at `M` 1024 and 2048.
- A weight base 1-60 KiB into its buffer leaves `K`=2240 at 320 and `K`=2304 clean, so the phase is not the IOVA's.

The weight area counts in whole 64 KiB units. Forced to `F`=256 and 512, whose areas are 176
and 160 KiB by the granule count, `K` 3328-3968 stall at 32 channels and `K`=4096 is clean. A
linear 176 or 160 KiB area would have left 3328, 3584, 3712 and 3840 clean.

The same rule reproduces the convolution slice caps with no fitted constant. At `F`=0, a task
of four groups keeps this many of them at each slice:

| Slice at `F`=0 | Groups kept, of four |
|---:|---:|
| 144 KiB | 4 |
| 145 KiB | 3 |
| 148 KiB | 3 |
| 152 KiB | 2 |
| 156 KiB | 2 |
| 162 KiB | 1 |

At `F`=1024 the last exact slice is 96 KiB for 2 groups, 85 KiB for 3 and 80 KiB for 4. At
`F`=2048 it is 32, 21 and 16 KiB for the same counts. The escapes at 96 and 128 KiB (`F`=1024)
and 32 and 64 KiB (`F`=2048) follow too. The reserve in `r76_weight_slice_cap_at()` is this
arithmetic. This rests on the convolution slice-cap measurements, and no new arm with a kernel
larger than 1 was run against it.

##### Planning around the weight-phase rule

The int8 matmul plans around the rule, so no job stalls. The entry takes whichever of two
plans needs fewer jobs. One shortens the row window until its allowance holds the whole tile.
The other caps the tile at the groups the planner's own window holds. The function
`rocket_rk3576_weight_phase_groups()` states the rule, and `ROCKET_RK3576_MM_PHASE=0` turns
the plan off.

| `K` at `M` 512 `N` 1536 | Plan | First call |
|---|---|---|
| 2144, 2208, 2240, 2272, 2336, 2432, 2528 | one-row windows, full width, 8 jobs | 23.2-30.3 ms |
| 2304, 2560 (no stall) | unchanged | 23.8-27.0 ms, 28.5-32.3 ms |
| 4192 | 672-channel tiles | 62.0-63.6 ms |
| 4480, 4576 | 160- and 128-channel tiles | 83.2-90.0 ms |
| 4608 (no stall) | unchanged | 65.8-66.9 ms |

Every cell is exact with no retirement, on the first call and the repeat, three processes a
`K`, with the narrowing below turned off. At `K`=2240 the entry is also exact at `M` 192, 1024
and 2048, and the resident-weight entry is exact at four stall shapes.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rocket` 1.6.0, `tests/rk3576_retire_probe`, `tests/rk3576_mm_requant`,
2026-09-27]

The convolution planner plans by the same rule. The slice-cap table passes three bands the
rule stalls, each with enough output-channel groups:

- A slice in (64, 80] KiB at `F`=1024.
- A slice in (128, 144] KiB at `F`=0.
- At `F`=2048, any slice that does not divide 64 KiB, once the whole cube is past 192 KiB (the
  cube charge keeps a smaller cube off `F`=2048).

Planned by the slice-cap table alone, each shape stops where the rule says:

| Shape | Slice, allowance | Exact at | Refuses at |
|---|---|---|---|
| IC 96, k5, 48×64 | 75 KiB, `F`=1024 | 160 channels | 192 |
| IC 480, k3, 8×8 | 135 KiB, `F`=0 | 288 | 320 |
| IC 32, k3, 90×128 | 9 KiB, one task at `F`=2048 | 224 | 704 |

Each refusal is eight retirements with the surface partly written. The planner skips an
allowance whose area cannot hold the task's groups, and caps the tile at what the `F`=0 area
holds. With that skip, every one of these shapes computes exact with no retirement. They run
in 9-53 ms [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rocket` 1.6.0, `tests/rk3576_conv_band_probe`, 2026-09-27]. The skip covers the int8 direct path it was measured on.

##### The rule on the other convolution paths

The other convolution paths, and the fp16 matmul form, measure as follows:

- **One fp16 program writes 32 output channels and no more**, at every allowance, kernels 1-7,
  planes 8×16 to 64×176, and one and two input-channel slices. Past it the task completes,
  channels 32 and up are never written, and the call returns 0 with no retirement. An fp16
  call at more than 32 output channels is wrong unless its output channels are tiled, and the
  entry tiles them at 32. This is the same bound as the float first conv's.
- **The fp16 program's group for the rule is the float cube's 16 channels.** At `F`=2048, k9 and
  k11 at oc 32 on a 64×176 plane leave channels 16-31 unwritten in 6 of 6 runs. A 200 ms
  settle does not bring them back. The job completes on `PC_DONE`, so a stall on this path
  does not retire. The entry caps the tile at the rule's groups.
