- **The depthwise planner charges the whole cube**, which keeps it off the rule except at the
  area's edge. At `F`=2048, k3 on 16×10, a 65088-byte cube is exact, 65376 bytes is wrong in
  channels 0-47 and 65664 bytes stalls. Charging the cube at the feature granule the part
  holds (`r76_dw_cf()`) plans those at `F`=1024, exact. The boundary is fitted at k3 and
  `F`=2048 only.
- **The packed-image first conv cannot reach the rule**: its planner charges the task's whole
  cube, at most 1 KiB at `F`=256 and 512.
- **The fp16 matmul form refuses at K 4192, N 512** after eight retirements, where 4160 and 6144
  compute exact. It fails as a refusal, not silently. The rule's stop channel was not read.

[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rocket` 1.6.0, `tests/rk3576_conv_band_probe` `f:` and `d:` arms,
2026-09-27, the headline arms re-run]

##### Unpredicted stalls and the rule's scope

A stall the rule does not predict still computes. When a job retires with its surface partly
written, the entry halves the tile's width, down to 32 channels, and redoes the tile. The
width that completed is kept per `K` for the process, and `ROCKET_RK3576_MM_NARROW=0` turns
this off. Without the plan, the narrowing took 0.75-1.28 s on the first call and 31-91 ms
after it at these `K`.

**A job after a stalled one can write nothing.** With a 105 ms idle between jobs, 12 of 129 jobs
that followed a stalled job were retired unwritten. None of 71 that followed a completed job
was. The redo then computed as the rule predicts. Why the idle does not clear it is not known.

The rule was not measured on these:

- The int32 writer
- The K-split route
- Varied weight values
- The fp16 allowances `F`=256 and 512
- The depthwise boundary away from k3 at `F`=2048

Why the unit is 64 KiB, and why one fp16 program stops at 32 channels, are not isolated.

This stall is not the unnamed-completion-class population. The function
`rocket_submit_matmul` passes `job_flags = 0`, which is the correct hint for an int8-output
DPU convolution. The DPU does raise a completion on this path, and `NO_DPU_DONE` / `PPU_DONE`
describe other programs. So the job asks for the right completion and does not get it.

##### Chunk depths for the K-split

Five chunk depths were screened by redo count before being used as arms, at `M`=512 `N`=1536,
four warm reps each, with the first discarded. All five are clean, and all sit on the entry's
linear cost law, with the backstop count unmoved across the whole screen:

| Chunk depth | Time, ms | Plan |
|---:|---|---:|
| 768 | 12-14 | 2 |
| 1280 | 16.7-16.9 | 2 |
| 1792 | 22.5-23.1 | 4 |
| 2048 | 25.4-27.1 | 4 |
| 2304 | 27.9-29.7 | 4 |

At `K`=6144 the entry also computes, and the call costs 166 ms, 2.7x what the linear law
predicts. The single-task planner collapses the output tile there and plans 768 row tasks, so
the call is submit-bound rather than contraction-bound. This is the same
per-submit-cost-is-not-a-floor effect that the 32x-in-submits / 2.7x-in-wall result records,
met from the other end. That `K` is inside the entry's `K < 6176` refusal by 32, and it does
not wedge the part. The wedge that costs a reboot is the int32 K-split route, not a single call
at a large `K`.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 2026-08-11]

A three-chunk cut of `K`=8960 (6144 + 2304 + 512) therefore costs about 201 ms a call, against
~109 ms for the shipped four-chunk cut. That makes it an accuracy probe and not a candidate
default.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, rocket 1.6.0, 2026-08-11]

#### Calibration cost in the W8A8 frontend

Calibration is 58.6% of the frontend's accounted time, and with the column-outer scan 45% of it
is one loop nest. The `calibrate` bucket is the largest the RK3576 W8A8 frontend accounts for,
at 171.3 s a process against 47.5 s of `entry`. The run is Qwen2.5-1.5B `pp2048 -ub 2048`, at
772 calls of which 386 are calibration forwards. The bucket's two halves separate cleanly, and
the profiler prints the split directly. The `calscan` bucket is a **subset** of `calibrate`,
carrying the bootstrap's per-column maximum scan alone.

| half | per process | per call | control-to-control spread over 3 processes |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `calscan`, the per-column max scan | 78.9 s | 102.25 ms | **10.37%** |
| the rest, two device GEMMs a chunk plus the `N`-wide loops | 119.7 s | 119.71 ms | **0.96%** |

The device half is exactly the two extra passes and nothing else. A calibrating call spends
119.71 ms there, against 59.55 ms of `entry` for a converged call, which is 2.01x. The
arithmetic over the matmul profile's own call count agrees: 1644 device passes =
548 converged + 1096 bootstrap. The `accounted` total's 33.3% converged share is 45.0 s,
against the frontend's 47.5 s `entry`, a 5.5% per-call overhead gap. A bootstrap pass costs
what a converged pass costs, and the calibration regime is two of them.

The two spreads are the finding to carry forward. The device-and-loops half holds to 0.96%
across processes, while the scan half spreads 10.37%. So the noisy part of this route's
largest bucket is a host memory-access term, not the device. After the loop swap below, the
spread of `calibrate` itself falls 5.20% -> 0.90%: removing the term removes the variance with
it. [HW sweep, 2026-08-11]

#### The calibration scan's loop form

The scan's cost is entirely its loop form, and two bit-identical rewrites take it to 1.0% of
calibration. The function `rk76_bootstrap_pass` takes each column's maximum. Written
column-outermost, as `for n { for m { C8[m*N + n] } }`, it reads a row-major `M`×`N` int8
surface at a stride of `N` bytes. Written `m`-outermost against an `N`-wide running max, it
reads the same surface once, sequentially.

The arithmetic is identical. A column saturates exactly when its largest |code| reaches 127,
so the flag falls out of the maximum. The `llama-perplexity --chunks 4` run returns PPL
12.8615 +/- 1.17731 to every printed digit both ways. That check matters here because a wrong
colmax on this route computes a full, correctly sized, plausible surface.

The table covers three interleaved processes an arm. The governor is pinned, 7 of 7 processes
return rc=0, and the run has 0 redos and a backstop delta of 0:

| bucket, per call | column-outer | row-major | delta | 9 pairings | own control spread |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|
| `calscan` | 102.25 | 10.72 | **-89.52%** | -90.07 .. -89.02 | 10.37% |
| `calibrate` | 221.95 | 128.73 | **-42.00%** | -43.86 .. -40.41 | 5.20% |
| `entry` | 59.55 | 58.39 | -1.95% | -4.26 .. +1.38 | 2.54% |
| `quant+rot` | 78.99 | 79.04 | +0.06% | -3.95 .. +2.61 | 2.75% |
| `dequant` | 9.09 | 9.08 | -0.06% | -0.27 .. +0.16 | 0.14% |
| `valloc` | 2.22 | 2.21 | -0.10% | -0.29 .. +0.12 | 0.18% |

At the wall the loop swap is 1.42x on the forward a user meets. The per-rep `samples_ns` of
`llama-bench` separates the regimes by construction. At `NCAL`=2, the warm-up process's
forward and timed rep 1 are the calibrating ones. Rep 1 goes 140.02 -> 98.59 s (-41.43 s).
Reps 2 and 3 move -2.9% and -2.8%, which is this instrument's own process-to-process drift and
not the lever.

A single-prompt prefill is 100% calibration regime, so 1.42x is what a first prompt gets. A
converged prefill is unchanged.

The row-major form leaves one term, the scan's scalar rate, and the shipped byte form removes
it. With an `int` accumulator, the row-major form measures 1.30 ns an element on the device, a
scalar rate. The reason is that `if (v > mx[n]) mx[n] = v;` is a conditional store and does not
vectorize. Written unconditionally, the loop vectorizes. Held in a `uint8_t` instead of an
`int`, it vectorizes 16 elements at a time instead of 4. A standalone on the board
(a standalone micro, `M`=2048, best of 3) prices all four forms:

| ns/element, -O2 / -O3 | `N`=256 | `N`=1536 | `N`=8960 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| column-outer | 5.69 / 5.34 | **17.74 / 16.25** | 12.33 / 12.44 |
| row-major, conditional store, `int` | 1.76 / 1.33 | 1.73 / 1.38 | 1.74 / 1.32 |
| row-major, unconditional store, `int` | 0.41 / 0.35 | 0.41 / 0.52 | 0.43 / 0.35 |
| row-major, unconditional store, `uint8_t` (shipped) | **0.11 / 0.11** | 0.13 / 0.20 | 0.13 / 0.14 |

The byte accumulator's win is lane count, not the accumulator's cache residency, and the `N`
sweep shows it. The win is flat at 2.4-3.7x over `rowmax_uncond` at every `N`. It is largest
at `N`=256, where the `int` accumulator is 1 KB and cannot be a residency problem at all. A
residency reading needs the win to grow with `N` (35 KB at `N`=8960 is past this part's
32 KiB L1d, 1 KB at 256 is not). A "narrowing buys nothing, the win was the vector max"
reading needs 1x, and the same three cells refute both readings.
[HW sweep, standalone, -O2 and -O3, 2026-08-11]

The narrowing is exact rather than an approximation. Over an int8 surface |code| is 0-128,
which a byte holds including the `-128` rail, and 128 >= 127 saturates as before.
[HW sweep, standalone, -O2 and -O3, 2026-08-11]

The byte form ships. The flag `ROCKET_RK3576_CALSCAN` selects the form: 0 is column-outer,
1 is row-major `int` and 2 is row-major byte, the default. The table compares the byte form
against `CALSCAN=1` over three interleaved processes an arm. The governor is pinned, 7 of 7
processes return rc=0, and the run has 0 redos and a backstop delta of 0:

| bucket, per call | `int` accumulator | byte accumulator | delta | 9 pairings | own control spread |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---:|
| `calscan` | 12.41 | **1.18** | **-90.51%** | -90.50 .. -90.52 | 0.05% |
| `calibrate` | 133.36 | 120.88 | -9.36% | -11.15 .. -6.97 | 1.72% |
| `cal_nonscan` (the device half) | 120.95 | 119.70 | -1.03% | -3.09 .. +1.68 | 1.90% |
| `entry` | 60.38 | 59.43 | -1.57% | -6.21 .. +3.66 | 4.60% |
| `quant+rot` | 79.17 | 79.76 | +0.74% | -0.62 .. +3.51 | 4.09% |
| `dequant` | 9.14 | 9.09 | -0.58% | -0.69 .. -0.47 | 0.16% |
| `valloc` | 2.21 | 2.21 | +0.10% | -0.18 .. +0.35 | 0.18% |

The measured 10.5x sits inside the standalone's own 9.4-13.4x for the same two forms
(1.32-1.74 -> 0.13-0.14 ns an element), so the device reaches the emitted code's rate. The
scan is 1.0% of `calibrate`, against 45% in the column-outer form. The wall follows on the
calibrating rep alone. Rep 1 goes 91.28 -> 86.34 s (1.057x), with all nine pairings negative
and magnitudes spanning -1.80 to -8.23 against a 4.0% control spread. Reps 2-3 move -0.87%
and -0.94%.
[HW sweep, 2026-08-11]

The `llama-perplexity --chunks 4` run returns PPL 12.8615 +/- 1.17731 to every printed digit
under all three forms. The -0.58% in `dequant` is unexplained. No mechanism in this change
reaches it, it is 0.05 ms a call, and it is visible only because `dequant` resolves 0.16%.
[HW sweep, 2026-08-11]

**Read a bucket's level from the run that produced the delta, never from a stored one.** The
`calscan` bucket resolves 0.05% across three interleaved processes in one run. Its level moved
15.8% between two runs (10.72 -> 12.41) on a branch in which not one statement changed. That
branch was recompiled inside a function that had gained a second branch. The `calibrate`
(128.73 -> 133.36) and `entry` (58.39 -> 60.38) buckets moved with it. A tight within-run
spread is a property of the binary, not of the machine.

The column-outer scan's sub-mechanism is L1d set conflict, not TLB reach, and the `N` sweep
separates the two with opposite signs. At `M`=2048, a column walk over `N`=1536 spans
3.1 MB = 768 pages, which fits this part's L2 TLB. At `N`=8960 the walk spans
18.3 MB = 4480 pages, which does not. So a TLB reading predicts that `N`=1536 is the cheaper.
It is the more expensive, at 1.32x per element.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-11]

The set-conflict reading predicts that ordering. At 64-byte lines and 128 sets, stride 1536
gives `gcd(24, 128)`=8, and so 16 of 128 sets, against 32 for both 8960 and 256. At `N`=256,
set count is confounded with a span that fits L2. The 1536-vs-8960 pair has no such confound,
and it is the cell that decides. The row-major form is flat across all three `N`, as expected
of a sequential pass. [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-11]

#### Host predictors for bootstrap pass one

Bootstrap pass one cannot be replaced by a host predictor. Its error is bounded, and that
bound, not its accuracy, is the property that matters. A calibrating W8A8 call runs two whole
GEMMs where a converged one runs one, and pass one exists only to place pass two's scale. Pass
one reads back at `cbound` = 127/(128·Σ|qB|+1), the analytic accumulator bound. So the obvious
lever is to compute its answer on the host and delete the GEMM.

Two host predictors, scored against the device's own answer over 1.50M columns, are both
refuted. The columns are every column of all 193 offloaded weights of Qwen2.5-1.5B at
`M`=2048, `-ub` 2048, over both calibration forwards.

The readout instrument is `ROCKET_RK3576_CALMAP=<file>`, which writes one row per column per
calibration forward. Each row holds these fields:

- The analytic bound
- Pass one's estimate
- Pass two's refined estimate
- The saturation flag
- Both operands' second moments

| over 1.50M columns | median | p99 | max |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| analytic bound's overshoot `abound/est2` | **61.8x** | 1075x | 1.08e4x |
| absolute error of `est1` (pass one, a device GEMM) | 11.4% | 747% | 8367% |
| absolute error of `z·sqrt(Kc)·rms(qA)·rms(qB[n])`, one global `z` | 23.5% | 142% | 794% |
| absolute error of `abound/D`, one global `D` | 25.5% | 1531% | 1.62e4x |

Read as absolute error, the second-moment predictor looks close, 23.5% against pass one's
11.4%, and better than pass one in the tail. **That statistic is the wrong one, because the
consumer's tolerance is asymmetric.** Pass two's scale is `sc2` = 127/(est·`BOOTMARGIN`). An
estimate that sits above the truth costs pass two readback resolution and nothing else. An
estimate that sits more than `BOOTMARGIN`=1.5 below the truth saturates pass two. The
pipeline then keeps `est·1.5`, an underestimate of the column maximum, as the answer.

Scored that way, the three separate completely:

| replacement for pass one | columns saturating pass two | worst underestimate | pass two's readback, p50 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| **pass one as shipped** | **0.000%** | **1.488x** | 84 codes |
| `z·G0`, `z` at the log-mean | 12.35% | 342.6x | 84 |
| `z·G0`, `z` set to clear 99.9% of columns | 0.043% | 60.6x | 14.9 |
| `z·G0`, `z` set to clear every column | 0.000% | 1x | **0.245** |
| `abound/D`, `D` at the log-mean | 19.72% | 28.9x | 90.3 |
| `abound/D`, `D` set to clear every column | 0.000% | 1x | 3.12 |

Pass one never sits more than 1.488x below the truth, and `BOOTMARGIN`=1.5 is exactly the
constant that covers it. The margin is structural rather than fitted. Pass one reads back a
real product at a scale that is a proven upper bound. Its readback maximum is a small integer
(median 2 codes, p90 3, p99 5, and 21.5% of columns at the one-code floor). Its whole error is
that integer's rounding.

A host predictor has no such floor, so making it safe on the worst column costs every other
column its resolution. The max-safe second-moment fit puts 99.98% of columns under 8 readback
codes, against 0.83% with pass one as shipped. **A cheap estimator can be better on average
and unbounded in the direction that hurts. Such an estimator is worse than an expensive one
whose error is bounded by construction.**

The same readout answers two further questions:

- **A chunk cannot inherit its neighbor's estimate.** Across the four chunks of one split
  `ffn_down`, the median column's `max_c/min_c` colmax ratio is 1.79x, p10 1.30, p90 11.0,
  p99 66.3. Measurement confirms the code's own argument, that each chunk's accumulator is
  over its own contraction.
- **The second calibration forward raises the stored colmax on 49.9% of columns.** It raises
  2.03% by more than 1.5x and 0.061% by more than 3.0x, which is past the `CALSAFE`=3.0
  headroom. That exposure is 87x the row subsample's, and it is a column count. The columns
  that lose coverage clip only their top accumulator rows, and the perplexity arm below finds
  the loss unresolvable. Read this readout as the bound on what `NCAL`=1 risks, not as its
  cost.

[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-11. What it cannot see: perplexity, another model, another
`M`, and decode.]

#### A row subsample for pass one

Pass one does not necessarily need all `M` rows, and the cost cap predicted for a row
subsample is low by 1.37x. Pass one's estimate is a maximum over `M` accumulator rows. A
subsample keeps the property the host predictors lack. It is still a readback of a real
product at a proven upper bound, so it is still floored by the same quantum. It undershoots
only by the max-of-`M'` against max-of-`M` gap. The flag `ROCKET_RK3576_CALROWS` caps pass
one's rows, and pass two always runs at full `M`.

The activations are deterministic, so the full-`M` run's stored colmax is the reference the
subsample is trying to reproduce. **Score the subsample on the stored colmax, over all
columns.** A subsample makes pass two saturate exactly on the columns where pass one came up
short. Scoring only the non-saturating columns drops the evidence.

| against the full-`M` run, 750080 columns | `M'`=512 | `M'`=128 |
|---|---:|---:|
| pass two saturates, of (column, forward) pairs | 8.04% | 19.82% |
| median stored colmax, arm/reference | 0.9911 | 0.9792 |
| columns under by more than 1.5x | 0.264% | 1.384% |
| columns under by more than `CALSAFE`=3.0 | **0.0007%** (5) | **0.0060%** (45) |
| worst underestimate | 6.094x | 6.094x |
| `calibrate` bucket | **-28.2%** | **-36.3%** |

At full `M` the saturation rate is 0.260%. The median column loses 0.9-2.1% of its colmax,
which `CALSAFE`=3.0 absorbs entirely. What it does not absorb is the worst column, at 6.094x
under. **Those columns are not what costs, and the table above scores the wrong surface.** See
the next section.

The cost cap is 26.5% and the measurement is 36.3%, so the cap is low by 1.37x, for a named
reason. The cap counted `alloc` and `stamp` as `M`-independent, because the profile separates
them from the row-scaling terms. Both are `M`-dependent, because the submit count is
`M`-dependent (31360 -> 16660 at `M'`=128), and `stamp` alone moves -48.9%.

[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-11. One
process a cell with the `CALMAP` instrument on. The buckets exclude the dump but not its cache
effects, so read these as a bracket, not a price. What it cannot see: perplexity, decode,
another model.]

#### Row-subsample cost and `BOOTMARGIN`

A row subsample costs the calibration forwards' own output, and `BOOTMARGIN` buys it back. The
frozen scale is not the consumer that a subsample hurts. Measured by perplexity, cutting pass
one's rows puts all of the cost on windows 1 and 2, the `NCAL`=2 calibration forwards. Windows
3-8, the only ones the frozen scale governs, stay inside +/-0.042 nats, with both signs at
every row count tried.

The mechanism is that a calibrating call hands back pass two's surface, at
`sc2` = 127/(`est1`·`BOOTMARGIN`). A subsampled `est1` is low, so `sc2` is high and pass two
clips. It clips 8.04% of columns at `M'`=`M`/4 and 19.82% at `M`/16, against 0.26% as
shipped. The code's own comment, that this surface "costs resolution and nothing else", holds
only while the surface does not saturate. Raising `BOOTMARGIN` with the row cut restores it,
and the raise is free at full rows:

| Qwen2.5-1.5B, `--chunks 8`, `AROW`=1, K-split on | `BOOTMARGIN` 1.5 | 2.25 | 3.0 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| full rows | **9.9348** | 9.8884 | n/a |
| `CALROWS`=128 (`M`/4) | 10.0746 (+1.41%) | **9.8804** | n/a |
| `CALROWS`=32 (`M`/16) | 11.8374 (+19.15%) | n/a | 10.0345 (+1.00%) |

Paired per window against the 9.9348 control, `CALROWS`=128 at 2.25 is -0.0055 +/- 0.0069
(se). The `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 arm alone is -0.0047 +/- 0.0076. Neither is resolvable, so do not
read either as an improvement.

What is resolved is the repair. Windows 1 and 2 fall from +0.126/+0.065 to +0.030/+0.013 nats
at `M`/4, and from +0.519/+0.900 to +0.060/+0.058 at `M`/16. **Both of those paired figures
are 8-window numbers, and both are window-set artifacts.** The subsection below measures them
at depth.

##### The row cut and the margin at depth

At depth the row cut cannot reach a converged window at all, and the 8-window figures above
are an artifact. A 2×2 over the two dials at 62 converged windows settles the question the
table above could only pose. The shipped pair, `CALROWS`=128 + `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25, also ran
on two further models, and it costs no model of three anything at depth:

| Model | Windows | Contrast | Distance from zero | Sign count | Sign test p |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Llama-3.2-1B | 3-64 | +0.0009 +/- 0.0020 se | 0.45 se | 28 of 62 worse | 0.53 |
| SmolLM2-1.7B | 3-29 | -0.0092 +/- 0.0035 se, 0.91% better | 2.59 se | 21 of 27 better | 0.0059 |
| Qwen2.5-1.5B | 3-29 | -0.0038 +/- 0.0034 se | 1.11 se | 9 of 27 worse | 0.1221 |

Qwen2.5-1.5B's headline figure moves 8.5745 -> 8.5569 = -0.205%.

Compare the three at a matched depth, which is the only comparison a level admits. At d=29
they read +0.0026 +/- 0.0030 (Llama), -0.0092 +/- 0.0035 (SmolLM2) and -0.0038 +/- 0.0034
(Qwen). They are heterogeneous (Q = 6.665 on 2 df), so quote them separately. No pooled
number is defensible, unlike `NCAL`=1's. For an unmeasured model, the number to carry is the
random-effects mean -0.0028 with tau = 0.0050. Over the first two models alone tau is 0.0077,
and the third model lands between them.

Heterogeneity here is a property of the first two models rather than a measurement of the
third. Q is 6.55 at its arithmetic minimum, with the third model exactly at the pooled mean,
and 7.3-10.6 anywhere else.

The cut alone, at the shipped 1.5 margin, is null on the converged windows too: -0.0011 +/-
0.0022, 23+/39-, sign test p = 0.0559. The frozen-scale clipping route that the 8.04%
pass-two saturation figure suggests therefore does not exist at a measurable size. The +1.41%
in the table is an aggregate over 8 windows rather than a converged cost.

The phase split carries the whole effect. The cut alone puts +0.0908 and +0.1297 nats on the
two calibration windows, against -0.0011 on the 62 converged ones. The raised margin repairs
window 1 by 11x (+0.0908 -> +0.0083) and window 2 by 2.5x. The margin alone is +0.0002 +/-
0.0024 converged and +0.0226 on window 2. Both dials are calibrating-window effects, and
`BOOTMARGIN` does exactly the job it exists for.

**On this route eight windows is a misleading instrument, not only a weak one.** All five
contrasts move 0.005-0.011 nats between d=8 and their deepest reading, and two flip sign:

| Contrast | Model | d=8 | Deepest reading |
|---|---|---|---|
| Shipped pair | Llama-3.2-1B | -0.0065 | +0.0009 at d=64 |
| Margin alone | Llama-3.2-1B | -0.0060 | +0.0002 at d=64 |
| Cut alone | Llama-3.2-1B | -0.0050 | -0.0011 at d=64 |
| Shipped pair | SmolLM2-1.7B | +0.0017 | -0.0092 at d=29 |
| Shipped pair | Qwen2.5-1.5B | -0.0144 +/- 0.0046 | -0.0038 at d=29 |

The d=8 -> d=29 step is ~0.010 nats on three of three models: +0.0091 (Llama), -0.0109
(SmolLM2) and +0.0106 (Qwen). The three agree to within 10% in magnitude and not at all in
sign. A magnitude that reproducible with an unpredictable sign is what an artifact of the
first six converged windows looks like. Qwen's recorded -0.0055 +/- 0.0069 sits 1.07 se from
its own d=8 re-read. It is therefore consistent, and no cross-build discrepancy lies behind the
difference.

The calibration phase's damage splits differently per model, and only its sum is stable. The
third model, Qwen, inverts the ordering of the two windows:

| Model | Window 1 | Window 2 | Sum |
|---|---|---|---|
| Llama-3.2-1B | +0.0083 | +0.0528 | 0.0611 |
| SmolLM2-1.7B | +0.0241 | +0.0521 | 0.0762 |
| Qwen2.5-1.5B | +0.0296 | +0.0126 | 0.0422 |

Either window alone ranges 3.6-4.2x across the three, while their sum ranges 1.8x. Gate the
calibration phase on the sum. A threshold placed on window 2 from the first two models' 1.3%
agreement fails on the third, while the dial has plainly taken effect.

[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-21. The sweep is eight cells,
with one `.so` and one corpus md5-gated throughout and the model md5-gated on the last two.
The ubatch is `-ub` 512, so `CALROWS`=128 is exactly `M`/4, under governor `ondemand`. What it
cannot see: the two dials separately on any model but Llama, because the 2×2 ran there only.
The other two models' readings are therefore about the shipped pair and cannot attribute to
one dial. It also cannot see the `BOOTMARGIN`×`CALROWS` interior, another `-ub`, or decode.]

Two points about instruments follow. The aggregate perplexity and the paired test disagree
(+19.15% against 1.43 se) because 2 windows of 8 carry all of it. Score the phase, not the
run. The shipped saturation report is **silent through every cell**, because the calibrating
branch passes `NULL` for `sat_elems`. A monitor scoped to the converged phase cannot see a
defect confined to calibration.

The lever is therefore `CALROWS`=`M`/4 **with** `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25: two dials, never one.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-12. A same-build control ran at both ends of the first arm and is identical to every
printed digit. What it cannot see: one model, 8 windows of one text, one `-ub`, and decode.
The whole term lives in the calibration forwards, so a converged prefill never pays it.]

**The two tables above are `M`=512 cells, and the clipping figures beside them are `M`=2048
cells.** The accuracy costs come from `llama-perplexity -ub 512`, where `M`/4 is
`CALROWS`=128. The 0.26 / 8.04 / 19.82% clip fractions come from
`llama-bench -p 2048 -ub 2048`, where `M`/4 is `CALROWS`=512. Both are written as a fraction
of `M`, so four numbers from two experiments read as one experiment.

##### `CALROWS` across ubatch sizes

`CALROWS` is an absolute row count while its damage follows a ratio, so one setting is a
different cut at every ubatch. The dial never scales: `ggml-rocket.cpp` clamps it
(`if (rk76_calrows() > 0 && rk76_calrows() < M) M1 = rk76_calrows();`), so `CALROWS`=128 is
`M`/4 at `-ub` 512 and `M`/16 at `-ub` 2048. Five `llama-perplexity --chunks 8` cells ran at
`-c/-b/-ub` 2048 on Qwen2.5-1.5B. They are scored on `Dcal`, the nats added over the two
`NCAL`=2 calibrating windows. The score is depth-independent, and those windows are where all
of this dial's effect lives:

| `CALROWS` | ratio at `M`=2048 | `Dcal` | 8-window | converged 3-8 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 512 | `M`/4 | +0.1276 | +1.27% | -0.0045 +/- 0.0046 |
| 128 | `M`/16 | +0.6761 | +8.73% | -0.0010 +/- 0.0036 |
| 32 | `M`/64 | +1.9985 | +28.56% | +0.0019 +/- 0.0013 |
| 128 + `BOOTMARGIN` 2.25 | `M`/16 | +0.1052 | +0.98% | -0.0045 +/- 0.0040 |

The ratio governs, and the absolute row count does not. The `M`=512 anchors are
`CALROWS`=128 -> 0.1906 and `CALROWS`=32 -> 1.4186. Against them, matched-ratio pairs agree to
1.49x at `M`/4 and 2.10x at `M`/16, while the matched-absolute pair at `M1`=128 disagrees by
3.55x.

The ratio is not exact, and the residual has one sign. At a matched ratio a larger `M` is
more forgiving, and the correction grows with the depth of the cut. That is the signature of a
logarithmically-shaped running maximum rather than a power-law one. Pass one's `est1` is a
maximum over the first `M1` rows, so the undershoot is `f(M1)/f(M)`, one non-decreasing curve
divided by itself. That ratio is a pure function of `M1/M` only where `f` is a power law.
Re-expressing the dial as a fraction is therefore the right shape, and it still owes its
accuracy a measurement at each ubatch.

At `-ub` 2048, `CALROWS`=128 + `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 costs +0.98% over 8 windows and reads
-0.0045 +/- 0.0040 on the converged ones. The margin repairs 6.4x of the cut's own +8.73%, the
same mechanism measured at 11x on window 1 at `-ub` 512. The same pair reads -0.55% at
`-ub` 512. **The pair is not free at the larger ubatch, although it is better-than-free at
the smaller**, and the whole swing is in the calibrating windows.

##### `CALROWS` expressed as a fraction

Re-expressed as a fraction, the dial costs a quarter of what the absolute setting costs and
keeps 78% of the saving. At `-ub` 2048, `CALROWS`=512 is `M`/4. With `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 beside
it, the setting costs `Dcal` +0.0266, +0.301% over 8 windows, and -0.00043 +/- 0.00437 over
the converged windows 3-8. Against the absolute `CALROWS`=128 at the same ubatch (`Dcal`
+0.1052), that is 0.25x the damage. Against the bare `M`/4 cut (+0.1276), the margin repairs
4.80x.

On price, the recorded `llama-bench -ub 2048` figures are 28.2% of `calibrate` for
`CALROWS`=512 against 36.3% for 128. The fraction therefore keeps 77.7% of the saving for a
quarter of the cost. Express the dial as `M`/4 and not as a row count. At `-ub` 2048 that
setting is 512.

The margin's repair factor varies with the depth of the cut: 6.43x at `M`/16 against 4.80x at
`M`/4. A point estimate transported along that axis, the `M`/16 repair fraction applied to the
`M`/4 cut, predicted +0.0199 and missed by 1.33x. That miss is in the same direction as the
2.1x miss the ubatch transport made.

##### The clip fraction as a damage predictor

The clip fraction predicts the damage along the cut axis and not across the margin axis. The
margin `BOOTMARGIN` reaches the damage twice (`ggml-rocket.cpp:2288-2295`). First, it sets pass
two's scale `sc2 = 127/(est1*BOOTMARGIN)`, so a larger margin saturates fewer columns. Second,
it sets the recorded ceiling `est1*BOOTMARGIN` on a column that still saturates. Only the first
is visible in the clip fraction, and **the clip fraction is not a sufficient statistic for the
accuracy damage.** Raising the margin 1.5 -> 2.25 at `M`=2048:

| cut | regime | clip @1.5 | clip @2.25 | fall | damage removed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `M`/4 | no `AROW` | 8.0367% | 0.2886% | 27.9x | 79% |
| `M`/16 | no `AROW` | 19.8197% | 1.7809% | 11.1x | 84% |
| `M`/4 | `AROW`=1 | 4.3461% | **0.0836%** | **52.0x** | 79% |

The margin removes far more clipping than damage at every point. The fit
`Dcal = A * clip^1.855` on the five margin-1.5 points, evaluated at the margin-2.25 clips,
under-predicts the measured `Dcal` by 100.5x (`M`/4) and 13.6x (`M`/16). Refitting `A` through
the `AROW`=1 (`M`/4, 1.5) point puts clip and `Dcal` in the same activation regime, and makes
the miss worse, 318x. Something the saturation flag cannot see therefore carries the residual
damage at a raised margin. **A 3-minute `llama-bench` CALMAP readout cannot price this lever's
accuracy.** The lever is always two dials.

`AROW` itself nearly halves the clip fraction (8.0367 -> 4.3461% at margin 1.5, 1.85x), so a
clip fraction is a statement about its activation regime. The grid runs without `AROW`, and
every accuracy cell runs with it.

##### Resolution of the returned surface

The returned surface's resolution is a real third channel, and it carries only a fifth of the
residual. A calibrating call hands back pass two dequantized at `sc2 = 127/(est1*BOOTMARGIN)`
(`ggml-rocket.cpp:2315`), so the returned int8 quantum is `est1*BOOTMARGIN/127`. Here `est1`
is pass one's per-column max over `M1` rows at scale `cbound`. The scale `cbound` is set once
per weight from `sumabs` alone (line 2194) and never sees the margin.

The quantum therefore factorizes, and the two dials move it in opposite directions. The margin
coarsens it on every column, while a row cut shrinks `est1` and pushes it back. No clip
fraction can see this.

The resolution index `R/R_ctl = (margin/1.5) * est1(cut)/est1(full)` is therefore readable
from CALMAP bytes with no run. The uncontaminated set is blk.0's `attn_q`/`attn_k`/`attn_v`,
whose input is the embedding through an RMSNorm with no matmul upstream. On that set at
`M`=2048, `est1(512)/est1(full)` is 0.89714 and `est1(128)/est1(full)` is 0.77888:

| cell | cut | margin | `R/R_ctl` | clip | `Dcal` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `q_ctl` | none | 1.5 | 1.00000 | 0.2600% | 0 by definition |
| `q_r512` | `M`/4 | 1.5 | 0.89714 | 8.0367% | +0.1276 |
| `q_shipfrac` | `M`/4 | 2.25 | 1.34570 | 0.2886% | +0.0266 |
| `q_ship` | `M`/16 | 2.25 | 1.16832 | 1.7809% | +0.1052 |
| `q_g2p25` | none | 2.25 | **1.50000** | **0.0000%** | **+0.0081** |

The margin-only cell clips 0 of 1500160 columns, so it isolates resolution exactly: a 1.5x
coarsening of the returned surface costs +0.0081 nats. Interpolated to `q_shipfrac`'s 1.34570,
that is +0.0053-0.0056 under either a linear or a quadratic-noise form. That is 20-21% of its
+0.0266, and its clipping is at the control's own level. The remaining +0.021 nats, 79-80% of
the residual, is therefore the clip channel and not the returned quantum. It lies on a curve
that no linear statistic of the clipping reproduces.

The clip fractions in the table above are `AROW`=0 readouts, and the `Dcal` values are `AROW`=1
runs. That is why the in-regime numbers in the next subsection differ.

Damage is not monotone in resolution. The cell `q_ship` has a strictly finer surface than
`q_shipfrac` (1.16832 against 1.34570) and 4x the damage. The cell `q_g2p25` is the coarsest
cell buildable at margin 2.25, and it is 3.3x less damaging than `q_shipfrac`. An ordering
argument over the quantum alone predicts both backwards.

##### Separability of the calibrating-window damage

The calibrating-window damage is a separable product of the cut and the margin. Read in the
`AROW`=1 regime every `Dcal` was measured in, and with the resolution channel subtracted as
`D_res = 0.0162*(R/R_ctl - 1)`, the cells' clip damage is:

| cell | cut | margin | clip | mass `E` | `r` | `D_res` | `D_clip` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `q_ctl` | none | 1.5 | 0.0303% | 1.40e-05 | 1.00000 | 0 | 0 by definition |
| `q_g2p25` | none | 2.25 | 0.0000% | 0 | 1.00000 | +0.0081 | 0 |
| `q_r1024` | `M`/2 | 1.5 | 1.3775% | 1.27e-03 | 0.94636 | -0.0009 | **-0.0062** |
| `q_r1024g2p25` | `M`/2 | 2.25 | 0.0177% | 2.58e-05 | 0.94636 | +0.0068 | **-0.0174** |
| `q_shipfrac` | `M`/4 | 2.25 | 0.0836% | 1.24e-04 | 0.87151 | +0.0050 | +0.0216 |
| `q_g1p875r512` | `M`/4 | 1.875 | 0.4963% | 5.99e-04 | 0.87151 | +0.0014 | +0.0473 |
| `q_ship` | `M`/16 | 2.25 | 0.9604% | 1.39e-03 | 0.74417 | +0.0019 | +0.1033 |
| `q_r512` | `M`/4 | 1.5 | 4.3461% | 4.53e-03 | 0.87151 | -0.0021 | +0.1297 |
| `q_r128` | `M`/16 | 1.5 | 17.1747% | 2.41e-02 | 0.74417 | -0.0041 | +0.6803 |
| `q_r32` | `M`/64 | 1.5 | 38.3480% | 8.44e-02 | 0.62576 | -0.0061 | +2.0046 |

The product `D_clip = g(cut)*h(margin)` fits the six cut cells at `M`/4 and deeper to a worst
residual of 2.31% (rms 1.86%). Three `g` values and three `h` values, less one scale
degeneracy, are five parameters through six points. The fit therefore has one residual degree
of freedom. The cut factor `g` is 0.1327 at `M`/4, 0.6649 at `M`/16 and 2.0046 at `M`/64. The
margin factor `h` is 1 at margin 1.5, 0.3561 at 1.875 and 0.1590 at 2.25. Held out and then
measured, the third margin lands within 2.1% of the model's power-law-in-margin extrapolation
(predicted `Dcal` +0.0497, measured +0.0487).

The cut factor `g` is not a power law in the cut: it rises 5.01x, then 3.01x, per 4x
reduction. Two smooth two-parameter forms fit those three points. The form `(1-r)^2.52` in
the cut's own undershoot fits to 9.3%, and a log-linear form in the cut fraction fits to 15.6%.
Both are refuted at `M`/2 by sign (see below).

##### The margin factor `h`

The margin factor `h` is a power law in the margin, it extrapolates, and its exponent tracks
the K set. The factor was fitted only over margins [1.5, 2.25]. The margin 1.875 is an
interpolation between those anchors, so it separates no extrapolation form, and its own verdict
flips on the refuted `D_res` index. A margin of 1.0 is the library's floor, because
`bootmargin < 1.0f` is reset to 1.5. Two cells at cut `M`/4 and margins 1.25 and 1.00 settle
the extrapolation [HW sweep, Qwen2.5-1.5B, `-ub` 2048, `--chunks 8`]:

| cut | margin | clip | `Dcal` | PPL[8] vs control | implied `h` | power law | log-linear |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `M`/4 | 1.00 | 77.171% | **+0.92760** | +12.347% | **7.3094** | 6.9407 | 3.6386 |
| `M`/4 | 1.25 | 25.672% | **+0.32464** | +4.056% | **2.5645** | 2.3897 | 1.9075 |

The power law `h(m) = (m/1.5)^-4.778`, fitted entirely inside [1.5, 2.25], predicts a 1.5x
extrapolation to the floor within 5.3% and 7.3%. Both land high, so the true `h` is slightly
steeper still. A log-linear form in the margin misses by 2.02x and 1.35x and is refuted. The
readout free of `g` and of `D_res`, `Dcal`(1.0)/`Dcal`(1.25), is 2.857 against the power law's
2.912.

The exponent is not shared across architectures. SmolLM2-1.7B at the same cut and margin 1.0
reads `Dcal` = +0.97094 (PPL[8] 8.3207 against its own control 7.4265, +12.041%). Over the same
[1.0, 2.25] range its margin ratio is 30.60 against Qwen's 50.57, and its exponent is 4.22
against 4.84, 1.65x shallower. This bites anyone quoting one number. At margin 2.25 SmolLM2's
`M`/4 damage is 1.99x Qwen's, and at margin 1.00 the two models are 4.7% apart. A cross-model
margin figure taken at one end of the axis says nothing about the other.

The exponent is shared between two models that share a K set. In the table below, 4.230 and
4.219 agree to 0.26% across a tokenizer, a corpus, 8 blocks of depth and GQA-against-MHA. A
same-family model at a different K set reads 3.223. The exponent is therefore a property of
the K set (or of what co-varies with it), not of the architecture and not of the model.

The clip fraction at these margins is predicted, not only recorded. Before the cells ran, the
off-line readout over a CALMAP's own `est2/est1` distribution gave 78.2% and 26.0%. The cells
measured 77.171% and 25.672%, within 1.3%. Those clip fractions are 9x larger than 8.7%, the
largest the readout had been checked at.

**The truncation mechanism cannot predict the damage.** Taken literally, it puts
`D(1.0)/D(1.5)` at 21.9-54.2 against a measured 7.31, and at 3.37-3.82 when rescaled by its own
validation miss. Its direction is right, and no magnitude from it is usable.

##### The margin index across architectures and K sets

A third model separates the two channels, and a fourth says the margin index tracks the K set,
not the architecture. Two models give each channel two points moving opposite ways. That is
compatible both with one per-model scalar setting both channels and with two independent
channels. Llama-3.2-1B-f16 has the llama architecture and SmolLM2's exact `n_embd` 2048 /
`n_ff` 8192, at 16 blocks against 24. It runs its own control plus three cells and separates
the two explanations [HW sweep, `-ub` 2048, `--chunks 8`, `rocket` 1.6.0, backstop delta 0]:

| model | arch | blocks | `n_embd`/`n_ff` | `head_count_kv` | ctl PPL[8] | `D_res` | R over [1.00, 2.25] | exponent |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Qwen2.5-1.5B | qwen2 | 28 | 1536/8960 | 2 | 7.2534 | +0.00811 | 50.57 | 4.838 |
| SmolLM2-1.7B | llama | 24 | 2048/8192 | 32 | 7.4265 | +0.02060 | 30.60 | 4.219 |
| Llama-3.2-1B | llama | 16 | 2048/8192 | 8 | 11.4024 | **+0.01626** | **30.87** | **4.230** |
| SmolLM2-360M | llama | 32 | **960/2560** | 5 | 9.5608 | **+0.01484** | **13.65** | **3.223** |

The margin index is identical on the two llama models that share a K set. It reads 30.87
against 30.60, with exponents 4.230 against 4.219. These agree to 0.9% and 0.26% across an
8-block depth difference and across GQA-against-MHA, while Qwen sits 1.64x away. R is a ratio
at one cut, so both models' `g` and both models' mixed-`M` populations cancel out of it.

The agreement belongs to the K set, not to the architecture, and the fourth model is what says
so. SmolLM2-360M differs from SmolLM2-1.7B in the K set, and shares with it:

- The architecture
- The `tokenizer.ggml.pre` smollm over an identical 49152 vocabulary, so the same corpus
  segments into the same windows (true of no other pair here)
- The training family
- The base-checkpoint status

Its R is 13.65, 0.446x the anchor's. The two pairs are the finding, and neither needs a fit:

| pair | what moves | R ratio |
|---|---|---|
| SmolLM2-1.7B vs Llama-3.2-1B | tokenizer, vocabulary, corpus, depth 24 vs 16, `head_kv` 32 vs 8, base vs instruct | **1.009x** |
| SmolLM2-1.7B vs SmolLM2-360M | the K set, 2048/8192 vs 960/2560 (depth 24 vs 32, `head_kv` 32 vs 5) | **0.446x** |

Depth and head structure move in both pairs, so both are refuted again, now by a pair that
moves them the other way. The first pair refutes the tokenizer, the vocabulary, the corpus and
the tuning status. The width `n_embd` alone is refuted outright, because R is non-monotone in
it (960 -> 13.65, 1536 -> 50.57, 2048 -> 30.7). Both `n_ff` and `n_ff`/`n_embd` are monotone
over these four points. A fifth model separates them and refutes both, below.

The pair does not measure the K set alone. SmolLM2-360M K-splits none of its GEMMs, because
960 and 2560 are both `rk76_k_single_ok`. All three anchors split exactly one of the seven per
block (`ffn_down`, K >= 6176). Model scale co-varies as well. The move is therefore "the K set
or the split population or scale", jointly.

The flag `ROCKET_RK3576_FORCESPLIT=1` separates the split. A fifth model separates the other
two: SmolLM2-135M, at the same `n_ff`/`n_embd` ratio and 0.600x the scale. It splits nothing
either, so the split population is held fixed there, and it refutes the ratio, `n_ff` alone
and scale together, below.

[HW sweep, `-ub` 2048, `--chunks 8`, `rocket` 1.6.0, `.so` `ea40bc1b`, backstop delta 0,
2026-08-22. Scored under a gate that refuses unless nine pre-existing `Dcal` values
reproduce to 5e-4 and all three anchors' published R to 0.02.]

The resolution channel is the model-specific one, and it moves where the margin index does
not. Llama-3.2-1B's `D_res` is 0.789x SmolLM2's and 2.004x Qwen's. It moves with depth, but
nowhere near proportionally: 0.789x for a 0.667x block ratio. The reading sits 1.5% from the
boundary between "tracks the architecture" and "scales with depth", so carry the two ratios and
not a label.

The fourth model dissociates the two channels a third time, now on the K axis specifically.
SmolLM2-360M's `D_res` is 0.720x its same-family anchor's, while its R is 0.446x. A `D_res`
tracking `n_embd` through the first three points would have read +0.00215, a 6.9x miss. The
resolution channel therefore does not follow the K set either. It stays per-model over four
points spanning 2.54x (+0.00811 to +0.02060).

One per-model scalar setting both channels is therefore not supported. Channel A moves 0.789x
while channel B moves 1.009x. An anti-correlation law fitted on the first two models (log-log
slope -0.539) demands R = 34.76, and the cell measures 30.87. That is an 11.2% residual,
against 0.9% for "the index is simply the same on both". These same three points also refute
baseline sharpness as a driver. Against PPL[8], `D_res` is non-monotone: Qwen reads 7.2534 and
0.00811, SmolLM2 7.4265 and 0.02060, and Llama 11.4024 and 0.01626.

**Two properties of Llama-3.2-1B bite anyone transporting a number to it.** Its
`head_count_kv` is 8 against SmolLM2's 32. Two of the seven projections a block offloads
therefore carry `N`=512 rather than 2048, and the pair is not a pure depth contrast. Also,
24.35% of its calibrated columns sit at `M`=1024 (309760 of 1272320), against SmolLM2's 8.57%
and Qwen's 19.0%. On a quarter of them `CALROWS`=512 is an `M`/2 cut, and the "cut `M`/4"
label covers only 75.65%.

Both properties cancel out of R, and neither cancels out of a level. **Dcal(cut 512, 1.00) =
+0.90033 is therefore not comparable across models**, even though Qwen's +0.92760 and
SmolLM2's +0.97094 sit beside it.

The clip-fraction readout transports to a third model and holds. It measures 75.040% at cut
512 / margin 1.00, against 76.7% predicted from the other two models' 77.171% and 76.14%. The
margin-2.25 no-cut cell clips 0 of 1272320 columns, exactly as on both others. That is what
makes `D_res` a measurement rather than a response minus a model.

##### The denominator of the margin index

The margin index is the reciprocal of a small difference, and a fifth model puts that
difference at zero. The index `R` is `D_clip(cut M/4, 1.00) / D_clip(cut M/4, 2.25)`, where
`D_clip(m)` is `Dcal(cut, m)` minus the no-cut `D_res(m)`. **That denominator is a difference
of two same-signed quantities of order 0.02 nats, and nothing constrains it to stay
positive.**

SmolLM2-135M is a llama at 576/1536 with 30 blocks and `head_kv` 3. It uses smollm over the
same 49152 vocabulary and is the HuggingFaceTB base checkpoint. Its `n_ff`/`n_embd` is
2.666667, exactly SmolLM2-360M's, at 0.600x its width. It puts the denominator at -0.00338
[HW sweep, `-ub` 2048, `--chunks 8`, `rocket` 1.6.0, `.so` `ea40bc1b`, backstop delta 0,
2026-08-22]:

| model | ratio | `D_res`(2.25) | `Dcal`(cut, 1.00) | `Dcal`(cut, 2.25) | `D_clip`(2.25) | `R` | raw ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Qwen2.5-1.5B | 5.8333 | +0.00811 | +0.92760 | +0.02656 | +0.01845 | 50.57 | 34.92 |
| SmolLM2-1.7B | 4.0000 | +0.02060 | +0.97094 | +0.05278 | +0.03218 | 30.60 | 18.40 |
| Llama-3.2-1B | 4.0000 | +0.01626 | +0.90033 | +0.04577 | +0.02951 | 30.87 | 19.67 |
| SmolLM2-360M | 2.6667 | +0.01484 | +0.69288 | +0.06634 | +0.05150 | 13.65 | 10.44 |
| SmolLM2-135M | **2.6667** | **+0.02332** | **+0.51870** | **+0.01994** | **-0.00338** | **-158.11** | **26.02** |

**Read the denominator column, not the `R` column.** The four anchors' 3.7055x spread in `R`
decomposes exactly into a 1.3276x numerator move and a 2.7911x denominator move. So 78% of the
log spread is the reciprocal of that small difference. The ladder 50.57 / 30.87 / 30.60 / 13.65
is mostly denominator. Fit `Dcal` directly on (cut, margin). Do not derive `D_clip`, and
do not quote `R` for a model whose denominator has not been read.

On the fifth model, the clip term at the shipped margin is consistent with zero, not negative.
Its two calibrating windows disagree in sign, -0.00709 and +0.00371, where SmolLM2-360M's agree
(+0.00155, +0.04995). The net is 38x the reconstruction precision, so the arithmetic is exact.
The quantity itself is unresolved, and an index dividing by it is undefined rather than large.

The mechanism is the two channels netting out. Margin 2.25 coarsens the returned quantum on
every column, while a row cut shrinks `est1` and pushes it back the other way. On a model whose
resolution cost is high and whose clipping is cheap, the cut is therefore a partial antidote to
the margin.

The margin channel itself is intact on this model. At the same cut, margin 1.00 costs +0.51870
against margin 2.25's +0.01994, a 26.0x ratio in the same direction as all four anchors. A
negative `R` here means the instrument failed, not that the channel reversed.

The ratio `n_ff`/`n_embd` is refuted as what sets the index, on the one statistic that survives
a zero denominator. The raw `Dcal(cut, 1.00)/Dcal(cut, 2.25)` reads 26.02 against
SmolLM2-360M's 10.44 at an identical ratio, 2.49x apart. The raw statistic is also
non-monotone in `n_ff`: 34.9 at 8960, 18.4 at 8192, 19.7 at 8192, 10.4 at 2560 and 26.0
at 1536. That non-monotonicity does not carry to model scale. The raw ratio is itself 76%
denominator, and on its numerator alone scale is monotone (see the next section).

The resolution channel does not scale with width either, and the fifth model is what says so.
On the smallest model `D_res` is +0.02332, above all four anchors. That is 1.96x what an
in-family power law through the two SmolLM2 checkpoints predicted (+0.01190), and in the wrong
direction. Over five models it spans 2.875x with no monotone relation to width, depth, `n_ff`
or baseline loss. Its no-cut margin-2.25 cell clips 0 of 409344 columns, so this is a
measurement and not a response minus a model.

The clip-fraction readout holds on a fourth model: 80.859% at cut 512 / margin 1.00. The
other three read 77.171% (Qwen), 75.040% (Llama) and 77.351% (SmolLM2-360M). It is nearly
model-independent at a given (cut, margin), 1.078x over four models spanning 5.8x in `n_ff`,
and read in its own prompt regime.

[Scored under a gate that refuses unless nine pre-existing `Dcal` values reproduce to
5e-4 and all four anchors' published `R` to 0.02. The branch points were computed before the cells.]

##### The direct (cut, margin) surface

Read directly, the (cut, margin) surface shows that the subtraction is sound where clipping
dominates, and that the raw ratio inherits the index's defect. This section reads all 32
calibration cells at `-ub` 2048 as one response, `Dcal(cut, margin)`, with no `D_clip` and no
index. Read that way, they say three things the derived form hid [HW sweep, five models, `.so`
`ea40bc1b`, corpus `3ce70e93`, `rocket` 1.6.0].

The `D_res` subtraction is validated where the clip channel dominates. The division fails, not
the subtraction. Separability predicts that `D_clip(cut, 1.50)/D_clip(cut, 2.25)` does not
depend on the cut. The term `D_res(1.50)` is zero by construction, because the control is
margin 1.5 with no cut.

On Qwen2.5-1.5B the unsubtracted margin ratio at cuts 128 and 512 reads 6.427 and 4.805,
1.337x apart. Subtracting the independently measured no-cut +0.00811 makes them 6.963 and
6.918, 1.0066x apart. That is a 51x reduction bought with one constant that was never fitted to
either cell. At cut 1024 both readings are negative and 18.6x apart. A `D_clip` is therefore a
sound quantity at `M`/4 and deeper, and an unsound one near its own zero. The defect is the reciprocal, not the difference.

The sign of `D_clip(M/4, 2.25)` is which side of a minimum the cut falls on. Along margin 2.25
the two models read:

| Cut | Qwen2.5-1.5B | SmolLM2-1.7B |
|---|---|---|
| 128 | +0.10521 | n/a |
| 512 | +0.02656 | +0.05278 |
| 768 | +0.00931 | n/a |
| 1024 | -0.01065 | +0.03770 |
| 1536 | +0.00335 | +0.02405 |
| 2048 | +0.00811 | +0.02060 |

Qwen crosses its own no-cut level just above 768 and bottoms near `M`/2. SmolLM2-1.7B is
monotone over the cuts it has, so its crossing is above `M`*0.75. SmolLM2-135M's `M`/4 cell
already sits below its no-cut cell, so its crossing is below `M`/4. The crossing point is
model-dependent, and that is what flips the denominator's sign.

The raw ratio carries 76% of its spread in the denominator, the same defect as `R`'s 78%.
Decomposed at the ends of the five-model ladder (Qwen over SmolLM2-360M), the numerator moves
1.3388x and the denominator 2.4973x. Their product, 3.3433x, is the raw ratio exactly.

On the numerator alone, model scale is monotone, and it is the one architectural driver left
standing. The cell `Dcal(cut M/4, margin 1.00)` is the largest in the corpus (0.52-0.97 nats
against a reconstruction precision of 8.79e-5) and subtracts no model. It is a level, so only
the three checkpoints that share the smollm tokenizer segment the corpus identically and are
comparable:

| model | params | `n_embd` | `n_ff` | blocks | `n_ff`/`n_embd` | calibrated columns | `Dcal`(`M`/4, 1.00) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SmolLM2-135M | 0.135B | 576 | 1536 | 30 | 2.6667 | 409344 | +0.51870 |
| SmolLM2-360M | 0.360B | 960 | 2560 | 32 | 2.6667 | 651264 | +0.69288 |
| SmolLM2-1.7B | 1.710B | 2048 | 8192 | 24 | 4.0000 | 1769472 | +0.97094 |

The numerator is monotone in parameters, `n_embd`, `n_ff` and the calibrated-column count. It
is non-monotone in depth (30 / 32 / 24), which refutes depth a third time. The two checkpoints
at an identical `n_ff`/`n_embd` differ 1.336x, which refutes the ratio on the numerator as
well as on the raw statistic. **Model scale is not refuted**: the raw statistic that argues
against it is three-quarters denominator.

The clip fraction is not the driver. At that same cell it reads 80.859 / 77.351 / 77.171 /
75.040% on four models, 1.078x, while the response spans 1.872x. The calibrated-column count is
what remains collinear with scale on every model here. Separating the two needs a cell that
moves the population at a fixed checkpoint.

The margin axis is not a power law on the direct response. Qwen at cut `M`/4 over five margins
reads:

| Margin | `Dcal` | Local log-log exponent to the next margin |
|---|---|---|
| 1.00 | +0.92760 | -4.705 |
| 1.25 | +0.32464 | -5.120 |
| 1.50 | +0.12764 | -4.318 |
| 1.875 | +0.04870 | -3.325 |
| 2.25 | +0.02656 | n/a |

The local exponent is non-monotone, with a 1.54x spread, and one power law over the range
misses by 1.230x at its worst point. What survives is the direction and the end-to-end size:
34.9x between the two ends at that cut on that model.

The two channels' dissociation needs no index at all. SmolLM2-135M carries the lowest
numerator of the five (+0.51870, 0.534x the highest) and the highest `D_res` (+0.02332, 2.875x
the lowest). That one model refutes "one per-model scalar sets both channels" with no fit and
no derived quantity.

[A reader that re-runs the same nine-value reproduction gate before it reads anything.
Scope: one corpus, one ubatch, calibrating windows only. The levels are comparable within a
tokenizer and nowhere else.]

##### The calibrated-column population

The calibrated-column population is not the driver: 4.09x more columns give more clipping and a
quarter of the damage. The setting `ROCKET_RK3576_FORCESPLIT=1 ROCKET_RK3576_KCHUNK=512` cuts
every offloaded K into 512-chunks (960 -> 512+256+128+64, 2560 -> 5×512). A calibrating call
logs one column per output column per chunk. SmolLM2-360M's calibrated population therefore
moves 651264 -> 2666496, 4.0944x, at a byte-identical checkpoint, tokenizer, corpus and window
segmentation. That breaks the collinearity between model scale and column count that every
model on the board carries.

The population closed form, generalized to a per-GEMM chunk count, predicted both integers
exactly before the control ran: total 2666496, minority 443776, share 16.643%. That makes 12 of
12 integers over six model configurations, and the first at a chunk count the shipped path
never produces.

| cell | PPL[4] | `Dcal` (windows 1-2) | clipped |
|---|---|---|---|
| `q_fsctl` (this arm's control) | 11.3676 | n/a | 0 of 2666496 |
| `q_fsr512g1p0` (cut 512, margin 1.00) | 11.8050 | **+0.16382** | 2250316 (**84.392%**) |
| `q_fsg2p25` (no cut, margin 2.25) | 11.3775 | **+0.00448** | 0 |

[HW sweep, 3 cells `rc=0`, `-ub` 2048 `--chunks` 4, `.so` `ea40bc1b`, corpus `3ce70e93`,
backstop 650 -> 650, 0 dmesg WARNINGs, taint 4608, `ondemand`, 2026-08-22.]

The population is refuted as the driver, and by more than a null. Against the same
checkpoint's unsplit +0.69288, the cut-512 margin-1.00 damage under 4.09x the columns is
0.2364x, down. The clip fraction goes up 1.091x (84.392% against 77.351%). More columns, with
more of them clipping, give a quarter of the damage. A term that grows with the
calibrated-column count cannot produce that.

Both channels fall at this chunk size. The resolution term `D_res` reads +0.00448, 0.3016x the
unsplit +0.01484, against the clip channel's 0.2364x. A second chunk size separates them again
(see below).

The split's own arithmetic is not what is being read. The FORCESPLIT control reads PPL[4]
11.3676 against the unsplit control's 11.4391, 0.9937x. The chunked baseline is therefore, if
anything, slightly better on this model. That refutes the "the split swamps the dial" reading
on its own covariate.

[The K-split's recorded 1.60x perplexity cost belongs to a different configuration. It is what
turning `KSPLIT` on buys for a `K >= 6176` `ffn_down` that is otherwise declined to the CPU.
Nothing on this model reaches that bound.]

The cap was declared before the cells. The arm moves the population and the contraction depth
together, so it bounds what a population term can contribute and does not subtract it.

[Registered and pushed before the control cell. Scored under a gate that refuses unless eleven already-scored `Dcal` values reproduce to
5e-4.]

##### Clip damage at a second chunk size

The clip damage is a law in the chunk depth, not in the chunk count. At a second chunk size the
two channels come apart again. `ROCKET_RK3576_KCHUNK=256` on the same checkpoint takes the
population to 3563520 columns. That is 5.4717x the shipped 651264 and 1.3364x the 512-chunk
cell. It is a third exact forward integer from the closed form, which is exact on 14 of 14. The
three cells ran at `--chunks` 4 and `-ub` 2048, 231-264 s each, with a backstop delta of 0:

| | unsplit | `KCHUNK`=512 | `KCHUNK`=256 |
|---|---|---|---|
| calibrated columns | 651264 | 2666496 | 3563520 |
| column-weighted chunks a GEMM | 1 | 4.0943 | 5.4717 |
| deepest chunk | 2560 | 512 | 256 |
| `Dcal`(cut 512, margin 1.00) | +0.69288 | +0.16382 | **+0.07245** |
| `Dcal` as a fraction of unsplit | 1.0000 | 0.2364 | **0.1046** |
| clip fraction of that cell | 77.351% | 84.392% | **85.271%** |
| damage per clipped column, nats | 1.375e-06 | 7.280e-08 | **2.384e-08** |
| `D_res` = `Dcal`(no cut, 2.25) | +0.01484 | +0.00448 | **+0.01013** |

A reciprocal law in the chunk count is refuted. Its apparent 3.2% fit at `KCHUNK`=512 comes for
free: it is the law agreeing with its own first measurement. The local exponent on the count
over the segment between the two split cells is 2.8135, against the 1.0230 that the 512 point
alone gives. A reciprocal law gives an exponent of 1.

The maximum chunk depth is the variable that fits. Halving `KCHUNK` halves the deepest chunk
exactly, while it raises the column-weighted count only 1.3364x. The greedy walk is *ragged*, so
count and maximum depth are not reciprocal, even though count and mean depth are reciprocal by
construction. The measured fall is 0.4423x, against 0.5000x for a halved depth (1.13x) and
0.7483x for the count (1.69x). The segment exponents are 1.177 and 2.814. The residual is in the
direction of super-linearity in depth [two split points, one model].

`D_res` reverses, so the contraction depth does not name a quantity the two channels share. At
`KCHUNK`=256 it reads +0.01013, which is 2.2614x its `KCHUNK`=512 value and 0.6827x the unsplit
value. `D_res` is therefore *non-monotone* in the chunk count, with a minimum at `KCHUNK`=512,
while the clip channel falls 0.4423x over the same step. Two competing terms are visible, and
neither combination reproduces the size. Against a measured 2.2614x, `count*quantum` gives
1.277x, `count*quantum²` gives 1.220x and `count²` gives 1.786x [hypothesis, named, not
isolated].

The cross-tab by chunk depth is the durable half, and it is monotone in both columns. Over the
control cells' own CALMAPs it reads:

| `Kc` | mean `est1` | clip fraction of the cut cell |
|---|---|---|
| 64 | 69528 | 87.742% |
| 128 | 85110 | 85.586% |
| 256 | 94860 | 82.961% |
| 512 | 128443 | 82.346% |

A shallower chunk is quantized finer. The returned quantum is `est1*margin/127`, and `est1`
falls 0.5413x from `Kc`=512 to `Kc`=64 inside one run. A shallower chunk also clips more,
because a partial over fewer terms has a heavier relative tail. The second cause is that a
512-row maximum undershoots the 2048-row maximum more often. The reading that a shallower
contraction clips less is refuted within a single run as well as between runs.

**`Kc` is not a sufficient label for a population.** The `Kc`=256 columns read `est1` 94860 at
`KCHUNK`=512 and 102305 at `KCHUNK`=256, because they sit at different offsets into K in the
two walks.

The tail sub-population is a control the split does not reach. The K=960 walk ends 128+64 at
both chunk sizes, so those 589824+589824 columns are the identical population in both cells.
They read identical (`est1` 1.0002x and 0.9997x, clip 1.0008x and 1.0004x). The two cells
therefore differ *only* in their deep chunks. A common additive term can only pull a ratio
toward 1, so the measured 0.4423x is an upper bound on the deep chunks' own fall.

The cap in the arm's own registration is wrong. It declares the arm unable to separate chunk
count from chunk depth "by construction", and the ragged walk separates them.

[Registered and pushed before the control cell. Scored under a gate that refuses unless thirteen already-scored `Dcal` values reproduce to
5e-4.]

##### Three checkpoints at a fixed chunk depth

At a fixed chunk depth the per-column clipping converges across models and the damage does not,
and the numerators invert. The chunk depth is a knob, so it can be held equal across models that
differ in everything else. `ROCKET_RK3576_FORCESPLIT=1 ROCKET_RK3576_KCHUNK=512` gives all three
checkpoints that share the smollm tokenizer a maximum chunk depth of exactly 512. The walks are
`576 -> 512+64` and `1536 -> 3x512` on the 135M, `960 -> 512+256+128+64` and `2560 -> 5x512` on
the 360M, and `2048 -> 4x512` and `8192 -> 16x512` on the 1.7B. Their shipped maxima are 1536,
2560 and 2304.

Before the cells ran, the closed form gave two exact forward population integers. They are
853248 columns with the `Kc` histogram {512: 478464, 64: 374784}, and 6684672 columns with
{512: 6684672}. All five values landed, which makes the form exact on 16 of 16.

| | SmolLM2-135M | SmolLM2-360M | SmolLM2-1.7B |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Dcal`(cut 512, margin 1.00), shipped walk | +0.51870 | +0.69288 | +0.97094 |
| the same at `KCHUNK`=512 | **+0.36230** | **+0.16382** | **+0.13742** |
| as a fraction of its own shipped reading | 0.6985 | 0.2364 | **0.1415** |
| column-weighted chunk count `C` at `KCHUNK`=512 | 2.0844 | 4.0943 | 4.8571 |
| `p` in `FALL = C^-p` | 0.4886 | 1.0231 | **1.4710** |
| clip fraction of that cell | 84.481% | 84.392% | 82.727% |
| `D_res` = `Dcal`(no cut, 2.25) at `KCHUNK`=512 | +0.00758 | +0.00448 | +0.00750 |
| as a fraction of its own shipped reading | 0.3252 | 0.3019 | 0.3640 |

[HW sweep, 6 cells `rc=0`, `-ub` 2048 `--chunks` 4, `.so` `ea40bc1b`, corpus `3ce70e93`. Each
cell ran 95-607 s, backstop 689 -> 689, 0 dmesg WARNINGs, taint 4608, `ondemand`, 2026-08-22.
The 360M column is arm (ac)'s, not re-run. Levels are comparable here only because the three
share a tokenizer.]

The maximum chunk depth does not set the level, and the ordering inverts. At a fixed maximum
depth the three numerators span 2.637x, *wider* than the 1.8719x they span on their own shipped
walks. They run monotone decreasing in scale, where the shipped readings run monotone
increasing. That is neither of the two outcomes the question had: not a collapse, and not a
surviving spread.

A common multiplicative fall is refuted with the inversion, since it predicts that the 1.8719x
is preserved. So is `1/C`, which predicts +0.24885 and +0.25701, missing 1.46x high and 0.53x low.
No single power of the count fits either: `p` is itself monotone in scale over a 3.01x range.

What does converge at a fixed depth is the per-column observable. Read on the `Kc`=512
sub-population alone, which has the same chunk depth in all three walks, the cut cells give:

| `Kc`=512 sub-population | SmolLM2-135M | SmolLM2-360M | SmolLM2-1.7B | spread |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| clip fraction | 81.985% | 82.346% | 82.727% | 1.0091x |
| mean `est1` | 117277 | 128443 | 115208 | 1.1149x |

The depth mechanism therefore controls what it is claimed to control at the column level,
across three checkpoints that differ 12.7x in parameter count. The damage those columns do to
the loss is 2.637x apart at the same time. What the split does to a column belongs to the chunk
depth, and what it does to the loss does not.

The two channels separate a sixth time, and here `D_res` is the well-behaved one. The resolution
channel falls by a nearly common factor on all three models, 0.3252, 0.3019 and 0.3640, a 1.21x
spread. The clip channel falls 0.6985, 0.2364 and 0.1415, a 4.94x spread. A single
multiplicative response to the split describes one channel and not the other, on the same
three cells.

The arm declared a cap before the cells, and the cells confirm it. At `KCHUNK`=512, `C` is
2.0844, 4.0943 and 4.8571, monotone in scale over exactly these three checkpoints. The arm
therefore cannot separate a chunk-count driver from a scale one. The inversion refutes both, so
the separation is not needed. The arm also does not hold the depth *distribution* equal. On the
135M, 43.9% of the columns sit at `Kc`=64, the 360M has 66.4% below 512, and the 1.7B is uniform
at 512.

[Registered and pushed before either control cell. Scored under a gate that refuses unless ten already-scored `Dcal` values reproduce to 5e-4.]

##### The `KCHUNK` ladder on SmolLM2-135M

The maximum chunk depth is not the driver, and the `n_embd`-K GEMMs carry the damage. A
five-rung `KCHUNK` ladder ran on one checkpoint (SmolLM2-135M, `-c/-b/-ub` 2048, `--chunks` 4).
Each rung runs against its own control, because a different chunk size is a different
arithmetic:

| `KCHUNK` | `K`=576 walk | `K`=1536 walk | max depth | `C` | columns | `Dcal`(512, 1.00) | `D_res` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| off | 576 | 1536 | 1536 | 1.0000 | 409344 | **+0.51870** | +0.02332 |
| 2048 | 512+64 | 1536 | **1536** | 1.9156 | 784128 | **+0.37641** | +0.02164 |
| 1024 | 512+64 | 1024+512 | 1024 | 2.0000 | 818688 | **+0.37081** | −0.00783 |
| 512 | 512+64 | 3x512 | 512 | 2.0844 | 853248 | **+0.36230** | +0.00758 |
| 256 | 256+256+64 | 6x256 | 256 | 3.2533 | 1331712 | **+0.16241** | −0.00257 |
| 128 | 4x128+64 | 12x128 | 128 | 5.5909 | 2288640 | **+0.06283** | +0.00343 |

`KCHUNK`=2048 and the unsplit control have the same maximum chunk depth, and `Dcal` falls 27.4%
between them. At 2048 the greedy walk leaves `K`=1536 whole: 2304 and 2048 both exceed it, so
the walk picks 1536, while `K`=576 still splits `512+64`. A law in the maximum chunk depth
predicts exactly zero on that pair. It is refuted with no fitted exponent and no argument about
which tensors carry the loss. **The ladder cannot reach `KCHUNK`=64, which refuses**: `ffn_down`
needs 24 chunks against the walk's 16-chunk cap.

`ffn_down` is nearly inert. Its whole split takes one chunk of 1536 down to `3x512`. That is a
3x cut in the program's deepest chunk over 12.2% of the columns, and it is worth 3.7% of `Dcal`.
The `n_embd`-K family (q/k/v/o/gate/up and the output head) carries 88% of the 31.1% fall, on
87.8% of the columns. The 1024-vs-512 pair moves only inside `ffn_down`'s 3.7%. It is a real
measurement of an aggregate, and it says nothing about the mechanism.

A count power law with the single parameter `p`=0.4886 is fitted at unsplit -> 512. It predicts
unsplit -> 2048 (0.7258 measured against 0.7278) and unsplit -> 1024 (0.7149 against 0.7127)
both to 0.3%. It misses 256 and 128 by 1.79x and 3.56x. The boundary falls exactly where
`n_embd` stops walking `512+64`. The exponent is therefore a property of how finely that family
is split, not of the program's chunk count. The segment exponents swing 41x on the depth:

| segment | 1024 -> 512 | 512 -> 256 | 256 -> 128 |
|---|---|---|---|
| exponent on the count | 0.5616 | 1.8023 | 1.7540 |
| exponent on the depth | 0.0335 | 1.1576 | 1.3702 |

The 512 -> 256 segment ratio transfers across models to 1.3%. It is 0.44827 on the 135M against
the 360M's measured 0.44225, where the level at a fixed depth is 2.637x apart. A ratio is
portable across these checkpoints in a way no level is.

The two channels separate by tensor family. In exactly the two cells where `ffn_down` is one
chunk, `D_res` sits at the unsplit level. In the four where it is split, `D_res` collapses to
at most 0.0078 in absolute value, but with alternating sign. This is an observation over two
points against four, not a mechanism [hypothesis].

The mean `est1` of `ffn_down` itself falls monotonically with `Kc`:

| `Kc` | 1536 | 1024 | 512 | 256 | 128 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ffn_down` mean `est1` | 185839 | 148655 | 132503 | 108084 | 89437 |

The 749568 `n_embd`-side columns are frozen at `512+64` in the 2048, 1024 and 512 cells, and
they hold `est1` to 1.0013x. That cross-tab is exact because the CALMAP carries the tensor name
in column 0. It is also free, because a ragged walk leaves a sub-population untouched.

The population closed form is exact on 20 of 20 integers. Four of them are forward predictions
here, 818688, 1331712, 2288640 and 784128 columns, each with its `Kc` histogram. The `Kc`=64
sub-population holds the same 374784 columns in all five split cells, and reads `est1` within
1.0054x and clip within 1.0033x. The per-`Kc` clip transfer is good to 0.9 absolute, but it is
not monotone, since the 128 rung clips less than the 256 rung:

| rung | 1024 | 256 | 128 |
|---|---|---|---|
| predicted clip, % | 84.506 | 84.945 | 86.083 |
| measured clip, % | 84.643 | 85.826 | 85.244 |

The chunk count is refuted too, and the `n_embd` family's own maximum depth is what both
refutations leave standing. A sixth rung, `KCHUNK`=320, holds that family at exactly two chunks
and at a mean depth of exactly 288. The greedy walk takes `K`=576 to `320+256` where 512 took
it to `512+64`, and (512+64)/2 = (320+256)/2, while its maximum depth moves 512 -> 320. The
`Dcal` value falls 2.06x, +0.36230 -> +0.17629, against a count law's prediction of 0.9626 of
the 512 rung. The count law is refuted at 1.98x, and the mean-depth reading with it.

The two designed pairs therefore bracket the answer from opposite sides. One shares a global
maximum depth and differs 27.4%, and the other shares a count and a mean depth and differs
2.06x.

Over seven rungs, a power law in the `n_embd` maximum depth fits to a worst factor of
1.1624x, against the global count's 1.3934x. The three cells sharing an `n_embd` depth of 512
read within 3.89%, while their global `C` spans 8.81%. The local exponent still runs 2.72,
1.53, 0.37 and 1.37 over the segments. **What is supported is therefore an ordering and a
residual, never an exponent.** The regression's 1.20x edge is weak beside the two pairs, which
carry the result.

A chunk depth that is not a power of two takes a narrower rotation, and that cannot reach
`Dcal`. The 320 rung is the first such depth run here, and there `rk_hadamard_ok` is false. The
rotation is therefore the block-diagonal fallback at b = `Kc & -Kc` = 64, narrower than a full H
of the chunk's own width. The rotation is orthonormal, so the exact per-chunk accumulator is
unchanged. Because `Dcal` differences two cells at the same `KCHUNK`, the width cancels to first
order [expected]. It survives as a second-order term, since `est1` is read off the quantized
product and not the exact one [expected].

On the control's own level, where the width does act at first order, both single-regressor fits
are refuted. The column-weighted depth and the rotation width correlate +0.9963 across the five
split rungs, and decorrelate only at 320. There depth-only predicts 15.3112 and rotation 15.0787,
against a measured 15.2165. Those misses are 3.2x and 5.3x their own worst in-sample residuals.
Only a chunk-count fit survives there (15.2061, error +0.0104 against a residual of 0.0255).
That fit is unregistered, so its survival is an observation owing a forward band [expected].

The per-`Kc` clip ordering inverts within a single cell. At `KCHUNK`=320, `Kc`=256 clips 81.153%
against `Kc`=320's 82.457%, so the shallower chunk clips less. That is the opposite of the 512
rung's 87.667 against 81.985. The mean `est1` inverts with it (106069 against 87610, where the
deeper chunk sums more terms). The two chunks cover different k-ranges (`n_embd` k[0,320)
against k[320,576)), and the range beats the depth. The cell therefore shows, inside one cell
rather than across two, that `Kc` is not a population label.

The whole-cell clip is 81.879%, below every other split rung and below the transfer's own 83.540
prediction. The transfer's per-`Kc` misses here, −2.725 and −0.812 absolute, are its worst.

The reading that the `Kc`=64 chunks carry the damage is refuted from cells already on disk. The
320 rung is the only walk without them. That population is held at exactly 374784 in all five
other split rungs, while `Dcal` spans 5.99x. Its fraction reading predicts a 0% cell below the
128 rung's +0.06283, and the 320 rung sits 2.81x above it. Whether removing them contributes to
the 2.06x fall is unbounded. No per-column statistic can bound a loss share, and this rung has
no frozen sub-population.

[Registered, each pushed before its cells. Scored under a gate that refuses unless seven already-scored `Dcal` values reproduce to
5e-4. Twelve cells, 92-156 s each, backstop delta 0.]

Neither family's split does anything on its own: `D_res` collapses only when both are split.
The one reachable chunk size whose `n_embd`-K family takes a single chunk is `KCHUNK`=576. There
the forced branch of `rk76_ksplit` returns the same one-element list as its non-forced branch.
So 73.1% of the columns are bit-identical to the unsplit cell, and the rung measures
`ffn_down`'s 1 -> 4 chunk step alone. It completes a 2×2 whose other three corners are already
on disk:

| | `ffn_down` whole | `ffn_down` split |
|---|---|---|
| **`n_embd` whole** | +0.02332 (unsplit) | **+0.02376** (`KCHUNK`=576) |
| **`n_embd` split** | +0.02164 (`KCHUNK`=2048) | −0.00783 … +0.01187 (four rungs) |

Splitting `ffn_down` four ways with `n_embd` whole moves `D_res` by +0.00044, which is 1.9% of
the collapse the both-split rungs show. Splitting `n_embd` alone moves it −0.00168. The reading
that `D_res` collapses wherever `ffn_down` is split held over five cells, and it is refuted.
Every one of those five cells split both families, so the reading took a condition of two
families one family at a time.

The family localization is not additive either. At this rung `Dcal` reads +0.51361, an
`ffn_down` step of −0.00509 against the −0.01411 the same tensor costs with `n_embd` split. That
is 0.360x, and 0.98% of its own baseline against 3.75%, so a scale effect does not explain it. A
share measured for a tensor family is a property of the other family's state, not of the tensor.
The 0.360x is not an interaction of the two families: the matched-count rung below reads the
same step at +0.00689, the opposite sign. The two `ffn_down` steps differ because they are
different chunk counts on a curve that is not monotone in the count.

The control-level chunk-count fit is refuted forward. The cell reads 15.2313 against a predicted
15.2897, a miss of 2.3 worst in-sample residuals. The depth and rotation fits, which it beat
post hoc, read 7.4 and 10.6 on the same measure. Its miss is monotone in the extrapolation
(0.0255 worst inside the fitted range, −0.0584 at `C`=1.25328, −0.0955 at `C`=1). It therefore
fails as an extrapolation rather than as a fit.

The bit-identity of 73.1% of the columns is what predicts the cell. That agreement conceals a
sign inversion: `ffn_down`'s own control step is +0.0050 here against −0.0060 at the
2048 -> 512 pair.

**Most `KCHUNK` values are not rungs.** On this model, 182 values of `k` pass
`rk76_k_single_ok` and give a terminating walk. The greedy walk collapses 137 of them onto the
`KCHUNK`=2048 rung's walk, and only 16 distinct `C` exist. Setting `KCHUNK`=1536 lands on one of
the 137, a bit-identical replicate rather than a new point. **Print the whole map before
choosing a value.**

The forward population is exact (513024 columns, `Kc` 576:443904 256:34560 128:34560). The
whole-cell clip reads 82.538% against a predicted 81.214%. The miss belongs to the prediction,
which substituted the 2048 rung's `Kc`=1536 clip rate for the unsplit cell's.

**The bit-identity is the program's, not the columns'.** Block *L*'s `n_embd` GEMMs read
activations that passed through block *L*−1's `ffn_down`. Once the other family is split, only
block 0's columns are identical in value. Five cells share the `n_embd` walk [512,64]: `KCHUNK`
2048, 1024, 768, 832 and 512. Their `frozen` group's clip rate spans 84.174% to 85.629%, a
1.455 pp spread, on identical arithmetic over identical 749568 columns.

Inverting a whole-cell clip rate for one chunk's contribution carries a `d/dr` lever of 10.84
here, so that spread is 15.8 pp on the result. **No per-`Kc` rate is quotable out of such an
inversion**, and any gap read off one is inside its own substitution error. Of the
demonstrations that `Kc` is not a population label, the two that compare rates measured *inside
one cell* are unaffected. So is every `Dcal` and `D_res` figure above, since each is a
difference between whole cells.

##### The `ffn_down` chunk-count dose-response

The `ffn_down` chunk-count dose-response is not monotone, and `C` does not index the control
level. Two further rungs come from the one walk group that holds `n_embd` at [512,64] and moves
only `ffn_down`. Each is therefore a matched pair against a cell already on disk.

**`KCHUNK`=832** gives `ffn_down` [832,512,128,64], four chunks with `n_embd` split, the
matched-count complement of the 576 rung above. It reads `Dcal` = +0.38330, a 1 -> 4 step of
+0.00689 against −0.00509 for the same step with `n_embd` whole. The two steps have opposite
signs. The fixed-`n_embd` dose-response therefore reads:

| chunk count | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Dcal` | +0.37641 | +0.37081 | +0.36230 | **+0.38330** |

It reverses at the fourth chunk and lands above the one-chunk cell. A three-point extrapolation
from the first three cells gives a per-chunk step of −0.00560 then −0.00851, apparently growing.
It misses by 0.0324, which is 3.8x the largest measured step. **Do not fit a `Dcal` law in the
chunk count.**

**`KCHUNK`=768** gives `ffn_down` [768,768], which matches the `KCHUNK`=1024 rung's `C`
(2.00000), chunk count (2), column population (818688) and `n_embd` walk exactly. The two rungs
differ only in the depth mix (maximum 1024 -> 768) and the rotation width. The rotation width
differs because `rk_hadamard_ok` admits 1024 and 512 at full width and gives 768 only H_256 over
three aligned blocks. The rung reads `Dcal` = +0.36823, a move of −0.00258, under a third of the
smallest measured per-chunk step. At held `C` and held count, the depth mix and rotation width
are nearly inert on this channel. That is what makes the 832 rung's +0.021 specific to that walk
rather than generic to a re-cut.

The same pair moves the control level 0.0496 in PPL[4]: 15.1990 against 15.2486, on two cells
identical in `C`, chunk count, population and `n_embd` walk. The chunk-count, maximum-depth and
rotation fits were each fitted on that column. Their worst in-sample residuals are 0.0255, 0.0295
and 0.0262, and the walk-specific variation at a *single* point of `C` is 1.7-1.9x every one of
them. Perplexity here is deterministic, so the gap is the two cells' difference and nothing
else.

`C` does not index the control level. This is measured directly rather than by
extrapolation, on one pair at one `C`, which bounds those fits without measuring a law. The two
channels dissociate again inside the same two cells. Over the two calibrating windows the
controls differ by only −0.00264 nats, 1.02x what `Dcal` moves. The whole 0.0496 therefore lives
in the non-calibrating windows.

The `D_res` reading is +0.00732 at 832 and +0.01911 at 768. The both-split cells span
[−0.00783, +0.01911], against three one-or-neither cells at [+0.02164, +0.02376]. The both-split
collapse therefore holds as a strict ordering over ten cells, with its margin down from 0.0098
to 0.0025. Both forward populations are exact (887808 and 818688 columns, `Kc` histograms
exact).

`KCHUNK`=832 is the only measured cell on this model whose `ffn_down` walk goes below 128. It is
the one carrying a 64-deep chunk, which is the standing candidate for the reversal [hypothesis].
The `KCHUNK` values 960 ([960,512,64]) and 896 ([896,512,128]) join 512 ([3x512]) as a
three-cell family at `C`=2.08443, count 3 and `n_embd` [512,64]. The three differ only in the
minimum chunk depth. That is the pair structure which the 768 rung shows carries almost nothing
on its own.

[Registered and pushed before the six
