cells. Scored under a gate refusing unless eight already-scored values
reproduce to 5e-4, with a second reader for the frozen-group spread. Six cells, 95-105 s
each, backstop delta 0.]

Open: what sets the cross-model level, and whether the interaction is a property of the two
families or of chunk count against baseline.

[Registered and pushed before the cells.
Scored under the same seven-value gate. Three cells, 90-107 s each,
backstop delta 0.]

##### `BOOTMARGIN` and the no-cut response

`BOOTMARGIN` covers a drift between the two calibrating forwards, and below 1.25 the no-cut
response turns around. Four no-cut cells on Qwen2.5-1.5B span the whole margin axis at
`-ub` 2048. There is no row cut in any of them, so each one's whole reading is what the margin
alone does:

| `BOOTMARGIN` | `Dcal` (windows 1-2) | clipped of 1852160 | what the cell is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1.00 | **+0.04066** | 961471 (**51.911%**) | clipping-dominated, not a resolution reading |
| 1.25 | **-0.00761** | 4760 (0.257%) | near zero confound |
| 1.50 | 0 | n/a | the control, zero by construction |
| 2.25 | +0.00811 | 0 | zero confound |

[HW sweep, 2 new cells `rc=0`, `--chunks` 4, `.so` `ea40bc1b`, backstop 650 -> 650, taint 4608,
0 WARNINGs, 2026-08-22.]

The margin exists to cover a drift between the two calibrating forwards. At no cut, `est1` is
pass one's maximum over *all* `M` rows. The natural reading is that pass two at margin 1.00 then
saturates exactly at its own maximum and barely clips. It does not. Pass one's quantization
changes every downstream activation, so pass two's per-column maximum is a different draw.
**At zero headroom, 51.9% of columns exceed it.**

A margin of 1.25 covers all but 0.257%, and 1.50 and 2.25 cover essentially everything. So
`BOOTMARGIN` is headroom for that drift, not slack for nothing.

By this construction there is no zero-confound resolution cell below margin 1.5. A no-cut cell
stops being one exactly where the margin stops covering the drift, so `D_res(1.00)` is not
measurable this way.

The no-cut response is non-monotone in the margin. Its minimum over the four measured points is
1.25, rather than the library default of 1.50. There `Dcal` = -0.00761, a real gain at full rows
on this model. That reading covers the calibrating windows only. It is not a converged-window
result, and it leaves the shipped recommendation untouched.

Both forms used above for `D_res(m)` have the wrong shape below the control margin, not merely
the wrong coefficient. The form linear in the quantum and the form quadratic in it are both
monotone through 1.5. At 1.25 they give -0.00270 and -0.00198 against a measured -0.00761 (2.82x
and 3.84x, right sign). At 1.00 they have the wrong sign entirely, against +0.04066. **The
correction inside every published `R` and every `h`, `res_at_1p0`'s `-2/3 * D_res`, is wrong in
sign at the one margin it was written for.**

Four of the five margins at cut `M`/4 have a measured no-cut cell, so
`D_clip(cut, m) = Dcal(cut, m) - Dcal(no cut, m)` is measured minus measured there. Over matched
intervals that subtraction takes the margin exponent's local spread from 1.322x to 1.192x. It
tightens the axis toward a power law without straightening it. Only 1.875, arm (y)'s own
held-out point, stays modeled.

[Registered and pushed before the first cell.]

##### The row cut at `M`/2

The row cut at `M`/2 is a net accuracy gain, and the model does not reach there. At `-ub` 2048,
`CALROWS`=1024 costs `Dcal` = -0.00703 at margin 1.5 and -0.01065 at 2.25 against `q_ctl`,
*negative* at both. The 8-window headline is -0.382% and -0.571% (7.2257 and 7.2120 against a
control 7.2534). The converged windows 3-8 read -0.00393 +/- 0.00268 and -0.00586 +/- 0.00340
se, with 4 of 6 better in each. With n=6, the converged column decides nothing on its own. Both
cells are `rc=0` and `.so`/corpus/model md5-gated, with a backstop delta of 0.

The dial is read back out of the cells' own bytes rather than off the launcher. The
`r` on the clean set of each perplexity process's own CALMAP is 0.94335. That is strictly
between the no-cut 1.0 and the `M`/4 cut's 0.87151, so 1024 rows were used.

The damage is therefore non-monotone in the cut. As the cut deepens, `Dcal` runs:

| cut | 2048 | 1024 | 512 | 128 | 32 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| margin 1.5 | 0 | -0.0070 | +0.1276 | +0.6761 | +1.9985 |
| margin 2.25 | +0.0081 | -0.0107 | +0.0266 | +0.1052 | |

There is a zero crossing between `M`/2 and `M`/4 on both margins. The shipped `M`/4 setting sits
past an optimum, rather than at the cheap end of a rising cost.

A row cut is an accidental percentile-clipping calibrator [hypothesis, the sign, the margin
ordering and the crossing are measured, and this mechanism is not isolated]. Pass one's `est1`
is a per-column maximum, and cutting its rows makes that maximum an under-estimate. So
`sc2 = 127/(est1*margin)` gets finer on every column while a few columns clip. Below some cut,
the finer quantum on the bulk is worth more than the clipped tail.

That is why the gain is larger at the coarser margin: -0.0107 at 2.25 against -0.0070 at 1.5.
At 1.5 the baseline quantum is already fine, and there is less to win. It is the same trade
that a percentile or MSE-optimal clipping calibrator makes deliberately.

This has two consequences for the model above. First, its `g` cannot be a smooth positive
function of the cut, so it is bounded to `M`/4 and deeper. **It must not be extrapolated
shallower.** Second, `D_res = 0.0081*(R-1)/0.5` with `R = (margin/1.5)*r` is refuted as a
resolution index for `R < 1.5`. Two cells that clip essentially nothing read +0.0081 at R = 1.50
and -0.0107 at R = 1.42. So `D_res` is not monotone in `R`, and the margin and the cut do not
reach the returned surface through one combined index.

The margin rescales `est1` by a constant on every column, and a row cut rescales it by a
column-dependent amount. Every `D_clip` reported in the sections above inherits that. **Read
them as `Dcal` minus a *model*, not as a measured split.**

##### Width of the `M`/2 gain

The gain is a knife-edge at `M`/2, and it is narrower than a four-point interpolation can see.
Bracketing that minimum at margin 2.25 puts a cell on each side of `M`/2, and both come back
positive. The whole cut axis at the shipped margin, as `Dcal` against `q_ctl`, reads:

| cut | 2048 | **1536** | 1024 | **768** | 512 | 128 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `M`/ | 1 | 1.33 | 2 | 2.67 | 4 | 16 |
| `d` = 1-`r` | 0 | 0.020650 | 0.053643 | 0.090150 | 0.128489 | 0.255830 |
| `Dcal` | +0.00811 | **+0.00335** | -0.01065 | **+0.00931** | +0.02656 | +0.10521 |
| vs no cut | 0 | -0.00476 | -0.01876 | +0.00120 | +0.01845 | +0.09710 |

The minimum is therefore at `M`/2, and the well around it is sharp. One rung shallower (a 25%
cut rather than 50%) keeps only 25.4% of the gain. One rung deeper (62.5%) keeps -6.4% of it,
so 768 is already worse than not cutting at all. The 8-window headlines, which are not
converged costs, are 7.2534 at 2048, 7.2404 at 1536, 7.2120 at 1024 and 7.2648 at 768.
[HW sweep, 2 cells, `rc=0`, `.so`/corpus/model md5-gated, backstop delta 0, 2026-08-22]

No interpolation of the four cells measured before these two reaches either value, and all
three interpolations tried get the sign wrong at 1536. The three are a cubic in `d`, a two-term
`0.00811 - K d + C d^k` in `d`, and a cubic in ln(`M`/`M1`). They fit the same four points in
three variables. At cut 1536 they predict -0.0053, -0.0193 and -0.0091, against a measured
+0.00335. They spread 3.6x among themselves and lie on the wrong side of zero from the
measurement. The cause is that the feature they interpolate across is narrower than their own
knot spacing.

**A row-cut curve on this axis must be measured at the cut it is quoted at.** Four knots an
octave apart are not a curve here.

The mixed-`M` population is larger than its weight count suggests, and it does not explain the
dip. Under `llama-perplexity` at `-ub` 2048, 352000 of 1852160 calibrated columns (19.0%) sit at
`M`=1024. By weight count that group is only 4 of 197. The prompt of `llama-bench` gives a
uniform `M`=2048, which is why `r` above is the `M`=2048 population's.

Since `CALROWS` clamps only when it is strictly below `M`, that group is uncut at both 1536 and
1024 and first cut at 768. The mixture therefore predicts a kink at 768 and nothing at all at
1024, while the minimum is at 1024. Re-weighting `d` by the mixture (0.0167 at 1536, 0.0435 at
1024, 0.0769 at 768 and 0.1143 at 512) rescales the abscissa and leaves the dip exactly as
sharp. The dip survives every reparametrization tried, and it is not an artifact of the
mixture.

The gain is Qwen's, not the route's. SmolLM2-1.7B at the same cut and margin reads `Dcal` =
+0.03770, where Qwen reads -0.01065: the opposite sign at 3.5x the magnitude. Its headline is
7.4427 against 7.4265 (+0.218%), where Qwen's is -0.571% [HW sweep, 2 cells, 2026-08-22].

The SmolLM2 cell runs against its own `-c` 2048 control. A `-c` 2048 run re-segments the
corpus, so no `-c` 512 level and no other model's control is comparable. Both cells are `rc=0`
and md5-gated, with a backstop delta of 0. The next section completes that model's cut axis and
says where the difference lives.

**`CALROWS`=1024 at `-ub` 2048 is not shippable.** It is the only setting on this axis that
beats the control at margin 2.25. Its advantage does not survive a 25% move of the
`CALROWS`/`M` ratio in either direction. On the second model run at this ubatch it is a cost
rather than a gain. Because `CALROWS` is an absolute row count, every ubatch change moves that
ratio. Two models make up the whole corpus at `-ub` 2048, so **quote the `M`/2 gain as Qwen's
own number and nothing more**.

[The margin-1.5 axis has no 1536 or 768 cell, so the well's width there is unmeasured.]

##### The cut axis on SmolLM2-1.7B

The second model has no optimum at all, and the resolution channel is 2.54x model-specific.
Three more SmolLM2-1.7B cells complete that model's margin-2.25 cut axis, each against its own
`-c` 2048 control (PPL[8] 7.4265). The table sets them beside Qwen's:

| cut | `M`/ | Qwen `Dcal` | Qwen ppl8 | SmolLM2 `Dcal` | SmolLM2 ppl8 | SmolLM2 clip |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2048 | 1 (none) | +0.00811 | -0.290% | **+0.02060** | -0.606% | **0.0000%** |
| 1536 | 1.33 | +0.00335 | -0.179% | **+0.02405** | +0.191% | 0.0294% |
| 1024 | 2 | -0.01065 | -0.571% | +0.03770 | +0.218% | n/a |
| 512 | 4 | +0.02656 | +0.301% | **+0.05278** | +0.063% | 0.3705% |

SmolLM2's damage is monotone in the cut depth, and the model has no optimum. Every cut is worse
than not cutting, where Qwen's damage has a minimum at `M`/2. **The dip is Qwen's alone, and no
cut is free-or-better on both models at `-ub` 2048.** Subtracting each model's own no-cut level
leaves the row cut's own term. That term is opposite in sign at both shallow cuts: -0.00477
against +0.00345 at 1536, and -0.01876 against +0.01710 at `M`/2. At `M`/4 both models pay,
+0.01845 against +0.03218 [HW sweep, 3 cells, `rc=0`, `.so`/corpus/model md5-gated, backstop
delta 0, 2026-08-22].

The no-cut cell clips exactly zero columns on both models (0 of 1500160 and 0 of 1769472). It
therefore measures the resolution channel with its confound nulled rather than merely small. It
costs +0.00811 against +0.02060, a 2.54x model difference. A third model reads +0.01626 at the
same zero confound (0 of 1272320 columns). The channel therefore spans 2.00x across three
models. It does not follow the margin index, which two of the three share to 0.9%.

Splitting the models' gap at the one cut both ran gives 25.8% to the margin channel and 74.2% to
the cut channel. Both channels differ, and the cut channel carries three quarters.

**The mixed-`M` population differs between the models and must come out before a magnitude is
quoted.** Read off each model's own control CALMAP, the `M`=1024 minority is:

| model | `M`=1024 columns | calibrated columns | share |
|---|---|---|---|
| Qwen | 352000 | 1852160 | 19.0% |
| SmolLM2-1.7B | 151552 | 1769472 | 8.57% |
| Llama-3.2-1B | 309760 | 1272320 | 24.35% |
| SmolLM2-360M | 110464 | 651264 | 16.96% |

The shares spread 2.8x, and the spread does not follow width: the two models sharing
`n_embd`/`n_ff` sit at its two ends. Since `CALROWS` clamps only when it is strictly below `M`,
more of Qwen's columns escape uncut at a given cut.

###### The mixed-`M` population

The mixed-`M` population is the output head plus one block's FFN, and it is exactly derivable
from the GGUF header. Cross-tabbing a control CALMAP by GEMM group, rather than only by `M`,
shows what the minority is. It is not scattered through the model. Every block's q/k/v/o sits
at `M`=2048, and the `M`=1024 rows are the output head plus exactly one block's gate, up and
down. The mechanism is upstream of this driver: `llama-perplexity` asks for logits on only
half of each 2048-token window. So ggml prunes the graph to those output rows before the final
block's FFN, and everything before the prune runs at the full ubatch.

The CALMAP logs one column per output column of each offloaded GEMM. A K-split `ffn_down` logs
its `N` columns once per chunk, so per calibrating call:

    per_block = 2*n_embd + 2*head_kv*head_dim + 2*n_ff + n_embd*chunks(n_ff)
    total     = n_blocks*per_block + vocab
    minority  = vocab + 2*n_ff + n_embd*chunks(n_ff)

where `chunks(K)` is `rk76_ksplit`'s greedy walk down `RK76_KCHUNK_PREF` and is 1 for any `K`
the part takes whole. The formula reproduces all ten measured integers, five minority counts
and five totals, over five models, exactly, not approximately. Both second-order terms are
load-bearing. The minority share each reduced formula gives, against the measured share:

| Measured | Head alone | Per-chunk `ffn_down` logging dropped |
|---:|---:|---:|
| 19.005% | 16.41% | 21.50% |
| 8.565% | 5.56% | 9.82% |
| 24.346% | 20.16% | 29.04% |
| 16.961% | 15.09% | 16.96% |

The formula predicted the fifth model from its header before its control cell ran, and the
measurement landed on both integers. SmolLM2-135M (576/1536, 30 blocks, `head_kv` 3, vocab
49152) was registered at total 409344 and minority 105600, share 25.797%, and measured
409344 / 105600. That share is the highest of the five, which run 8.565 / 16.961 / 19.005 /
24.346 / 25.797%. The smallest model sits highest, because the dominant term is `vocab/total`
and a smaller model divides the same vocabulary by less.

The share is therefore set by the vocabulary size, the FFN width and the `ffn_down` chunk
count. It follows neither width nor depth, because its dominant term is `vocab/total` and the
vocabulary is an axis independent of both. That is why the two models sharing `n_embd`/`n_ff`
(vocab 49152 and 128256) sit at opposite ends of the spread. **Compute the share from the
header before quoting any cross-model calibration magnitude.** It needs no CALMAP pass.

[host-computed, derived from the four control CALMAPs and the GGUF headers, and confirmed as a forward prediction on a fifth, 2026-08-22. It does not
establish which block the FFN belongs to, which is inferred from the prune rather than read
from the `key` column. The rule is also a statement about `llama-perplexity` at `-ub` 2048,
not about `llama-bench`, where the `M` column is uniform.]

Modeling the damage as a function of the per-column ratio alone, and adding across the two
groups, changes the shipped fraction's model ratio. It goes from a raw 1.74x to 1.19x
[expected, derived, not measured, it is a linearization, and no independent consistency check exists
inside the data]. The re-weighting cannot touch the opposite signs. Re-weighting a population
multiplies the cut term by a positive factor, and a positive factor cannot change a sign.

`Dcal` and the 8-window headline disagree in direction across the two models, which is what
the calibrating-window scope means in practice. At `M`/4, SmolLM2 costs 1.99x Qwen's `Dcal`
while its headline is 5x smaller (+0.063% against +0.301%). The margin alone at full rows is a
headline gain on both models (-0.290% and -0.606%), while it costs on the calibrating windows.
So `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 does not trade accuracy away outside the windows it repairs. The converged
column (windows 3-8) is negative in 9 of the 10 cells either model has run here. At n=6 per
cell and with a shared control, those are not independent readings, and none resolves.

[What this cannot see: any margin but 2.25, or any ubatch but 2048. It cannot see a converged
cost, because `Dcal` is the two calibrating windows' damage and a positive still owes a depth
cell. It cannot see decode, or a third model, since heterogeneity at `-ub` 512 is Q = 6.665 on
2 df. Nor does it see SmolLM2's behavior between `M`/2 and `M`/4, where Qwen's zero crossing
sits. It says where the models differ, in a cut-indexed term or not, and not why. A carrier
indexed by neither would land in the no-cut cell identically, and the two models differ in
`n_embd`/`n_ff` (1536/8960 against 2048/8192).]

A saturating form in the clip fraction is refuted. The form `D_clip = A*x/(x + x0)` with
A = 0.1434 holds the four cells at `x` <= 4.35 to 12%. Its supremum is A at every `x`, yet
`q_r128` sits 4.86x above it and `q_r32` 14.13x above it. At margin 1.875 it reads 1.65x high.
Clipping is the carrier, but no function of the clip fraction alone predicts the damage.

Severity is not a discriminator either. The cells `q_r512` and `q_shipfrac` clip by the same
relative amount, mean 1.11485 against 1.11564, 0.07% apart, at 52x different clip counts. A
cut cell's saturated columns therefore always sit about a tenth over the ceiling, and only
their number moves.

The clipped mass is not a discriminator either, nor is the depth the mass sits at. Normalized
by clip count, `D_clip` spreads 13.51x across the three cut cells. Normalized by clipped mass
it spreads 6.85x, and normalized by mass weighted with the blocks downstream of each clipped
column it spreads 6.93x. The depth profile of the clipped mass is near-identical between them
(22-30% in `blk.0-3`, 36-47% in `blk.18-27`).

The `llama-bench` prompt under-states the clip fraction 2.0-5.5x against the wiki corpus.
Every clip fraction here comes from `llama-bench`, whose prompt is synthetic, and every `Dcal`
comes from the corpus. The clip fractions at the one cut where both regimes have a CALMAP, and
at `M`/4:

| Cut | Margin | Corpus | `llama-bench` | Corpus / bench |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| The shared cut | 1.5 | 2.7975% | 1.3775% | 2.03x |
| The shared cut | 2.25 | 0.0968% | 0.0177% | 5.47x |
| `M`/4 | 1.875 | 1.6602% | 0.4963% | 3.35x |

The undershoot `r` transports far better: 0.94335 corpus against 0.94636 bench at `M`/2
(0.32%), and 0.90584 against 0.87151 at `M`/4 (3.9%). **So quote a clip fraction with its
prompt named, and treat a `llama-bench` figure as a lower bound.** The undershoot `r` can be
carried across. The fitted model above is indexed by cut and margin rather than by the clip
fraction, so the seam does not reach it.

`AROW` does not move the clip fraction by a constant, and its effect does not keep its sign.
The factor by which the flag moves each cell's clip fraction:

| Cell | Factor |
|---|---:|
| No-cut control | 8.6x (0.2600% -> 0.0303%) |
| `q_r512` | 1.85x |
| `q_shipfrac` | 3.45x |
| `q_r128` | 1.15x |
| `q_r32` | 0.923x (35.4030% -> 38.3480%) |

At `q_r32` the flag increases clipping. A single regime factor cannot carry a clip fraction
across the cut axis. One fitted on the shallow cuts extrapolates through 1 before the deep
ones.

The clip fraction at any margin is computable off-line from a fixed cut's bytes. Pass one's
`est1` is margin-free given its input. A column therefore clips exactly when the oracle's
uncapped colmax exceeds `est1*margin`, and both vectors are on disk for every cut already run.

Two controls bound the computation. The first is `est1` itself, which is identical on the
clean set (0 of 4096). It drifts on 7.25% of all columns between margins 1.5 and 2.25. The
drift concentrates on small-code columns, with a p50 relative move of 1/3, because a
calibrating call's returned surface is the next layer's input. The second control re-evaluates
the sweep against the same cut's other `est1` vector. That moves the predicted clip by 0.9% at
margin 1.875, rising to 9.1% at 3.0, where the counts get small.

Against the device the computation reads 1.6% high at margin 1.5, 1.7% low at 2.25 and 5.7%
high at 1.875. The cells that produce it are `llama-bench`, whose prompt is synthetic, while
every `Dcal` comes from the wiki corpus. That is a regime seam beside `AROW` that nothing yet
measures.

**`est2` is a readback, not the accumulator.** Pass two writes
`est2[n] = max(1, max_m |C8|) / sc2[n]` with `sc2 = 127/(est1*margin)`, and sets `sat[n]` at
code 127 (`ggml-rocket.cpp:2013-2027`). On a saturated column it is therefore capped at the
ceiling. It is quantized to the returned surface's own code, margin/127 of `est1`, which is
1.77% at margin 2.25, and floored at one code. A no-cut cell at margin 2.25 saturates nothing.
Its `est2` is therefore the only uncapped per-column maximum on record, and the one instrument
that can price severity.

`Dcal` is reconstructible from any cell's own window line and is not carried between sessions.
A `llama-perplexity` `[k]` value is the cumulative PPL over the first k windows. Window k's
nats are therefore `k*ln(P[k]) - (k-1)*ln(P[k-1])`, and `Dcal` is the arm-minus-control sum
over the two calibrating windows. The reconstruction reproduces four independently scored
values to < 5e-4 nats.

[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-22. Three CALMAP cells (172-181 s) and one
perplexity cell (600 s), `.so`, corpus and model md5-gated, all `rc=0`, backstop delta 0,
taint 4608, 0 `dmesg` WARNINGs. The off-line sweeps took no device time. The two cells that
refute the saturating form ran in an earlier arm and cost only their CALMAP.]

The controls of that arm each report a magnitude. The oracle saturates 0 of 1500160 columns.
Where a cell did not clip, the oracle reproduces its own `est2` to a clean-set p90 of 1.1%,
the readback quantum's own size. It reproduces each cell's recorded `sat` flag on 99.805-100%
of clean-set columns, against a 12.7% count error at depth. The `Dcal` reconstruction above is
the fourth control.

What the arm cannot see: any model but Qwen2.5-1.5B, any ubatch but 2048, decode, or the
converged column (`Dcal` is the calibrating-window damage). It cannot see the 4 of 197 weights
that calibrate at `M`/2 under `llama-perplexity` but not under `llama-bench`. It cannot see
whether `h` keeps its shape outside [1.5, 2.25], and 1.875 is an interpolation in margin
though an extrapolation in `x`. Nor can it see any cut between `M` and `M`/4, where `g` is
unmeasured.

[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-21. One perplexity cell (678 s) and one CALMAP cell (219 s), `.so`, corpus and model
md5-gated, both `rc=0`, backstop delta 0, taint 4608, 0 `dmesg` WARNINGs. The est1 readout
took no device time.]

The est1 readout carries a control that must succeed. At fixed input `est1` is
margin-independent, measured at 0 of 4096 mismatching rows on the clean set. That set is
small by necessity. Of all rows, 11.7% disagree between two cells that differ only in the
margin. None of them is in the graph's first weight, and the share rises with depth. The cause
is that a calibrating call's returned surface is the next layer's input.

What that arm cannot see: any model but Qwen2.5-1.5B, any ubatch but 2048, the
`BOOTMARGIN`×`CALROWS` interior, decode, and depth. On depth, the converged column came back
-0.00522 +/- 0.00287 (5 of 6 windows better) against a null at `-ub` 512. The 6 windows
cannot carry the 27-62-window arms' weight, so that reading is recorded rather than claimed.

[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-21. One perplexity cell and four CALMAP cells, `.so`, corpus and model md5-gated, all
`rc=0`, backstop delta 0, 0 `dmesg` WARNINGs.]

A readback out of the instrument's own bytes confirms that `BOOTMARGIN` armed. On saturated
columns, `est2/est1` is 1.506-1.507 at margin 1.5 and 2.257-2.259 at 2.25. That separates "the
margin did not arm" from "channel one is inert". The scorer reproduces five recorded values
before scoring anything new. What it cannot see: another model (every anchor is
Qwen2.5-1.5B's), another ubatch, the rest of the `BOOTMARGIN`×`CALROWS` interior, or decode.
Nor can it see depth, since 6 converged windows cannot carry the 27-62-window arms' weight.

The ubatch does not set `M` uniformly under `llama-perplexity`. Of the 197 weights, 4
calibrate at `M`/2: `blk.27.ffn_{down,gate,up}` and `token_embd`. They are the last block's FFN
and the output head, which llama.cpp runs on a reduced token set. That holds at both
ubatches, so it dilutes the contrast symmetrically. Under `llama-bench` the `M` column is
uniform.

[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-21.
The `.so`, corpus and model were md5-gated, and all cells returned `rc=0`, with backstop
delta 0 and 0 `dmesg` WARNINGs. A `--chunks 4` control reproduces the `--chunks 8` control's
first four windows to four decimals. What it cannot see: another model (both anchors are
Qwen's), another margin, `-ub` 4096, decode, or depth, since 6 converged windows cannot carry
the 27-62-window arms' weight.]

##### The row cut's saving

What the row cut saves is a formula in the ubatch, and it is 18% at `-ub` 512. The saving is
linear in the rows the cut removes, so it is a property of `M` and not of the dial's setting
alone. At `M`=512 with `NCAL` at its shipped 2 (the accuracy cells' configuration) and CALMAP
off, `CALROWS`=128 + `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 removes 17.93% of `calibrate` per calibrating call
(105.02 -> 86.19 ms, 18.02% common-mode-corrected, 18.13% drift-corrected, 18.08% on a
reversed repeat). That is 7.42 s once per process over 394 calibrating calls. With the
common-mode process shift that its own `entry` bucket reports removed, `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 alone
is free at -0.17%. Its submit count is identical to the control's.

| `CALROWS` | `calibrate`/calibrating call | saving | submits |
|---|---|---|---|
| full `M`=512 | 105.02 ms | n/a | 12828 |
| 256 | 93.21 ms | 11.24% | 10868 |
| **128** (`M`/4, shipped candidate) | **86.19 ms** | **17.93%** | **9710** |
| 64 | 82.23 ms | 21.70% | **9710** |

Fitting the three cut points through the origin gives `s(M,M1) = (M - M1) / (1099 + 2M)` in
rows. Fitted entirely at `M`=512, it predicts the two `M`=2048 figures at 29.6% and 37.0%. The
measured values are 28.2% (`CALROWS`=512) and 36.3% (`CALROWS`=128). That is within 1.4
points across a 4x change in `M`, a different instrument state and a different epoch. So the
lever's value scales with the ubatch: ~18% at `-ub` 512, ~37% at 2048, ~43% at 4096 [the last
two extrapolated].

The ceiling at `-ub` 512 as `CALROWS` -> 0 is 24.1%. A cut at `M`/4 already takes 74.3% of
it, against a recorded +19.15% perplexity cost at `M`/16. So `M`/4 is near-optimal on price as
well as on accuracy.

The submit count floors at 128 rows, and the saving does not. The cells `r128` and `r64` emit
the identical 9710 submits, yet `calibrate` still falls 86.19 -> 82.23 ms across that floor,
13x the repeat spread. Above 128 rows the saving is submit-proportional to 0.2%, at 6.025 /
6.037 / 5.941 us per removed submit on three cells. Below it a second term carries the saving.
The row tile is quantized at 128 for submit count, while device time inside a submit still
scales with the actual rows [hypothesis]. **A row-count sweep that reads only the submit count
calls the dial dead below 128, and is wrong by 4 ms a call.**

`NCAL` does not change this fraction. It changes how many calibrating calls a process makes,
not what one costs. The shipped `NCAL`=2 reads -17.9% here, against -17.2% measured at
`NCAL`=1 two epochs earlier (86.1 -> 71.4 ms a calibrating call, submits 10690 -> 9131). So
the 28.2 -> 17.2 gap between the recorded figures is `M`, not `NCAL`. The 4-window aggregates
in these cells (8.7163 / 8.7435 / 9.0511) are the calibration-window artifact above, and they
are **not** accuracy reads.

[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, seven
`--chunks 4` cells, `setarch -R` armed by readback, one `.so`/corpus/model all md5-gated,
backstop delta 0, 2026-08-21. What it cannot see: decode, another model, the pool on, an
unpinned layout, and the accuracy of any row count but 128.]

#### `NCAL`=1 does not reach either model, and it buys a whole calibration forward

Dropping the second calibration forward changes what the frozen scale is built from. Its
exposure therefore lands in the converged windows, where no margin reaches, unlike the row
subsample's. On Qwen2.5-1.5B at `--chunks 8`, against the same-build `NCAL`=2 control of
9.9348:

| Qwen2.5-1.5B, `AROW`=1, K-split on | PPL | paired d(NLL) | windows 3-8 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `NCAL`=1 | 9.9702 | +0.0036 +/- 0.0039 (se) | +0.0028 +/- 0.0050, 4 of 6 positive |
| `NCAL`=1 + `CALROWS`=128 / `BOOTMARGIN`=2.25 | 9.9501 | +0.0015 +/- 0.0071 | -0.0055 +/- 0.0071 |

Neither is resolvable, and the readout's 0.061%-of-columns objection would have needed
roughly 18x the measured windows-3-8 shift to show. The saturation report is live in the
converged phase, unlike the calibrating branch, which feeds it `NULL`. It stayed silent in
both cells, which bounds the converged saturating fraction under its 0.1% threshold.

A second model agrees, and the depth this took is the finding. SmolLM2-1.7B is the model whose
`ffn_down` is `K`=8192, and it paid 4.2x more than Qwen for a frozen per-tensor scale (6.77x
against 1.600x). It ran at both window counts, with the same `.so` in the same boot:

| SmolLM2-1.7B, `AROW`=1, K-split on | windows | `ctl` | `NCAL`=1 | converged windows |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `--chunks 8` | 8 | 10.3354 | 10.4800 (**+1.40%**) | +0.0148 +/- 0.0113, 3 of 6 positive |
| every window the text has | 29 | 8.5473 | 8.5735 (**+0.31%**) | **+0.0024 +/- 0.0044**, 13 of 27 |

At depth the effect is 0.55 se from zero and within 0.06 se of Qwen's +0.0028. The two models
therefore agree on this term, and the 4.2x factor does not appear in it. Whether that factor
survives the per-row scale on any other term is a separate question. The cell that would
answer it, `AROW` against `nosplit`, is an 8-window difference (see below).

**Eight windows is a measured-inadequate instrument on this route.** The 29-window run
reproduces the 8-window arm's six per-window differences exactly, so nothing changed but the
sample. The +1.40% headline was a property of which windows the text starts with. So was a
cross-model ratio of 3.9x that matched the rival's predicted 4.2x. Both were wrong by 4.5x.

Every 8-window number in this corpus inherits that doubt, Qwen's own +0.36% included, and a
Qwen depth pair is unrun. The aggregate itself is exact rather than noisy: `ctl2` reproduces
`ctl` at +0.0000 on every window, on both models. The uncertainty is therefore the window
sample's alone.

The row subsample transfers to the second model as well, framing included. Its entire cost
there is again the one calibrating window (+0.0241 nats at window 1 against Qwen's +0.0296,
same window, same sign). It stays independent of `NCAL` at -0.0004 +/- 0.0040 over windows
2-8, the best-resolved number in that arm, which refutes composition at 5 se. That was
measured composed with `NCAL`=1. A `CALROWS`-alone cell at the shipped `NCAL`=2 on this model
is unrun.

One caution is retired rather than confirmed. SmolLM2's `ctl` returned 10.3354 on a different
build from the one that first recorded 10.3354, where two Qwen builds spanned 0.28%. That
spread is build-specific arithmetic that reached one model and not the other, not a general
between-build error bar.

The two dials are independent, measured rather than assumed. A second forward can repair a
column whose subsampled pass-one estimate under-read it. The row cut's frozen-scale damage
could therefore have composed with `NCAL`=1, and it does not. The composed cell differs from
plain `NCAL`=1 over windows 2-8 by -0.0065 +/- 0.0074 (se). The row cut's calibration-window
cost reproduces exactly: window 1 is +0.0296 nats here and +0.0296 at `NCAL`=2.

Window 1 is byte-identical under both settings (`calibrating` is `cal_done < ncal`, 0 is below
both 1 and 2, and the freeze happens after the surface is written). Its d(NLL) is +0.000000,
which makes it a free control inside every `NCAL` cell. Its own wall time is that process's
noise floor on identical work, measured here at 5.4%.

The price is read at the buckets rather than at a wall. `NCAL`=1 replaces one calibrating call
per weight with a converged one. The saving is therefore `calibrate`-per-call minus
`entry`-per-call, not the `calibrate` bucket:

| per frontend call, `-ub` 512 | `NCAL`=2 | `NCAL`=1 |
|---|---:|---:|
| calibrating (`calibrate`/`cal_calls`) | 117.01 ms | 115.38 ms |
| converged (`entry`/converged calls) | 58.14 ms | 57.09 ms |

The two arms agree to 1.4% and 1.8%, and the ratio is 2.01x. That is one bootstrap pass per
extra device pass, which is what the code emits. Net 58.6 ms per replaced call over the
model's 197 offloaded GEMM sites projects 11.5 s. The measured offloaded-path delta is 11.6 s
(90.2 -> 78.6 s), a closure of 0.994.

**That saving is paid once per process and does not scale with prompt length.** The -12.9%
above is a share of a four-window run's offloaded path, not of a deployment. Do not quote it
as one.

The run wall moved the other way (4.38 -> 4.62 min) and carries no signal. Window 1's
identical work differed 5.4% between the two processes, and the totals differed 5.5%. The
wall pair is therefore entirely inside the process noise floor. The `quant+rot`
bucket, which this arm cannot reach, moved +5.5% in the same pair.

[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-12. The profiled `--chunks 4` cells reproduce the unprofiled `--chunks 8` runs'
window-4 cumulative to four digits in both arms (8.7163 and 8.7238). So `ROCKET_MM_PROFILE`
does not perturb the output. What it cannot see: one model, 8 windows of one text, one `-ub`,
decode, and whether `CALROWS`'s own -28.2% still holds at `NCAL`=1. That ratio was measured
at `NCAL`=2, and its composition here is [expected].]

#### The calibration regime

The calibration regime is 2.6x the converged one, and a short bench never leaves it.
`ROCKET_RK3576_NCAL` defaults to 2, and a calibration forward is two device passes (the colmax
bootstrap). The first two micro-batches each weight sees therefore cost about twice what every
later one does. Measured per frontend call, a calibrating call takes 94.2 ms against 36.4 ms
converged at `M`=512. That is 2.59x, not 2.0x, because the bootstrap's own host work is
outside the entry.

**The `calibrate` bucket is not the removable cost: subtract the `entry` it displaces.** A
calibrating call returns before the converged path's device call, so it never pays the
`entry` bucket at all. The removable term is `calibrate - entry` = 171.3 - 47.5 = 123.8 s a
process. The directly observed excess of the two calibrating forwards over a converged one is
127.8 s, which leaves a 3.2% residual. The residual is the calibrating path's own `dequant`
and the scale loops. Quoting the 171.3 s bucket as a saving over-states it by 1.39x, and any
bucket standing in for a branch needs the same subtraction.

**This is a measurement trap, not a defect.** `llama-bench` runs one untimed warmup plus `-r`
timed reps. The share of a bench that is calibration is therefore set by how many micro-batches
a pass contains, which is `-p / -ub`. At `-p 2048`:

| `-ub` | forwards per weight, `-r 1` | of which calibration |
|---|---:|---:|
| 512 | 8 | 2 (25%) |
| 1024 | 4 | 2 (50%) |
| 2048 | 2 | **2 (100%)** |

So a `-ub` sweep at `-r 1` varies the ubatch and the calibration fraction together. The
reported t/s falls 12.07 -> 11.49 -> 9.67, in exactly the direction that makes the larger
ubatch look worse. **A `pp512 -r 1` bench of this route is 100% calibration regime**: every
offloaded matmul runs the device twice. Read the `entry` and `calibrate` buckets, which
separate the regimes by construction, rather than the wall. Alternatively, give the run enough
reps that the converged path dominates.

The accounting closes on both paths. Frontend `entry` against the library's own accounted
total for the same calls agrees to 2.2-5.0% on the converged path. In the measurement before
the `calscan` bucket existed, the calibration path left 27% at `M`=512, rising to 48% at
`M`=2048, unbucketed. That gap was the bootstrap's per-column maximum scan. The scan has its
own `calscan` bucket, and the term itself is spent (1.0% of `calibrate`).

One-time weight preparation is 26.7-29.7 s (`wt_rotate` 15.6-17.1 s, `wt_quant` 11.0-12.6 s
over 166 weights). It is paid once at first use and cached by name, so it lands in
`llama-bench`'s untimed warmup and in a real user's first prompt.

**Building `ggml-rocket` for this part needs `-DGGML_ROCKET_NATIVE_FP16=OFF`.** The option
defaults on and adds `-march=armv8.2-a+fp16` for the RK3588's A76/A55. This part is ARMv8.0-A
(`/proc/cpuinfo` has neither `asimdhp` nor `asimddp`), and the resulting `.so` would SIGILL in
the convert kernels.

### The route's accuracy inside a model

The route costs 1.011x perplexity, measured on the part inside a model. `llama-perplexity` ran
over the first 8 chunks of wikitext-2 test at `n_ctx`=512. Both arms used the same GGUF, the
same text and the same boot, CPU arm then NPU arm:

| arm | wikitext-2 PPL (8 chunks) | wall |
|---|---:|---:|
| CPU only, F16 | 9.7872 +- 0.58866 | 13.01 min |
| NPU, W8A8 | 9.8969 +- 0.59556 | 6.55 min |

The perplexity ratio is 1.011x, and the wall ratio (1.99x) corroborates the `pp512` bench's
2.01x on the same denominator. The 1.008x and 1.020x figures are host arithmetic over a
simulator, and this number is taken off the device inside a model.

The measurement scores the block-diagonal rotation. Every offloaded call here is `K`=1536,
which is neither a power of two nor `60*2^k`. All of them therefore take the block-diagonal
Hadamard fallback. That construction lives as a static in `ggml-rocket.cpp`, and no gate in
`rocket-userspace` can link it. It composes: against the unrotated route's chaos (a 2%
per-column divisor change took SmolLM2 from 10.58 to 2.6e10), a 1.1% move is nothing.

**It is not the same quantity as the simulator's 1.008x, and the two must not be diffed.** The
simulator's figure is against fp32 with an exactly frozen colmax. This one is against the same
GGUF's F16 CPU arm, with the shipped bootstrap at `CALSAFE`=3.0, over 8 chunks whose own error
bar is +-0.59. What it does say is that nothing on the composed device route costs a model an
order of magnitude.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, llama.cpp b10356, Qwen2.5-1.5B F16, measured 2026-08-11]

### Datapath blockers for a GEMM frontend

Two things stop a GEMM frontend on this part, and both are in the datapath rather than in
the speed.

#### The K-split wedge

At prefill scale, a `K` past one contraction does more than cost more: it wedges the NPU.
At `M=512 K=8192 N=2048` (plane 32×16, K slice 4608, 2 slices, N tile 256, narrow writer,
256 submits), `rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576()` returns `-4` after the write guard spends its
eight power cycles. The part then raises the driver's DMA-error interrupt,
`WARNING: rocket_job.c … rocket_job_irq_handler`. That warning is one of the handler's two
`WARN_ON`s over `PC_INTERRUPT_RAW_STATUS_DMA_{READ,WRITE}_ERROR`.

**The NPU is then unusable across processes.** Shapes that ran clean minutes earlier come
back `-4`. A `rmmod`/`modprobe` does not recover it: core 0 then fails to probe with `-22`
alongside `rk_iommu … FORCE_RESET command timed out`. **Only a reboot clears it.** Treat
any measurement taken after one of these as contaminated.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, one shape, 2026-08-07]

**`rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576()` declines that `K` instead of routing onto it.** It returns
`ROCKET_E_UNSUPPORTED` with nothing submitted and no BO allocated. The wedge shape comes
back in a 64 ms process (21 MB of that is generating the operands), and the `dmesg`
warning delta is 0. A control shape run immediately afterwards in the same boot computes
at 26.1 ms. A caller that needs a larger `K` runs it on the host, and
`ROCKET_RK3576_MM_KSPLIT=1` takes the route anyway, for measurement.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, 2026-08-08]

The refusal covers the whole class, and it has to, because nothing narrower is measured.
Small cells in the class compute. `M=32 K=8192 N=128` and `M=16 K=16384 N=64` ran green in
the gate list for several sessions, and `M=64 K=8192 N=32` scored exact three times. But
every axis that separates them from the wedge moves together, so no boundary can be fitted
to them:

| cell | submits | weight cube | per-submit output surface |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `M=32 K=8192 N=128` | 2 | 2304 KiB | 16 KiB |
| `M=16 K=16384 N=64` | 4 | 1152 KiB | 4 KiB |
| `M=64 K=8192 N=32` | 4 | 576 KiB | 8 KiB |
| the wedge | 256 | 4608 KiB | 512 KiB |

All four share `ks`=4608 and a 144 KiB resident slice. The route's own coverage stays in
the gate list through `rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576_i32()`, which is called directly and
carries the same warning in its header.
[pure, `tools/rk3576-planprobe.c cell`, 2026-08-08]

The class is `K >= 6176`, not `K > 4608`, and the threshold does not move with `M` or
`N`. The single-task planner shrinks the output-channel tile by halves, down to 32
channels, before it gives up. So the boundary is the CBUF pool at a one-group weight cube,
which is a condition on `K` alone, and not the K-slice cap. The pure planner, enumerated
over thirty `(M, N)` cells with `M` 64-2048 and `N` 512-8192, gives one answer in every
cell. The smallest 32-multiple `K` that takes the int32 K split is 6176 at every one of the
thirty.
[host-computed, pure enumeration, `tools/rk3576-planprobe.c map`, 2026-08-06]

#### The single-group band below the K split

Between the two thresholds the output tile collapses in one step. The cost is a silent
2.7x, not the 32x its submit count suggests. `r76_weight_slice_cap()` is stated over the
*group count*, while the resident slice it is compared against, `32*K`, does not depend on
the tile at all. So once `32*K` passes the two-group cap of 156 KiB, the only tile left is
32 channels. The ladder:

| `K` | largest output tile | why |
|---|---|---|
| `<= 4608` | up to 2048 | `32*K <= 144 KiB`, the >= 4 group cap |
| `4609-4736` | 96 | 3 groups, 148 KiB |
| `4737-4992` | 64 | 2 groups, 156 KiB |
| `4993-6144` | 32 | one group: only the CBUF pool binds |
| `>= 6176` | none | the K split |

At `M=512 N=2048` that is 32 submits at `K`=4608 and 1024 at `K`=5120. At `N`=4096 it is
64 against 2048. **Nothing refuses and nothing computes wrong**, and a frontend that sizes
a model by "does it run" will not see it.
[host-computed, pure enumeration, `tools/rk3576-planprobe.c map`, 2026-08-06]

The band computes, and its wall shows that a submit count is not a cost. At
`M=512 N=2048`, with one process a cell and three processes a `K`, under `taskset -c 4` on
`ondemand`:

| `K` | tile | submits | wall, median of 3 processes | exact |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| 4608 | 1024 | 32 | 72.2 / 71.5 / 71.9 ms | 3 of 3 |
| 5120 | 32 | 1024 | 191.7 / 175.3 / 191.3 ms | 3 of 3 |
| 6144 | 32 | 1024 | 184.5 / 203.4 / 182.4 ms | 3 of 3 |

So the collapse costs 2.41-2.86x over the rep extremes and 2.66x on medians. 32x the
submits buys 2.7x the wall. The marginal cost of each of the 992 extra submits is 120 us,
where a wide-tile submit costs 2.25 ms. **A per-submit cost is not a floor: it scales with
the output tile.** The "~1.4 ms per submit whatever it carries" figure is a wide-tile
number, and it must not be carried into this band.

Inside the band the wall is flat in `K`. `K`=6144 carries 20% more MACs than `K`=5120 and
reads the same 182-203 ms. So the collapsed band is dispatch-bound rather than MAC-bound,
and the extra contraction depth is free. The band is still worth offloading, but at
roughly 1.8x rather than the 4.2x the same shape gets at `K`=4608. Scaling ggml Q8_0's
measured 531.8 ms at `M=512 K=4096 N=4096` by MAC count puts the CPU near 334 ms at
`K`=5120 `N`=2048 [expected, derived, not measured]. That compares with the NPU's 185.

Fixing the cap so that the band keeps its wide tile is capped at that 2.4-2.9x, on
`4993 <= K <= 6144` alone. The submit count is a per-`(M, N)` quantity, so neither the
ratio nor the 32x transfers to another column.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `rk3576_submit_floor`, 2026-08-08]

#### Whole-board deaths

The whole-board death is real, its cause is open, and it is not attributable to the
K-split submit. A ladder over that route on the shipped 1.5.0 module ran these cells in
order, to find which one kills the board:

- `M=64 K=8192 N=32` (3 submits)
- `M=128 K=8192 N=256`
- `M=512 K=8192 N=256`
- The wedge itself

It ran one cell per process on `ondemand`, with a single-threaded CPU reference between
cells. The board went down during it.

The ladder's logs survived the power cycle, and they show that it never got past the first
cell. The ladder's output and per-cell logs record `M=64 K=8192 N=32` three times.
Every rep reads `RC=0` and `WRONG=0 of 2048` over the whole surface, with walls of
14.4-16.5 ms. The boot carries zero `WARNING`/`Oops`, and its last NPU message is 45
minutes before the end. The journal stops abruptly at 04:29:45 UTC on a routine `sudo`
line, with no shutdown sequence.

So the board died after a clean repetition of the smallest cell in the ladder, with no NPU
fault of any kind recorded. That death is neither shape-selective nor announced. It does
not implicate the K-split submit, which had just returned `RC=0`.

A reading of this run from host unreachability alone concludes the opposite, and the logs
contradict it. Reachability was not evidence that night in any case. The workstation's own
LAN failed within ten minutes of the board going quiet, and its router needed a factory
reset. **Decide that a cell killed the part from a serial console or a post-power-cycle log
read, never from a ping.** The earlier sgemm A/B death therefore keeps its rival reading,
unrefuted: a power event, because that run was pinned to `performance` with four threads
busy.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, n=1 ladder, logs read 2026-08-06, cause unmeasured]

A third whole-board death refutes both rivals the second one left standing, if the three
are one class. A matmul correctness sweep (`tools/rk3576-mm-drain-sweep.sh`) started on a
board that had been up 1h56m, at taint 4096 with zero `dmesg` `WARNING`s. That board had
just completed a five-repetition device run cleanly. It went unreachable inside the next
four minutes and did not come back.

The conditions rule out the two candidates carried forward from the previous deaths. The
governor was `ondemand`, and the workload was pinned to one core with `taskset -c 4`. So
the sustained-all-core-on-`performance` reading does not apply. No shape in the sweep's
grid takes the K split at all. Every one is a single-task `K <= 4096` matmul, so the
K-split submit does not apply either.

What this run introduced that no earlier one had is `dpu_grace_us` = 0 on the matmul path.
That is a candidate and not a finding. The conv-path `rowmap` sweep weakens it, because
that sweep drove grace to 0 at 10 repetitions a cell with no incident.

The LAN is excluded as a rival for this death, which the previous death lacked. A second
host on the same subnet (the RK3588 board) answered SSH normally and could not ping the
RK3576, repeatedly, over ten minutes. That is the cheap discriminator. **Before you record
a board as dead, probe it from a third machine on its own LAN.**

The sweep wrote its log under `$HOME`, so that a reboot would not clear it, and
nothing reached it. A filesystem-wide search after the power cycle found no `mmdrain*` at
all. So the run died before its first line was flushed, and how far it got is
unrecoverable.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, n=1, no serial console, 2026-08-09, cause unmeasured]

A fourth death takes grace 0 out of the candidate set. The same sweep ran again on a board
up 7h05m, at taint 4096 with zero `WARNING`s, freshly rebuilt. That board had just returned
58 of 58 gate rows and a clean five-repetition matmul probe. The grace list was
`500 4000 250 100`, so 0 was never written. The governor was `ondemand` on one pinned core,
and again no shape had `K >= 6176`.

The board went unreachable anyway, and much faster. The launching `ssh` failed to deliver
an `echo` scheduled five seconds behind the detached start. That brackets the death at
under about 5 s from the first process, against the previous four minutes. The first cell
in the safe-first order is `M=128 K=2048 N=2048` at grace 500. Its grace write was a no-op
over the value already live, and it was the one grid shape nothing had run that session.
The sweep's log was again empty, and the RK3588 again excluded the LAN.

Each named cause of the four deaths is refuted as a necessary condition: `performance` plus
a sustained all-core load, the K-split submit, and `dpu_grace_us`=0. Each of those three
was named as "the thing this run introduced". Each was excluded by the next run, which set
it safely and died anyway. Three refuted candidates in a row is the shape of looking in the wrong place. Asking what else the harness introduced puts one candidate far
ahead of the rest on cost.

After four deaths, the leading candidate is one shape that no run had driven, and a single
foreground command tests it. The sweep's `SHAPES` array begins with `M=128 K=2048 N=2048`.
So that cell is the first device work of both the third and fourth death, and the
fourth's ~5 s bracket puts the death inside it. Before the fifth death, that shape appeared
nowhere else in this part's recorded corpus. It is not one of the sgemm A/B's shapes, not
one of the six "0 of 5" cells, and not in any gate row. It is a single-tile matmul, the
only 1-tile cell in the grid, and nothing has ever driven the entry at one task.

The discriminating test runs `rk3576_mm_corr 128 2048 2048 1` alone, in the foreground, as
the first device action after the power cycle. It runs before any loop and before anything
else. If that kills the board, the answer is a shape and no structural story is needed. If
it survives three times over, the shape is excluded at low cost, and the structural reading
below is what is left.

The structural reading comes second in order. What the three sweep deaths share, and the
survivors do not, is a detached loop (`setsid nohup`) running one cell per process. Each
process allocates and tears down tens of megabytes of BOs. The module carries neither the
BO-lifetime fix (`0014`) nor the IOMMU-domain fix (`0013`), and both were written against
observed render-group crashes on this part. This reading is [hypothesis], with no console
behind it. It is also in tension with the discipline that produced the structure (one cell
per process, so a wedge cannot contaminate the next cell).

Process churn alone is not sufficient. The 58-row gate list is a foreground loop of 58
device processes, and it completed cleanly on this same boot, minutes before the death. The
arm that tests the reading runs the identical grid as repetitions inside one process, and
under the patched module.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, n=1 per death, no serial console, 2026-08-12, cause unmeasured]

A fifth death attributes the class to the shape and clears the whole harness-structure
family. `M=128 K=2048 N=2048` ran alone:

- One foreground process, one repetition, one cell
- No detached loop, no module-parameter write, no governor change and no all-core load
- No `K >= 6176` shape

The board had been power-cycled 49 minutes earlier, and its whole parameter block was read
rather than assumed:

| parameter | value |
|---|---|
| module | shipped `rocket` 1.5.0 |
| bound entry | core 0, the only one |
| `poll_interval_us` | 50 |
| `dpu_grace_us` | 500 |
| `dpu_blind_us` | 250 |
| `rocket_batch_submit` | 1 |
| governor | `ondemand` |
| taint | 4096 |
| `dmesg` `WARNING`s | zero |

The two probe files were shipped by tar with `md5sum` matching at both ends, and the tree
was rebuilt at rc=0 first. The command was:

    timeout 120 sudo -E taskset -c 4 ./build/rk3576_mm_corr 128 2048 2048 1

It emitted the library's three initialization lines and nothing else. The `timeout 120`
never fired and the `ssh` never returned. So the board left the network before its own
120 s guard could act, and it took the whole system down, not just the NPU. Six probes over
two minutes from the RK3588 on the same subnet all read down. That RK3588 answered SSH
meanwhile and reported 1d8h of uptime, so the LAN is excluded again.

What that run measures is the negative space. The previous four deaths left these
structural candidates standing, and this run had none of them:

- A detached loop
- Per-process BO churn
- A module-parameter write
- A sustained load
- A governor state

The 58-row gate list is 58 foreground device processes, and it completes cleanly on the
same kind of boot. So the harness-structure reading above is refuted as a necessary
condition. The governor, the K-split submit and `dpu_grace_us`=0 were each refuted the same
way, and what is left is the cell.

The pure plan does not separate the killer from the cell that survives. Read with no
device, the shipped planner gives this plan: at `K=2048` the CBUF feature budget is
`8 * 32768 / 2048` = 128 pixels. So `r76_mm_plane` takes the widest divisor of `M` that
fits: `iw`=128 and `ih`=1. That is one row task, one N tile, one task in one submit. The
cell `M=256 K=2048 N=2048` is the most-run cell on this route and a 10-of-10 survivor. It
plans the identical per-task program at `iw`=128, and differs only in carrying two tasks
instead of one.

So on the axes a pure read can see, the difference between a board-killer and a survivor
is one entry in the task array. That this points at the task-array and submit path rather
than at the regcmd is [hypothesis]. The two cells also differ in every runtime quantity the
plan does not name. No serial console has ever been behind any of these deaths.

The plan's own basis for nominating the shape is wrong, though the nomination held.
"Nothing has ever driven the entry at one task" is false. In the cost table above,
`32x1024x1024`, `128x1024x1024` and `256x512x2048` all plan `ih`=1 and all compute,
`128x1024x1024` at 5.98 ms. A single-task submit is ordinary on this entry. Whatever the
part objects to is not the task count alone. It is not any bound the library models either,
and the same is already recorded for the K-split wedge above.

This outranks the dropped-write hazard as a port blocker. The shape `M=128 K=2048 N=2048`
is an unremarkable prefill shape, 128 tokens at a 2048-wide hidden state. Any GEMM frontend
that does not know to refuse it can reach it. A silently dropped output tail is
recoverable. **A whole-board wedge that needs a physical power cycle is not.**
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, n=1, no serial console, 2026-08-06, pure plan read from
`src/rocket_matmul_rk3576.c` and `src/rocket_hw_profile.c`, cause still unmeasured]

##### Kernel 7.1.7 and `rocket` 1.6.0

The death does not reproduce on the reflashed stack, and the attribution to a shape does
not survive it. On kernel 7.1.7 carrying `rocket` 1.6.0 in-tree, `M=128 K=2048 N=2048`
computes. That image builds the patch series in, rather than loading it out of tree. The
cell first ran exactly as it did when it killed the board, `timeout 120 sudo -E taskset -c 4
./build/rk3576_mm_corr 128 2048 2048 1`, as the only device action after a green control.
It returned clean in 16.583 ms.

It then ran 18 clean repetitions across 13 processes. The last 12 of them ran as a repeated
one-cell-per-process loop, the same harness structure that took the board 3 of 3 times.
The board stayed up throughout, with zero `dmesg` `WARNING`s, taint unmoved and the
backstop count unchanged.

Every other shape the class had blocked is clean on the same boot, one cell per process,
foreground, at the shipped default `dpu_grace_us`=0:

| shape | reps | result | wall |
|---|---:|---|---|
| `128x1024x1024` (the control) | 1 | clean | 4.75 ms |
| `128x2048x2048` (the killer) | 18 | clean | 16.4-17.2 ms |
| `128x2048x1024`, `128x1024x2048` (the K-vs-N discriminators) | 3 + 3 | clean | 9.5-9.7 ms |
| `256`, `384`, `512`, `1024` `x2048x2048` (the M ladder) | 5+3+3+3 | clean | 19.6-47.8 ms |
| `256x2048x4096` | 3 | clean | 38.1-38.8 ms |

That is 39 repetitions, 0 wrong elements and 0 deaths. The two discriminating cells the
class was waiting on are green. So are the two never-run shapes that had been reserved as
separate device costs.

This does not establish which change cured it. A reimage moves the kernel
(7.1.6 -> 7.1.7), the module (1.5.0 out-of-tree -> 1.6.0 in-tree, `0025` among the series)
and whatever else the image carries, all at once. So this is a joint change, and it
attributes nothing to any one patch.

The class does not reproduce under the conditions that produced it. That holds over 18
repetitions of the exact killer cell, in both the isolated and the looped harness. It is
not proof that the silicon cannot be wedged. Five deaths happened across sessions under
varied conditions, and 39 repetitions in one boot at one operating point cannot close that.

**Treat a never-run matmul shape as cheaper than it was, not free.** Before you apply any of
this to the module in front of you, read the board's own parameter block.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, kernel 7.1.7 / `rocket` 1.6.0, 39 reps over 9 shapes, one cell per process,
2026-08-10]

#### The K-split wedge's register program

The wedge is not an encoder overflow. The 143-op program that the wedge's first row task
emits has no value near its word:

- `CNA_WEIGHT_BYTES` (0x101C) is 0x480000, which is the weight BO's exact size.
- `CNA_DMA_CHANNEL` (0x107C) is `ic-1` = 4607.
- The CBUF data-entry count is 2304, against a programmed feature allowance of 4096
  granules.

Every library bound passes, one of them with no margin at all. The resident slice is
`32 * 4608` = 147456 bytes, against a `r76_weight_slice_cap` of exactly 147456 at >= 4
groups. The whole cube is 4.5 MiB against a 6 MiB bound. So whatever the part is objecting
to is not in the library's model of it.
[pure, dumped from `tools/rk3576-planprobe.c -r`, 2026-08-06]

#### Dropped writes at the drain deadline

The entry also rides the drain deadline at some prefill shapes. At `M=256 K=2048 N=2048` on
the shipped 1.5.0 module at `dpu_grace_us`=500, the surface comes back missing elements in
12 of 14 reps. The loss is 768 to 26112 of 524288 elements, and it wanders rep to rep. At
`dpu_grace_us`=4000 it is exact in 0 of 9, interleaved over three alternating passes, and
the failure returns every time the parameter goes back. The wrong elements sit at distance
99-100 from the reference, which is the `0xA5` output sentinel. They are dropped writes,
not wrong arithmetic, which is the drain deadline's recorded signature.

The other six shapes in the table are 0 of 5 each. Grace is the instrument and not the fix,
and the fix is the drain-deadline patch. The exposure is the point: **a GEMM frontend
cannot assume this entry computes at every prefill shape on the shipped module.**
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, measured 2026-08-07]

`rk3576_mm_corr` shows the lost surface per repetition. It scores every repetition of an
arbitrary shape and prints each wrong repetition's bounding box. Five repetitions of
`M=256 K=2048 N=2048` on the shipped 1.5.0 module at `dpu_grace_us`=500, pinned, in one
process, were 5 of 5 wrong. The five agree on structure:

| rep | wrong of 524288 | rows | cols | wrong / bounding box |
|---|---:|---|---|---:|
| 0 | 19840 | 128..199 | 1504..2047 | 0.506 |
| 1 | 2944 | 128..151 | 1824..2047 | 0.548 |
| 2 | 40576 | 128..239 | 1312..2047 | 0.492 |
| 3 | 17664 | 128..195 | 1536..2047 | 0.508 |
| 4 | 24688 | 128..207 | 1440..2047 | 0.508 |

- **The loss is confined to the last job.** The pure plan tiles this shape into two tasks of
  128 rows. Every wrong element in all five repetitions is at row >= 128, the first row of
  the second task. Rows 0..127 are exact in 5 of 5.
- **It always reaches the last column** and never the first: the low column edge wanders
  (1312-1824) and the high edge is 2047 in all five.
- **It is not a rectangle and not a whole-row suffix.** The wrong count is 0.49-0.55 of the
  bounding box in every repetition. So the front between correct and lost is diagonal in
  (row, column), consistent with a write order that advances in both axes. It is not
  consistent with a contiguous suffix of a channel-group-major scan, which would put the
  high row edge at 255.
- **Magnitude spans 14x at a fixed shape**: 2944 to 40576 elements, 0.56% to 7.7%. So a
  single repetition cannot price this hazard.
- The entry's host de-scatter always runs: 0 elements of 524288 retain the probe's host-side
  sentinel in any repetition. So what is lost is inside the device buffer, not a skipped
  copy-out.
  [HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, one shape, one process, 5 reps, 2026-08-09]

Every lost element is a dropped write, so the matmul entry reaches the mechanism that the
conv path's deadline was decoded as. The probe, `rk3576_mm_corr`, classifies each wrong
element by what it holds:

- The library's `0xA5` device stamp (the DPU never wrote the atom)
- Zero
- An arbitrary computed value
- The probe's host sentinel

A second five-repetition run of the same shape and configuration has `computed`, `zero` and
`no-descatter` all 0. It reads `drop == wrong` exactly, ratio 1.0000, in 5 of 5:

| rep | wrong of 524288 | drop | computed | rows | cols | wrong / bounding box |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---:|
| 0 | 3968 | 3968 | 0 | 128..155 | 1792..2047 | 0.554 |
| 1 | 17664 | 17664 | 0 | 128..195 | 1536..2047 | 0.507 |
| 2 | 19840 | 19840 | 0 | 128..199 | 1504..2047 | 0.507 |
| 3 | 5760 | 5760 | 0 | 128..163 | 1760..2047 | 0.556 |
| 4 | 22272 | 22272 | 0 | 128..203 | 1472..2047 | 0.509 |

So the two entries share the cause: the driver retires a job while the DPU is still
writing. Ten repetitions across two runs and two sessions agree on the confinement to the
second task and on the reach to the last column.

The drain-deadline patch cures this path, and on a 1.6.0 kernel grace is the inducer
rather than the hazard. On kernel 7.1.7 carrying `rocket` 1.6.0 in-tree, `dpu_grace_us` 0
means *wait for the writing block* instead of *retire on the next tick*. There the same
shape, probe, process discipline and pinned core read:

| `dpu_grace_us` | wrong reps | dropped elements |
|---|---:|---:|
| 0 (wait, the shipped default) | 0 of 5 | 0 |
| 500 (a capped deadline) | 5 of 5 | 247728 |

The green half is not an instrument failure. Dialing grace back to 500 on the *same boot
and same binary* brings the loss back at full strength. So the probe demonstrably retains
its discriminating power on this kernel. The output BO stamp is reported `0xA5` in both
arms, so drops are scorable throughout. Every one of the 247728 wrong elements classifies
as `drop`, with `computed`, `zero` and `no-descatter` all 0.

**Grace on a 1.6.0 kernel is therefore a research instrument that manufactures the hazard
on demand.** That makes the shape below cheap to map on a cured board.

Forcing a deadline on 1.6.0 also raises the driver's own DMA-error warning, and the taint
word cannot show it. One `warning: drivers/accel/rocket/rocket_job.c:895 at
rocket_job_irq_handler` appeared during the grace-500 arms, and none during any grace-0
work. It is the handler's `WARN_ON` on `DMA_READ_ERROR`/`DMA_WRITE_ERROR`, the early-fence
signature already recorded on the conv path. So the induced hazard is visible in `dmesg` as
well as in the surface. Expect it in a grace arm, and do not read it as a new defect.

The warning did not move `/proc/sys/kernel/tainted`. This image boots with the `W` bit
already set by an unrelated `rga3` warning, so the taint word was 4608 before and after.
**On an image whose boot already taints, count WARNINGs, not taint**: the word has no
resolution left.
[HW sweep, A/B, H96 MAX M9, one shape, one process per arm, 5 + 5 reps, 2026-08-10]

#### The geometry of the lost surface

What the two entries do not share is the shape of what is lost, and that shape is
undecoded. The conv path loses a contiguous suffix of output-channel groups. Here the loss
is anchored at both the last task's first row (128, in 10 of 10) and the last output column
(2047, in 10 of 10). It fills 0.49-0.56 of its own bounding box every time.

With the probe's scoring mask at 1.0 at every K, the reference is zero everywhere and the
output BO is stamped. So the wrong set is the dropped set, and that fill is the loss's own
geometry, not an artifact of the input pattern. A fill of one half anchored at a corner is
what a straight diagonal cut across a rectangle gives. So a bounding box and a count read
as a triangular front.

The per-row element map confirms the triangle and quantizes its front to 32 columns. The
map below is `ROCKET_MM_CORR_ROWMAP=1` on `M=256 K=2048 N=2048`, with grace 500 as the
inducer on the 1.6.0 kernel. It covers three repetitions and 308 wrong rows in total:

| | rep 0 | rep 1 | rep 2 |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| wrong rows | 108 | 104 | 96 |
| rows whose wrong columns are one contiguous run | 108 | 104 | 93 |
| rows whose run ends at column 2047 | 108 | 104 | 96 |
| rows whose run starts on a multiple of 32 | 108 | 104 | 96 |
| inversions in the per-row count | 1 | 0 | 0 |
| row 128 width -> last wrong row's width | 736 -> 32 | 640 -> 32 | 640 -> 32 |

- **Each wrong row holds exactly one run, and it always ends at the last column.** The count
  equals the span in 305 of 308 rows, so the loss is a suffix of each row, never scattered.
- **The per-row width falls monotonically** from the last task's first row to the last
  wrong row, with one inversion in 308 rows. It narrows 20-23x end to end and terminates in
  a single 32-wide run. It is a triangle, not a half-full rectangle.
- **The front moves in 32-column steps at about 8 columns per row**, and every run start is
  32-aligned in 308 of 308 rows. So a channel-group *granularity* is real here, but a
  channel-group *structure* is not. The conv path's loss is a union of aligned groups. This
  one is a single run whose edge is merely quantized to the same 32.
- **The apex does not reach the last row.** The wrong region ends at rows 235, 231 and 223
  of a 0..255 surface, one per repetition. So where the triangle terminates is a wall-clock
  race, and it varies per rep, exactly as the 14x magnitude spread already shows.

**Read this shape in de-scattered coordinates, and do not lift a write order off it.** The
probe scores the host-de-scattered surface, and the de-scatter un-permutes the cube layout.
So a linear frontier in the hardware's own write order can materialize here as a triangle.
The geometry is measured. Any mechanism it suggests is [hypothesis].
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, one shape, one process, 3 reps, 308 wrong rows, 2026-08-10]

The extent distribution runs closer to zero than the loud reps suggest. Over the ten
repetitions the loss spans 3968 to 40576 elements, 0.76% to 7.7% of the surface, at one
fixed shape and one fixed grace. A bracket fitted to the first run's 12-26k range missed
the second run's floor by 2x. **Do not price this hazard from a handful of repetitions.** A
probe that reports a small extent is not reporting a nearly-correct datapath.

#### Exposure across shapes

The exposure is not confined, and every "0 of 5" in the earlier tables is undersampling.
With grace 500 as an inducer on a cured 1.6.0 kernel, the whole grid can be run at depth.
At five repetitions a cell, the grid reproduces the confined-looking picture exactly. At
twenty-five it does not:

| shape | tasks | 5 reps | 25 reps | rate |
|---|---:|---|---|---:|
| `128x2048x2048` | 1 | 3 of 5 | n/a | 60% |
| `256x2048x2048` | 2 | 5 of 5 | n/a | 100% |
| `384x2048x2048` | 3 | 0 of 5 | 4 of 25 | 16% |
| `512x2048x2048` | 4 | 0 of 5 | 5 of 25 | 20% |
| `1024x2048x2048` | 8 | 0 of 5 | 0 of 25 | 0% |
| `256x2048x4096` | 2 | 5 of 5 | n/a | 100% |
| `128x2048x1024` | 1 | 0 of 5 | 0 of 25 | 0% |
| `128x1024x2048` | 1 | 0 of 5 | 0 of 25 | 0% |
| `128x1024x1024` | 1 | 0 of 5 | n/a | 0% |

- **`M`=384 and `M`=512 reach the hazard.** Both read 0 of 5 and both fail at 25. So
  confinement to `M`=256 is an artifact of the repetition count: five repetitions cannot
  see a 16-20% rate. **A "0 of 5" on this path bounds nothing.**
- **The rate falls sharply with the submit count** past `M`=256: 100%, then 16-20%, then
  0 of 25 at eight tasks. So submit count is real as a *rate* modifier and wrong as a
  *boundary*. Depth separates the two readings, and argument does not.
- **Both `K`=2048 and `N`>=2048 are needed.** Halving either one takes the cell to 0 of 25,
  while doubling `N` to 4096 keeps it at 100%. What the hazard tracks is the per-submit
  output surface, not `M` and not the task count on its own.
- Why more tasks lower the rate is [hypothesis]. A longer job gives the writing block more
  of its drain overlapped with later tasks' compute, which leaves less residual at the
  deadline. Nothing here measures it.

The method point outlives these numbers. The same grid, board and binary read as
"confined to one cell" at 5 repetitions and as "reaches at least four shapes" at 25.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, kernel 7.1.7 / `rocket` 1.6.0, grace 500 as inducer, one cell per
process, 2026-08-10]

#### The int32 output and the K split

The DPU emits its 32-bit accumulator on an integer program, and that is the K split. The
emitter `gen_conv2d_int8_rk3576_i32out()` takes `precision_int32` in DPU `0x4010`'s
output-width field and pins OUT_CVT to exact unity. The words that come back are genuine
int32 accumulators on the RK3588's int32 cube map,
`(c/4)*ow*oh_full*4 + 4*(y*ow+x) + c%4`. The decode drives operands whose every
accumulator is distinct, so that each one names exactly one word.

The writer's budget is one 16-byte atom per (16-channel block, pixel), whatever the output
element width is. So at four bytes an element, it delivers **the first eight output
channels of every thirty-two** and touches nothing else. The way around it is the weight
cube rather than a register. Program four times the output channels, and put real channel
`n` at programmed channel `32*(n/8) + n%8`. The surface that comes back is then the plain
int32 cube, with every channel present.

The budget is a function of the DPU's own operand width, `PROC_PRECISION`, not of the
output element's. Widening `0x4010[2:0]` from int8 to int32 makes it two atoms. The
delivered set becomes the first eight of every sixteen, so a real channel costs two
programmed ones instead of four. The arithmetic is bit-identical, and the CNA and CORE
programs are byte-unchanged. Two atoms is the ceiling, and int16, fp16 and bfloat16 reach
it too, the float ones destroying the arithmetic on the way. Neither int4 nor int8 reaches
it.

`gen_conv2d_int8_rk3576_i32out_wide()` emits it and `rocket_rk3576_i32_wide_word()`
addresses it. The map is a stream `s = 2*p + j` cut into runs of `ow*oh_full` atoms inside
32-channel super-groups. The decode covers `oc` 8 to 192 and pixel counts 4 to 33. **It
carries two bounds that fail silently**: `oc` must be a multiple of 32, and the surface
must be short.

The height rule the library enforces, `oh_full * oc < 4096`, is a safe cap and not the
boundary. With the cap lifted, a sweep finds the map exact well past it at `oc` 32, 64, 96
and 256. It finds the map wrong inside it at `oc` 128. The plane width is not in it. The
failures are neither monotone in height nor repairable by the writer's own redo. See
`chips/rk3576-regcmd.md` and `tests/rk3576_i32_height_bound.c`.

`rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576_i32()` drives the narrow writer by default and the wide one
under `ROCKET_RK3576_I32_OC_MULT=2`. Both are bit-exact against a CPU int32 model at K up
to 16384, and `rocket_matmul_int8_rk3576()` routes a K past one contraction through it.
The multiplier costs the N tile, and so submits. The measured count falls from 4 submits
to 2 at M=64 K=1024 N=2048, and from 8 to 4 at M=32 K=1024 N=4096. Below the tile cap the
two cost the same.

The wide one is opt-in because it **intermittently emits zeros**. On about one run in ten
of the shape that shows it most, a contiguous block of one super-group's stream comes back
zero. The rest of the surface is exact. It does not drop writes: when a sentinel stamps the
surface first, the bad atoms hold zero rather than the sentinel. So the writer reaches
them, and the data is wrong. On a fresh BO, which arrives zeroed, the two readings are
indistinguishable.

It is not the poisoning either, because it is indifferent to the idle ahead of it. The
corruption is contiguous in the writer's emission order and in no other, and it sits inside
a single super-group. It always ends two emissions short of that group's last, so the entry
detects it and redoes the row task. Over 40 runs of the worst shape there are 0 failures,
and 3 of the runs redo a task. No redo needs an idle in front of it.

Two traps come with it:

- **An i32out job poisons the next submit**, of any kind and across processes, because the
  state lives in the NPU and not in the fd. The poisoned submit then completes normally in
  the usual per-submit time and writes nothing. This is not a property of the int32 writer:
  it is the part's one wide-output hazard, and every fp16 program carries it too. The scope,
  the two negatives, and the law the guard is sized by are settled above under "The
  poisoning is one hazard". Check that a job wrote per task and not per tile. One poisoned
  submit among a row-split tile's leaves its own rows empty, while its siblings are full.
- **An output buffer must not be zeroed from the CPU before a submit.** The dirty cache
  lines race the DPU's DMA, and the writeback lands on top of the result.

The int32 sweep also wedged the IOMMU once, and only a reboot cleared it. The mechanism is
not established. The measured extent stays inside the allocated BO, so an overrun is not
the explanation the data supports. Details are in [rk3576-regcmd.md](rk3576-regcmd.md).
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, measured 2026-07-27]

Of the machine parameters, the CBUF is swept: its budget, the data/weight split, and the
field that trades between them. The matmul tile cap and the weight N-group are measured
against the matmul above. Only `kgroup_4b` remains carried over, and nothing reads it.

#### The op library on this part

The op library reaches this part. Convolution is behind `rocket_conv2d_int8_rk3576()`,
`rocket_conv2d_dw_int8_rk3576()` and `rocket_conv2d_fp16_rk3576()`. They take row-major
tensors and own the cubes, the row plan, the output-channel split, the de-scatter and the
submit-loop retries. Where the public entry's semantics are the ones the part computes, it
dispatches there, and a caller writes nothing chip-specific. Both `rocket_conv2d_dw_int8()`
and `rocket_conv2d_fp16()` do.

The direct entry, `rocket_conv2d_int8()`, does not. It writes a raw int32 accumulator,
where this part's direct int8 datapath requantizes on chip. So it keeps refusing and names
the entry that matches, exactly as `rocket_matmul_int8()` does. The gate
`tests/rk3576_conv_lib_gate.c` drives the emitter gate's own shape table through the
library: 111 pass bit-exact, 1 refuses.

Zero points are folded exactly and gated. The input's correction is `-in_zp*sum(w)` and
needs no border special case. The emitter programs the pad constant to the input zero
point, so a pad tap's true value is zero. The weight's correction rides in the coefficient
group's `B`, which the DPU adds, so it is programmed negated. The output's becomes the
`OUT_CVT` offset. Depthwise takes the input and output zero points and refuses a weight
zero point, whose group has no `B` field.

A handle packs a layer's weights once. Repacking them per call is where most of a graph's
wall goes, and the MobileNet section gives the 2.94x. The packing entry,
`rocket_conv2d_int8_pack_rk3576()`, takes the weights, the bias and the quant contract, and
returns a handle. Each inference runs it through `rocket_conv2d_int8_prepacked_rk3576()`.
One handle covers all three per-call entries: `desc.depthwise` picks direct or depthwise,
and a non-NULL `w_scale_oc` picks the per-axis requant.

The handle must freeze three things, and none of them is bookkeeping:

- The `fd`. A BO belongs to the file that created it, and an IOVA is per-fd. A handle run
  on another fd would hand the NPU addresses that mean nothing there, and the submit would
  still succeed. **So the handle refuses a foreign fd.**
- The output-channel tile. The weight cube's group count follows the tile rather than the
  layer. On the per-axis path, that tile is an accuracy choice made from the weights and
  the scales.
- The quant contract. Both zero points and all three scales are folded into the
  coefficient group at pack time.

The handle also holds the scratch whose size follows the frozen geometry: the feature cube,
the regcmd buffer and each tile's output surface. So an inference allocates no BO at all,
which matters more than the arithmetic does.

Reusing an output surface across inferences is safe on a part where "did this task write
anything" is a real check. There, the sentinel is re-stamped before every submit. With the
sentinel off, there is no check to mislead. Reusing the feature cube skips its zeroing
memset from the second call on. That is sound because the channels past `ic` are the
cube's zero padding, and nothing writes them again.

The gate compares outputs, not wall time. With `ROCKET_LG_RESIDENT=1`, it runs 173 shapes
of the library table through a pack plus two prepacked calls. It compares each call to the
transient output element for element, and 0 differ.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, `tests/rk3576_conv_lib_gate.c`]

The gate makes two calls because the second is the first to reuse a held cube and a held
surface. One call would not notice a stale byte.

The library's envelope is wider than the emitter's, and by a decoded amount. The
weight-slice cap bounds one program, keyed on the output-channel groups that program
drives. Splitting the output channels lowers the group count, and so raises the slice the
part accepts. Fourteen shapes the emitter refuses compute through the library, including
`ic=576 k=3` and `ic=5184 k=1` at four tiles of 32 channels.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, measured 2026-07-27]

The single-program rule is `ic*kh*kw <= 4608`, and through the library the bound is the
CBUF pool at one group, about `ic*kh*kw <= 5600`. One refusal survives, `ic=256 k=5` at
200 KiB, whose only recourse is an ic split that the on-chip requant forecloses.
[HW sweep, H96 MAX M9, measured 2026-07-27]

#### The per-submit floor and the completion poll

The per-submit floor is the driver's completion poll, at about 340 us. The
`INTERRUPT_MASK` `PC_DONE` pair (bits 28-29) rejects writes. So `patches/rk3576/npu/0006`
polls `PC_DONE` on an hrtimer, and `0009` makes the period `rocket.poll_interval_us`.

The probe `rk3576_submit_floor` runs an 8×64×32 int8 matmul, n=300, under `taskset` to
one A72. With the period at 50 us, its median is 329-331 us through 0020 and 344-348 us
through 0021 and 0022, against 1654 at the stock 1 ms. These are eight runs across three
modules, reproducing to within 20 us. A figure of 439 us, taken earlier in the series, does
not reproduce on any module now on the board. The floor bounds every submit-bound workload,
and on this part most workloads are submit-bound.
